**Rationale.** An impossible timing record is evidence that something in the measurement path is wrong, and a laboratory that clamps a negative duration to zero destroys exactly the signal that would have shown it. Charter Section 14.9 requires evidence loss, mutation, collision, adoption, or incomplete finalization to be treated as an explicit failure rather than concealed, and charter Section 12.10 records that operational differences can confound a comparison if they change timing or evidence coverage. The vocabulary of integrity problems and their dispositions is assigned to Section 14, and how such a record is preserved within an evidence root is assigned to Section 13; WP2-TIME-019 requires only that the problem be distinguishable, attributed, and retained.

### 11.11 Attribution of timing evidence

**WP2-TIME-020.** Every timing record a conforming laboratory retains **MUST** be attributable to the study, the planned run, the execution attempt, the laboratory worker, the model request or the deciding episode or decision it serves, and the registered concurrency profile to which it applies, naming each of those that applies and stating the absence of any that does not.

**Rationale.** Timing evidence that cannot be joined to the unit it describes is unusable for the comparative, long-horizon research the charter describes, and under concurrency it is actively misleading, because a duration observed while other units contended for the same resources means nothing without knowing which units those were. WP2-ISO-017 already requires every retained log or diagnostic record to carry explicit attribution to each entity of the Section 6 register whose activity it describes; WP2-TIME-020 states the corresponding obligation for timing records and adds the concurrency profile, so that a later analysis can relate an observed duration to the execution architecture and contention conditions under which it was observed. Stating the absence of an inapplicable identity, rather than omitting it, keeps an unattributed record distinguishable from one whose attribution does not apply — a timing record about a laboratory worker with no execution attempt assigned to it, for instance.

### 11.12 Options deliberately left unselected

**Unselected option.** Duration basis. The monotonic or equivalently safe basis WP2-TIME-013 requires may be a platform monotonic counter, a steady clock, a sequence of authority-issued marks, or another arrangement. No library, counter, resolution, or unit is selected.

**Unselected option.** Timestamp and instant representation. The wall-clock instants WP2-TIME-014 requires to be recorded separately may be represented in any calendar, precision, or encoding the evidence contract later adopts. No format, epoch, precision, or time zone is selected, and Section 13 rather than this section decides how evidence is written.

**Unselected option.** Clock synchronization. The synchronization uncertainty WP2-TIME-015 requires to be recorded may be established by a network time protocol, an authority-issued reference, a measured offset, or another arrangement. No protocol, service, or tolerance is selected, and WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting a numeric operating parameter.

**Unselected option.** Timing-record placement. The timing facts this section requires may be carried on the record of the unit measured, in a separate timing record, in an event stream, or in more than one of those. WP2-TIME-020 states the attribution property; no placement or join key is selected, and the placement of model-request records is assigned to Section 12.

**Unselected option.** Timeout values and stop conditions. Which timeouts, expiries, freshness tests, and stop conditions a laboratory applies, and at what values, are unselected. WP2-TIME-005 requires only that each identify its domain, and WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits this specification from selecting a duration, threshold, or bound.

### 11.13 Boundaries assigned elsewhere

This section states which time domains exist, what each measures, what may and may not influence simulated causation, and how timing evidence is measured, attributed, and preserved. It deliberately defines none of the following: the lifecycle transitions whose events serve as domain boundaries, which belong to Section 8; the scheduling, assignment, and commit mechanics whose intervals these domains measure, which belong to Section 9; the concurrency-profile categories under which observed durations are recorded, which belong to Section 10; the complete model-request lifecycle, the placement of request records, provider retry semantics, budget reservation, and token or monetary accounting, which belong to Section 12; the evidence format, inventory, replay, and retention of timing records, which belongs to Section 13; the typed vocabulary of the timing-integrity problems WP2-TIME-019 requires to be surfaced, which belongs to Section 14; the study-completion rules that fix a study's end boundary, which belong to Section 15; and the conformance checks, fault injections, and pass conditions for every requirement stated here, which belong to Section 17 and to the conformance document.

## 12. Model-request accounting, budgets, and provider behavior

### 12.1 What this section establishes

This section defines the implementation-neutral model-request and accounting graph of a conforming laboratory: the record types that identify a logical model request and everything that happens beneath and around it, the three lifecycle layers those records progress through, the complete attribution every such record carries, the duplicate-delivery, retry, fallback, and idempotency semantics that keep repeated activity from multiplying identity or spend, the budget and usage contract under which provider dispatch is authorized and accounted, and the provenance of provider behavior and operational exposure. It completes the model-accounting side of the ambiguities Section 5.14 items 5 and 8 carry forward; the questions the Section 6.13 rationale and Section 6.18 assign here — model-request record placement, the joins among request records, provider-behavior records, and budget attribution mechanics; the questions the Section 7.9 rationale assigns here — where request and response records live, which idempotency rule applies to a genuine repeat of one request, and how budgets are reserved and refused; the questions the Section 7.10 rationale assigns here — budget algorithms, reservation, exhaustion behavior, and refusal typing; and the questions Section 11.13 assigns here — the complete model-request lifecycle, the placement of request records, provider retry semantics, budget reservation, and token and monetary accounting — which Sections 9.13 and 10.13 assign here in their own overlapping terms: provider retry semantics, model-request record placement, and the model-request record and its accounting. It selects no mechanism: no provider, stack, storage, schema, wire format, dispatch path, idempotency realization, retry count, fallback order, reservation algorithm, pricing source, currency rule, or numeric operating parameter is selected anywhere in it, and the selections WP2-SCOPE-015 enumerates remain prohibited throughout this document.

Section 12.2 states how the model-request record model is read and carries the requirement that makes it binding. Section 12.3 holds the record register. Section 12.4 separates the three lifecycle layers and their stage events. Sections 12.5 through 12.7 state the logical-request, attribution, and idempotency requirements. Section 12.8 states the budget and usage contract. Section 12.9 states the provider-behavior requirements. Section 12.10 records options deliberately left unselected, and Section 12.11 states the boundaries assigned elsewhere.

Every substantive statement here is a Required future invariant carrying exactly one `WP2-MODEL-###` identifier, a Design rationale introduced by a **Rationale.** lead-in, or an Unselected option introduced by an **Unselected option.** lead-in, in the Section 4.3 sense, or else a register entry of Section 12.3, whose normative force flows through WP2-MODEL-001 rather than through an identifier of its own, an entry of the layer table or the event table in Section 12.4, each of which states how a layer or event is used in this section and carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements that cite it, WP2-MODEL-002 and WP2-MODEL-003 among them, an entry of the value-type table in Section 12.8, whose normative force flows through WP2-MODEL-034 rather than through an identifier of its own, or a boundary statement in Section 12.11. Nothing in this section is a Current fact or a Current limitation; those belong to Section 5, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps a current mechanism from being read as a requirement. Every defined term used here carries the meaning Section 3.1 assigns it, as WP2-SCOPE-005 requires, and every lifecycle term carries the meaning Section 8.3 assigns it, the release of an unused budget authorization being ordinary description in the carve-out sense Section 8.3 itself states for the release of a lease.

### 12.2 How the model-request record model is read

Each entry of the register in Section 12.3 states the same seven attributes in the same order. The register supplies the normative record-model definitions of this section, which WP2-MODEL-001 incorporates and makes binding clause by clause, except for the specific question an entry expressly assigns to a later section.

| Attribute | What the entry states |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | The domain within which the record's identity distinguishes one instance of the record type from every other instance of that record type. |
| Issuing or recording authority | The role answerable for creating the record or for recording the fact it states. |
| Required parent and source references | The identities the record has to name. |
| Cardinality | The counts that hold between records of this type and the records and entities they reference or are referenced by. |
| Point of existence | The point from which the record exists and can be referenced. |
| Correction or supersession rules | What may and may not be done with the record and its identity after it has been recorded. |
| May it exist without a provider call | Whether an instance of the record type can exist where no provider call was ever made for the logical model request concerned. |

**WP2-MODEL-001.** Every record of a record type of the register in Section 12.3 that a conforming laboratory creates, records, references, or relies upon **MUST** conform to every clause of the applicable register entry — its identity scope, issuing or recording authority, required parent and source references, cardinality, point of existence, correction or supersession rules, and rule on existence without a provider call — except only the specific question or clause that the entry expressly assigns to a later section, an exemption that reaches no other clause of that attribute or entry.

**Rationale.** An authority named in the register is a role — the party or process answerable for creating a record or recording a fact — and never a component, service, store, or product, exactly as Section 6.2 keeps every authority in the entity register a role; where a shared arrangement is concerned, the register speaks as WP2-ISO-015 speaks, of any component that serves model requests for more than one isolation unit, without requiring any component to exist. WP2-MODEL-001 is the single requirement that incorporates the register and makes every non-deferred clause binding, in the way WP2-ID-027 binds the entity register, WP2-LIFE-001 binds the lifecycle models, and WP2-SCHED-051 binds the assignment-authority record model, so that no individual table cell carries a requirement identifier of its own, and a cell that expressly assigns a question to a later section states a boundary rather than an answer, consistent with Section 4.3. The register below is the Section 12-local record model this section is permitted to state; it does not edit the Section 6 entity register, and where it names the logical model request it names the identity the Section 6.6 entry registers, adding no second identity for it and revising no clause of that entry.

### 12.3 The model-request record register

One register entry follows for each of the fifteen record types of the model-request and accounting graph, in lifecycle order. Each entry answers the seven attributes of Section 12.2 concretely, and a cell that expressly assigns a question to a later section defers exactly that question and nothing else.

**Logical model request.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | The identity scope the Section 6.6 register entry states in full: one logical model request, distinguished from every other model request across all concurrently executing studies and execution attempts once its recorded identity carries the lineage WP2-ID-016 requires, with WP2-ID-022 governing whether a locally scoped request identifier may form part of that identity. This entry adds no second identity for the logical request and revises no clause of the Section 6.6 entry, which continues to bind through WP2-ID-027. |
| Issuing or recording authority | The authority that owns the decision the request serves, as the Section 6.6 entry states, which Section 3.1 and WP2-SCOPE-009 keep distinct from serving provider identity and from route identity. |
| Required parent and source references | The references the Section 6.6 entry requires — exactly one execution-attempt identity, the planned-run and study identities above it, and the deciding episode or decision the request serves — together with the one immutable request specification bound to it under the next entry. |
| Cardinality | One execution attempt originates zero or more logical model requests, and one logical model request belongs to exactly one execution attempt, as the Section 6.6 entry states. One logical model request has zero or more local dispatch attempts and zero or more provider attempts beneath it, and it acquires at most one authoritative terminal simulation resolution, as WP2-MODEL-012 bounds. |
| Point of existence | At origination in the Section 6.6 sense, before any local dispatch attempt and before any provider attempt, as WP2-MODEL-006 requires. |
| Correction or supersession rules | Never reused, as the Section 6.6 entry states: a transport or delivery retry repeats the delivery of this already-identified request and keeps this identity, and a new cognitive decision or a materially changed request receives a new identity under WP2-MODEL-029. A material error is corrected under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026. |
| May it exist without a provider call | Yes. A logical model request may exist with zero local dispatch attempts and zero provider attempts, and the no-call resolution kind of the simulation-resolution entry below resolves such a request. |

**Immutable request specification.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every immutable request specification the laboratory records, across all studies and all concurrent execution: the request-specific binding record that fixes exactly what one logical model request asked — one such binding record per logical model request, distinct even where semantic content is identical — kept distinct, as WP2-MODEL-053 requires, from the immutable semantic content identity or digest it fixes, an identity two binding records may share exactly where their semantic content is identical, genuinely immutable, and exact. Its content and its content identity or digest carry no secret material, which is the secret-material half of the request-content rule that Section 16 states. |
| Issuing or recording authority | The authority that originates the logical model request, which binds the specification to the request it fixes. |
| Required parent and source references | Exactly one logical model request. |
| Cardinality | One logical model request binds exactly one immutable request specification record. One immutable semantic content identity or digest is shared by one or more binding records of identical, genuinely immutable and exact semantic content, and is never reused for different content, as WP2-MODEL-007 requires; two logical requests whose semantic content is identical remain two requests, each binding its own specification record, however their content identities compare. |
| Point of existence | At the origination of the logical model request it fixes, before any dispatch decision is made from it. |
| Correction or supersession rules | Never revised in place. A materially changed request is a new logical model request binding a new immutable request specification under WP2-MODEL-029, and a material recording error is corrected under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026. |
| May it exist without a provider call | Yes. The specification exists from origination whether or not any dispatch or provider attempt ever follows. |

**Local dispatch attempt.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every local dispatch attempt the laboratory records, across all logical model requests, execution attempts, and studies: one attempt to hand one already-identified request to a transport or provider path. |
| Issuing or recording authority | The authority that dispatches model requests for the owning execution attempt; where a component serves model requests for more than one isolation unit, its records are keyed as WP2-ISO-015 requires. |
| Required parent and source references | Exactly one logical model request, and through it the execution-attempt, planned-run, and study lineage; the identity and version of the registered retry or fallback policy where the dispatch proceeds under one, named through the retry and fallback lineage record. |
| Cardinality | One logical model request has zero or more local dispatch attempts, and each local dispatch attempt serves exactly one logical model request. A transport or delivery retry that is in fact dispatched produces a further local dispatch attempt for the same request, as WP2-MODEL-027 requires. One local dispatch attempt gives rise to zero or more provider attempts, each naming it. |
| Point of existence | When the dispatch of the already-identified request is attempted, which follows the request's origination under WP2-MODEL-006. |
| Correction or supersession rules | Never reused and never merged: a repeated delivery is a further local dispatch attempt rather than a revision of an earlier one, and a material recording error is corrected under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026. |
| May it exist without a provider call | Yes. A local dispatch attempt that never reaches a provider still happened, and its record is preserved with the outcome it in fact had. |

**Provider attempt.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every provider attempt the laboratory records, across all requests, execution attempts, and studies: one upstream call attempt, carrying a distinct identity per attempt, each provider retry and each fallback attempt among them. |
| Issuing or recording authority | The authority that makes upstream calls for the owning execution attempt, recorded with the configured provider, serving-provider, and route identities under which the call was made. |
| Required parent and source references | Exactly one logical model request and exactly one local dispatch attempt that produced it; the budget authorization or reservation under which the attempt was made, as WP2-MODEL-035 requires; the identity and version of the registered retry or fallback policy where the attempt is a further provider call under WP2-MODEL-028; and the exact provider-facing rendering or payload identity or digest the attempt used — exactly one per provider attempt, immutable for the exact rendered content it identifies, never reused for different rendered content, and shared with another provider attempt only where the rendered content is exactly identical, as WP2-MODEL-053 requires — its prompt and context identities, and the explicit derivation of that rendering from the immutable request specification of the logical model request it serves. |
| Cardinality | One logical model request has zero or more provider attempts. One provider attempt yields zero or one provider-accepted stage fact and zero or one provider-completed stage fact, each only where observed, and zero or more provider responses or failure results, a duplicate or late arrival among them recorded against the same attempt and preserved under WP2-MODEL-032. |
| Point of existence | When the upstream call is attempted. |
| Correction or supersession rules | Never reused. A further provider call for the same request is a distinct provider attempt under WP2-MODEL-028 rather than a revision or continuation of an earlier one, and a material recording error is corrected under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026. |
| May it exist without a provider call | No. A provider attempt is a provider call, although it may fail before provider acceptance is observed, and such an attempt remains a recorded provider attempt with its own accounting under WP2-MODEL-037. |

**Provider stage fact.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every provider-accepted and every provider-completed stage fact the laboratory records, in the stage vocabulary WP2-TIME-009 fixes. |
| Issuing or recording authority | The observing authority that records the stage where it is observable; a provider-reported boundary is recorded as the provider's, with the clock provenance WP2-TIME-015 requires. |
| Required parent and source references | Exactly one provider attempt, and through it the logical model request and its lineage. |
| Cardinality | One provider attempt carries zero or one provider-accepted stage fact and zero or one provider-completed stage fact. Where a boundary is not observable, the record states that it is unobserved or unavailable, together with the reason where one is known, under WP2-MODEL-017 rather than carrying an inferred stage fact. |
| Point of existence | When the stage is observed, or when the fact that it is unobserved or unavailable is recorded. |
| Correction or supersession rules | Never inferred after the fact, and never used to supply a missing boundary mark of a measured duration whose identified basis belongs to another clock or host, under WP2-TIME-023; a material recording error is corrected under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026. |
| May it exist without a provider call | No. A provider stage fact exists only against a provider attempt. |

**Provider response or failure result.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every provider response and every provider failure result the laboratory records, across all provider attempts. |
| Issuing or recording authority | The authority that observes or receives the result records it; the result's content is the provider's, recorded as such. |
| Required parent and source references | Exactly one provider attempt — the provider attempt that produced it, as WP2-MODEL-009 requires — and through it the logical model request. |
| Cardinality | Each provider response or failure result names exactly one provider attempt. One provider attempt may accumulate more than one recorded result where duplicates or late arrivals occur, each preserved with a non-authoritative disposition under WP2-MODEL-032 except the one, if any, that the engine-owned resolution path accepts. |
| Point of existence | When the response is received or the failure result is determined and recorded. |
| Correction or supersession rules | Preserved as recorded, never rewritten, merged, or silently promoted, except that a response Section 16.8 requires to be refused ordinary persistence and admission as evidence is preserved through the typed nonsecret incident record and any sanitized derivative that section states; the disposition vocabulary for duplicate, late, losing, rejected, and superseded results is assigned to Section 14. |
| May it exist without a provider call | No. |

**Local response receipt.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every local response receipt the laboratory records: the local receipt of one provider response, the local-response-received stage of WP2-TIME-009. |
| Issuing or recording authority | The authority that receives provider responses for the owning execution attempt. |
| Required parent and source references | The one provider response received, and through it the provider attempt and the logical model request. |
| Cardinality | One receipt is recorded per arrival of one provider response, and a duplicate arrival of the same response is a further local response receipt naming the same response rather than a revision of the first. |
| Point of existence | At local receipt. |
| Correction or supersession rules | Never merged with the provider-completion, validation, or resolution records whose separation WP2-MODEL-003 requires, nor with the local dispatch attempt whose own event WP2-MODEL-002 requires to be recorded distinctly; a material recording error is corrected under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026. |
| May it exist without a provider call | No. |

**Response validation.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every response validation the laboratory records: one evaluation of one provider response against the request content it was supposed to answer, the response-validated stage of WP2-TIME-009. |
| Issuing or recording authority | The validating authority for the owning execution attempt. |
| Required parent and source references | The exact provider response it evaluated, the provider attempt that produced that response and the exact provider-facing rendering or payload identity or digest against which the evaluation was made, the exact immutable request specification it evaluated that response against, the identity and version of the registered validation policy applied, the verdict recorded, and the validating authority, as WP2-MODEL-010 requires. |
| Cardinality | One response validation evaluates exactly one provider response against exactly one immutable request specification. A provider response is validated zero or more times, each evaluation a separate record with its own verdict, a response the simulation accepts carrying at least one such record, as the nonempty validation basis WP2-MODEL-054 requires of that acceptance. |
| Point of existence | When the evaluation is performed and its verdict recorded. |
| Correction or supersession rules | A verdict is never revised in place; a further evaluation is a further response validation record, and a material recording error is corrected under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026. |
| May it exist without a provider call | No. |

**Simulation resolution.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every simulation resolution the laboratory records: one engine-owned resolution act recorded for one logical model request, of exactly the kinds acceptance — the simulation-accepted stage of WP2-TIME-009 — rejection, expiry, supersession, cancellation, and no-call resolution, of which acceptance, expiry, supersession, cancellation, and no-call resolution resolve the request and a rejection resolves it only as WP2-MODEL-005 provides. The cancellation kind and the no-call resolution kind are resolution kinds of this record type, with their own clauses inside this entry, not separate record types. |
| Issuing or recording authority | The deciding engine authority of the independent simulated world the request serves, under charter Section 14.8's rule that the deterministic engine remains final authority over objective world state, legal affordances, and committed consequences; the no-call kind records the deciding authority it names. |
| Required parent and source references | Exactly one logical model request; the validated response it accepts and the nonempty validation basis WP2-MODEL-054 requires of the acceptance, where it is an acceptance, and the exact provider response it rejects or supersedes, where it is a rejection or a supersession; the deciding episode or decision it resolves; for the cancellation kind, the identity of the cancellation record of the Sections 8 and 9 cancellation authority whose effect reached the request — the recording of the resolution itself remaining with the deciding engine authority, the cancellation record being its triggering fact, consistent with WP2-MODEL-044; and, for the no-call kind, its reason and its deciding authority, as WP2-MODEL-016 requires. |
| Cardinality | One logical model request acquires at most one authoritative terminal simulation resolution, decided at the authority boundary WP2-MODEL-012 requires, and may acquire none: a request never resolved remains a validly represented pending request, in the pattern WP2-LIFE-008 establishes for a planned run whose authoritative outcome is never recorded, so a request whose every transport failed under no applicable registered retry or fallback policy remains representable as pending rather than forced into a resolution kind. A rejection resolves the request only as the registered retry or fallback policy provides, under WP2-MODEL-005; a rejection, a late arrival, or a losing resolution operation that is not authoritative is preserved with a non-authoritative disposition under WP2-MODEL-032. |
| Point of existence | When the deciding engine authority records the resolution. |
| Correction or supersession rules | Never silently altered: a response arriving after timeout, supersession, cancellation, or another authoritative resolution changes no recorded resolution under WP2-MODEL-015, and a material recording error is corrected under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026. |
| May it exist without a provider call | Yes. The no-call resolution kind resolves a logical model request that has zero local dispatch attempts and zero provider attempts, recording its reason and its deciding authority, and the cancellation kind resolves a request whether or not any dispatch or provider attempt was made for it. |

**Idempotency resolution.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every idempotency resolution the laboratory records: the typed resolution of one further presentation of an already-known request identity, of exactly three families of kinds — idempotent replay, answered from the recorded result; identity/content conflict refusal; and the unresolved-presentation dispositions WP2-MODEL-055 states, which are coalescing with or attachment to the in-flight request, deferral, refusal as a duplicate preserving the original request, and authorized transport retry. |
| Issuing or recording authority | The authority that resolves presentations of model requests; where a component serves model requests for more than one isolation unit, the recorded results it answers from are keyed as WP2-ISO-015 requires, so that one unit's presentation is never answered from another unit's recorded result and never attached to another unit's in-flight request. |
| Required parent and source references | The logical model request identity presented; the identity and version of the registered presentation-disposition policy under which the presentation was resolved, as WP2-MODEL-055 requires; the recorded result answered from, where the kind is idempotent replay; both content identities — the recorded one and the conflicting one — where the kind is identity/content conflict refusal, as WP2-MODEL-026 requires; where the kind is coalescing or attachment, the unresolved logical model request of the identity presented to which the presentation was attached or coalesced — the in-flight request WP2-MODEL-055 names, which is the unresolved request itself rather than any attempt beneath it, named whether or not any local dispatch attempt or provider attempt of it is presently in flight — together with the in-flight local dispatch attempt or provider attempt of that same logical model request, where one exists and is relevant; and the further local dispatch attempt the retry produced, where the kind is authorized transport retry and the retry was in fact dispatched, as WP2-MODEL-027 requires. |
| Cardinality | One idempotency resolution resolves exactly one further presentation. One further presentation receives exactly one current idempotency resolution, of exactly one of the kinds this entry states, a correction under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026 replacing rather than adding to it. One logical model request accumulates zero or more idempotency resolutions over its history. Of the kinds this entry states, only the authorized-transport-retry disposition leads to a further local dispatch attempt, and no other kind creates a provider attempt, a charge, or a second simulation resolution. |
| Point of existence | At the authority boundary at which the presentation is resolved, in the terms WP2-MODEL-024 fixes. |
| Correction or supersession rules | A refusal record of any kind this entry states is preserved as recorded — never deleted, overwritten, truncated, or rendered unverifiable — an identity/content conflict refusal carrying both content identities as WP2-MODEL-026 requires, the same evidence-preserving discipline WP2-ISO-031 and WP2-ISO-032 apply to the refusals WP2-ISO-030 requires; no idempotency resolution is rewritten to change its kind or its named identities, and a material recording error is corrected under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026. |
| May it exist without a provider call | Yes. An idempotent replay answers from a recorded result and makes no provider call; an identity/content conflict refusal, a coalescing or attachment, a deferral, and a duplicate refusal preserving the original request dispatch nothing; and an authorized transport retry is recorded by the disposition itself and, where it is in fact dispatched, by the further local dispatch attempt it leads to, which may itself never reach a provider. |

**Retry and fallback lineage record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every retry and fallback lineage record the laboratory records: the explicit lineage of one transport or delivery retry, one provider retry, or one fallback undertaken for one logical model request. |
| Issuing or recording authority | The authority that applies the registered retry or fallback policy for the owning execution attempt. |
| Required parent and source references | The logical model request; the prior local dispatch attempt or provider attempt the retry or fallback proceeds from; the identity and version of the registered retry or fallback policy applied; and every changed model, provider, serving-provider, route, prompt, context, and policy identity, together with the changed provider-facing rendering or payload identity or digest where the fallback changes it, which WP2-MODEL-030 makes a condition of a fallback's preserving its originating request and which this entry requires of every retry or fallback it records. |
| Cardinality | One retry and fallback lineage record describes exactly one retry or fallback, and one logical model request carries zero or more of them. |
| Point of existence | Recorded when the retry or fallback is undertaken — at establishment, in the WP2-ID-023 sense, rather than reconstructed afterwards. |
| Correction or supersession rules | Never rewritten to name a different predecessor or to omit a changed identity WP2-MODEL-030 requires it to record, and never rewritten so as to conceal an unregistered treatment change, which WP2-MODEL-031 prohibits; a material recording error is corrected under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026. |
| May it exist without a provider call | Yes. A retry or fallback whose further local dispatch attempt never reaches a provider is still recorded lineage. |

**Budget authorization or reservation.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every budget authorization or reservation the laboratory records, across every registered budget scope and every unit whose dispatch or reserved capacity it concerns. |
| Issuing or recording authority | The authority that decides authorization under the applicable registered budget policies. |
| Required parent and source references | The registered budget policy and scope identity and version of every scope authorized against; where it authorizes a dispatch, the logical model request whose provider dispatch it authorizes — the provider attempt begins to exist only at the dispatch so authorized, so the authorization names the logical model request and the registered scopes, and the provider attempt and its incurred usage or charge records name the authorization; where it reserves capacity under WP2-MODEL-048, the source and destination scopes its recorded transfer or reservation lineage names; and the study, planned-run, and execution-attempt lineage WP2-ISO-016 requires attribution to. |
| Cardinality | One budget authorization or reservation authorizes the making of one provider attempt against every applicable registered budget scope, deciding all of those scopes at the one boundary WP2-MODEL-036 requires, or reserves capacity for a named scope or unit under WP2-MODEL-048; an authorization is later settled, released, refused, cancelled, or held as unresolved exposure through the settlement or reconciliation entry below. |
| Point of existence | At the authorization decision, which precedes the provider dispatch it authorizes, as WP2-MODEL-035 requires. |
| Correction or supersession rules | Released or otherwise dispositioned only through a recorded event under WP2-MODEL-045, never by the disappearance of process-local state; a material recording error is corrected under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026. |
| May it exist without a provider call | Yes. An authorization may be released unused, and an authorized dispatch may never reach a provider. |

**Incurred usage or charge record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every incurred usage or charge record the laboratory records, one accounting record set per provider attempt, each provider retry and each fallback attempt among them. |
| Issuing or recording authority | The recording authority for the value kind concerned: a provider-reported value is recorded as the provider's, a locally derived value as the laboratory's own derivation, and a billed or reconciled value as its billing or reconciliation source's, each distinguishable under WP2-MODEL-040. |
| Required parent and source references | Exactly one provider attempt and the budget authorization or reservation that attempt was made under; every registered budget scope charged; and the value kinds of the Section 12.8 table that apply to it. |
| Cardinality | One provider attempt carries its own incurred usage or charge records, separately from every other attempt under WP2-MODEL-037, with provider-reported, locally derived, and billed or reconciled values separately recorded where more than one exists. |
| Point of existence | When usage is known, partially known, or determined to be unknown — an unknown value being represented as unresolved rather than as zero, under WP2-MODEL-038 and WP2-MODEL-041. |
| Correction or supersession rules | Never overwritten: a later or corrected value is recorded through the settlement or reconciliation entry below or as a correction under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026, with the earlier value preserved. |
| May it exist without a provider call | No. Incurred usage exists only against a provider attempt, although whether that attempt was accepted or charged may itself be unknown, in which case the exposure is unresolved rather than zero. |

**Settlement or reconciliation record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every settlement or reconciliation record the laboratory records: the typed disposition of usage or of a dispatch against authorization, of exactly the kinds settled, released, refused, cancelled, and unresolved exposure, the refused kind reaching the refusal of a dispatch already authorized, the refusal WP2-MODEL-043 requires in place of an authorization, and the recorded rejection of a losing or stale authorization operation that WP2-MODEL-036 requires to be preserved. |
| Issuing or recording authority | Conditional on the kind recorded: the authority that decided the authorization operation records the refusal recorded in place of an authorization under WP2-MODEL-043 and the rejection of a losing or stale authorization operation preserved under WP2-MODEL-036; the applicable reconciling authority under the registered budget policies records settlement, release, cancellation, the refusal of a dispatch already authorized, and unresolved-exposure reconciliation. The two roles may coincide, and nothing in this entry depends on that coincidence. |
| Required parent and source references | The budget authorization or reservation it dispositions, where one was recorded, and, for a refusal recorded in place of an authorization under WP2-MODEL-043, the logical model request whose dispatch was refused, the local dispatch attempt refused where one was recorded, and the exhausted or unavailable registered budget scope; for the rejection of a losing or stale authorization operation, the logical model request whose dispatch that operation attempted to authorize, the registered budget scopes it attempted to authorize against, the authority that attempted it, and the reason it did not prevail, as WP2-MODEL-036 requires; the incurred usage or charge records it settles against, where any exist; and the registered budget policy and scope identities and versions concerned. |
| Cardinality | One budget authorization or reservation receives zero or more settlement or reconciliation records over time; one refusal recorded in place of an authorization under WP2-MODEL-043, and one rejection of a losing or stale authorization operation preserved under WP2-MODEL-036, each stand on their own and concern exactly one refused or unauthorized dispatch of exactly one logical model request; and an unresolved exposure remains represented until a recorded reconciliation or disposition resolves it, as WP2-MODEL-046 requires. |
| Point of existence | At the recorded settlement, release, refusal, cancellation, or unresolved-exposure determination. |
| Correction or supersession rules | A further reconciliation names the record it supersedes rather than rewriting it, under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026. |
| May it exist without a provider call | Yes. The release of an unused authorization, and the refusal or cancellation of a dispatch never made, involve no provider call. |

**Provider-behavior observation.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every provider-behavior observation the laboratory records: one observation of one of the phenomena Section 12.9 enumerates — provider queueing and latency, rate-limit responses, outages and transport failures, correlated provider or route failure windows, provider rejection, timeout, model, serving-provider, or route substitution, fallback exposure, retry exposure, late completion, and returned-model or observed-provider mismatch. |
| Issuing or recording authority | The observing authority, interpreting what it observed under a registered policy it identifies by identity and version. |
| Required parent and source references | The identification set WP2-MODEL-050 states: the affected logical model requests and provider attempts; the provider and route identities concerned and the nonsecret provider-project or account-scope identity of the path observed, carried in the WP2-SCHED-047 form wherever a raw provider-project or account identifier would expose secret material; the applicable time domain under WP2-TIME-001, the timeout domain under WP2-TIME-005 for a timeout; the observing authority; and the identity and version of the interpreting registered policy, naming each of those that applies and stating the absence of any that does not, or that it was not observable. |
| Cardinality | One observation names one phenomenon over the requests, attempts, and window it concerns; a unit affected by several phenomena carries several observations, and a correlated window names every affected unit it reaches. |
| Point of existence | When the phenomenon is observed; what is not observable is recorded as unobserved or unavailable, together with the reason where one is known, rather than inferred, in the discipline WP2-TIME-010 and WP2-TIME-011 state. |
| Correction or supersession rules | The observation remains evidence: it never amends the registration it diverges from, under WP2-MODEL-052, and a material recording error is corrected under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026. The evidence form and retention of these records are assigned to Section 13, and what makes a provider deviation material is assigned to Section 14. |
| May it exist without a provider call | Yes. An outage, a rate-limit window, or a correlated-failure window may be observed and recorded while no provider call of the affected units proceeds, naming the requests and attempts it affected, refusals and never-dispatched requests among them. |

### 12.4 The three lifecycle layers and their stage events

Three lifecycle layers are kept apart. Each progresses on its own records, and none of them is the terminal outcome of another.

| Layer | What progresses | What its terminal outcome is not |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Logical request and simulation resolution | One logical model request, from origination to at most one authoritative terminal simulation resolution, that resolution engine-owned under charter Section 14.8. | Not a budget settlement, and not supplied by any provider attempt's outcome: a request whose every provider attempt failed remains pending for a registered retry or fallback where the applicable policy so provides, and is otherwise resolved only by the engine-owned resolution path, exactly as a request whose provider attempt succeeded is. |
| Local dispatch and provider attempt | Each local dispatch attempt and each provider attempt beneath a request, through the provider-accepted and provider-completed stage facts of WP2-TIME-009 where they are observable and through the local-response-received and response-validated stages of the result it produced, to its own recorded outcome. | Not the logical request's terminal outcome, which WP2-MODEL-004 keeps separate, and not a usage settlement, which the budget layer records. |
| Budget authorization, usage, and reconciliation | Authorization or reservation, incurred usage, and settlement, release, refusal, cancellation, or unresolved exposure, per applicable registered budget scope. | Not the request's resolution and not any attempt's outcome: an authorization released unused resolves no request, and a settled charge completes no provider attempt. |

Fourteen stage events are kept apart across those layers. Each is a separate recorded fact with its own provenance, none of them implies another, and the five stage names WP2-TIME-009 fixes — provider-accepted, provider-completed, local-response-received, response-validated, and simulation-accepted — name the same stages here.

| Event | Layer | What it is | What it is not |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Request created or emitted | Logical request and simulation resolution | The origination of one logical model request, in the Section 6.6 sense, binding its immutable request specification. | Not a dispatch, not a provider call, and not a budget authorization. |
| Dispatch attempted | Local dispatch and provider attempt | One local dispatch attempt hands the already-identified request to a transport or provider path. | Not proof that a provider was reached, and not a further logical request. |
| Provider accepted, where observable | Local dispatch and provider attempt | The provider-accepted stage fact of one provider attempt, recorded only where observed. | Not completion, not local receipt, and never inferred from a neighboring timestamp under WP2-MODEL-017. |
| Provider completed, where observable | Local dispatch and provider attempt | The provider-completed stage fact of one provider attempt, recorded only where observed. | Not local receipt, not validation, and not simulation acceptance, under WP2-MODEL-003. |
| Local response received | Local dispatch and provider attempt | The local-response-received stage: one local response receipt of one provider response. | Not validation, not acceptance, and not the request's resolution. |
| Response validated | Local dispatch and provider attempt | The response-validated stage: one response validation of one response against one immutable request specification, carrying the complete naming WP2-MODEL-010 requires. | Not simulation acceptance, which WP2-MODEL-013 keeps engine-owned. |
| Response accepted by the simulation | Logical request and simulation resolution | The simulation-accepted stage: the engine-owned acceptance naming the validated response it accepts, the deciding episode or decision it resolves, and the validation basis WP2-MODEL-054 requires. | Not a consequence of provider success, local receipt, or validation alone. |
| Response rejected | Logical request and simulation resolution | An engine-owned rejection of one response, recorded with its reason. | Not, by itself, the resolution of the logical request, under WP2-MODEL-005. |
| Response timed out | Logical request and simulation resolution | A timeout of the request or its response, evaluated and recorded in the time domain WP2-TIME-005 requires it to identify. | Not a logical expiry and not a logical supersession, which WP2-TIME-007 prohibits recording a timeout as, a logical expiry being a separate fact the engine records in its own domain; and not the erasure of a late response, under WP2-MODEL-014. |
| Response superseded | Logical request and simulation resolution | An engine-owned supersession recording that a response no longer answers the deciding episode or decision. | Not a deletion, and not a provider failure. |
| Fallback used | Local dispatch and provider attempt | A further attempt under the registered fallback policy, carried by a retry and fallback lineage record naming every changed identity. | Not a new logical request where WP2-MODEL-030 preserves the originating one, and never a concealed treatment change, under WP2-MODEL-031. |
| Provider or transport retry | Local dispatch and provider attempt | A transport or delivery retry dispatched again and producing a further local dispatch attempt, or a further provider attempt under the registered retry policy. | Not a new logical request, not a new decision, and — where it is a transport or delivery retry — not interchangeable with a registered replacement or a process restart, under WP2-SCOPE-006. |
| Cancellation | Logical request and simulation resolution | A recorded cancellation, under the applicable cancellation authority of Sections 8 and 9, preventing further provider dispatches under the cancelled authority and preserving in-flight exposure and incurred usage as WP2-MODEL-044 requires. | Not an erasure of request history, not a settlement of exposure already incurred, and not an absence of resolution: where a cancellation resolves the request, the cancellation resolution kind of the simulation-resolution entry of the register in Section 12.3 records it. |
| No-call resolution | Logical request and simulation resolution | The engine-owned resolution of a request for which no provider call is made, recording its reason and deciding authority. | Not a failure, not a timeout, and not an absence of record. |

**WP2-MODEL-002.** A conforming laboratory **MUST** record each of the fourteen stage events of the table in this section that occurs — request created or emitted, dispatch attempted, provider accepted where observable, provider completed where observable, local response received, response validated, response accepted by the simulation, response rejected, response timed out, response superseded, fallback used, provider or transport retry, cancellation, and no-call resolution — as a distinct event, separately identified and separately attributable to the records of the register in Section 12.3 and the entities of the Section 6 register that it concerns.

**WP2-MODEL-003.** A conforming laboratory **MUST NOT** collapse any two or more of provider completion, local response receipt, response validation, and simulation acceptance into one recorded event, or represent two or more of the three lifecycle layers of this section as one lifecycle, one progression, or one status.

**WP2-MODEL-004.** A provider attempt's terminal outcome **MUST NOT** be treated, recorded, or reported as, by itself, the terminal outcome or resolution of the logical model request it serves.

**WP2-MODEL-005.** The rejection of a provider response **MUST NOT** by itself resolve the logical model request that response served, otherwise than as the registered retry or fallback policy identified for that request provides, the request remaining pending for a registered retry or fallback where that policy so provides.

**Rationale.** These events are recorded where each in fact occurs, in the same discipline WP2-SCHED-001 applies to scheduling events: a laboratory whose request path produces no fallback, no retry, and no timeout records none of them, and WP2-MODEL-002 requires the events that occur to be recorded as distinct rather than requiring all fourteen to occur. Collapsing them is the ordinary way a model-serving path loses its evidence: Section 5.6 records that the current gateway record separates no provider-accepted stage from a provider-completed one and collapses distinct budget causes into one failure code, which is evidence about the current system rather than a requirement, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps it from being read as one. The three layers are separated for the same reason the five scalar status dimensions of Section 8.4 are: a single word that means at once "the provider answered", "the engine accepted", and "the budget settled" cannot represent a request whose provider attempt succeeded but whose response the engine rejected, or an accepted response whose charge remains unresolved. WP2-MODEL-005 states the rejection consequence in prohibition form so that the pending state needs no new value: what keeps a rejected response from ending the request is that no rule ends it, except the registered policy the request's records identify.

### 12.5 Logical-request and response invariants

**WP2-MODEL-006.** Every logical model request **MUST** exist as a recorded, identified request — under the identity the Section 6.6 register entry states — before any local dispatch attempt or provider attempt is made for it.

**WP2-MODEL-007.** A logical model request identity, and the immutable semantic content identity or digest that a request's immutable request specification fixes, **MUST NOT** be reused for different request content, two logical model requests whose semantic content is identical remaining two distinct request-specification bindings however their content identities compare.

**WP2-MODEL-053.** A conforming laboratory **MUST** keep three request layers distinct and recorded — the request-specific immutable-request-specification binding record of one logical model request, one such record per request as the register in Section 12.3 states; the immutable semantic content identity or digest that record fixes; and, for each provider attempt, the exact provider-facing rendering or payload identity or digest that attempt used, together with its prompt and context identities and its recorded derivation from the immutable request specification of the logical model request it serves — identical, genuinely immutable and exact semantic content being permitted one shared content identity across distinct request-specification bindings, a materially changed semantic request receiving a new logical model request under WP2-MODEL-029, each provider attempt binding exactly one provider-facing rendering or payload identity or digest, that identity or digest being immutable for the exact provider-facing rendered content it identifies, never reused for different rendered content, and shared by two provider attempts only where their provider-facing rendered content is exactly identical, and no payload format, canonicalization scheme, digest scheme, hashing scheme, prompt representation, or wire encoding being selected by this specification.

**WP2-MODEL-008.** Every local dispatch attempt and every provider attempt **MUST** name the one logical model request it serves.

**WP2-MODEL-009.** Every provider response and every provider failure result **MUST** name exactly one provider attempt, which is the provider attempt that produced it.

**WP2-MODEL-010.** Every response validation **MUST** name the exact provider response it evaluated, the provider attempt that produced that response and the exact provider-facing rendering or payload identity or digest against which the evaluation was made, the exact immutable request specification it evaluated that response against, the identity and version of the registered validation policy applied, the verdict recorded, and the validating authority.

**WP2-MODEL-011.** Every simulation acceptance **MUST** name the validated response it accepts, the deciding episode or decision it resolves, and the validation basis WP2-MODEL-054 requires of it.

**WP2-MODEL-054.** Every simulation acceptance **MUST** identify, as its validation basis, one or more response-validation records — the exact record, or the complete nonempty set of records, that the registered validation policy applicable to it requires — together with that policy's identity and version, the resolved policy-level verdict or combination of verdicts under which that policy permits acceptance, the immutable request specification concerned, and the exact provider-facing rendering or payload identity or digest of the provider attempt concerned, no response being accepted where the validation basis so required is empty, missing, unsuccessful, unresolved, or contradictory without a registered resolution rule, or is attributed to a different provider response, immutable request specification, or provider attempt, or relies on a response-validation record naming a registered validation policy identity or version other than the one the acceptance identifies.

**WP2-MODEL-012.** A logical model request **MUST NOT** acquire more than one authoritative terminal simulation resolution, the acceptance of a terminal simulation resolution being decided at an authority boundary that is atomic, or safe against competing resolution operations to an equivalent degree, at which no interleaving of competing resolution operations can make two of them authoritative for one logical model request or leave one partially recorded, and at which the resolution of a race between competing resolution operations is never inferred from wall-clock arrival order.

**WP2-MODEL-013.** A conforming laboratory **MUST NOT** infer, derive, or record simulation acceptance from provider success, local response receipt, response validation, or any combination of them, the acceptance being an engine-owned resolution recorded only by the deciding engine authority of the independent simulated world the request serves, which charter Section 14.8 makes final authority over objective world state, legal affordances, and committed consequences, a complete validation basis under WP2-MODEL-054 being necessary for an acceptance and never by itself conferring one.

**WP2-MODEL-014.** A provider response arriving after a timeout, a supersession, a cancellation, or another authoritative resolution of the logical model request it serves **MUST** remain preserved and attributable to the provider attempt and the logical model request it belongs to, that preservation being discharged, where Section 16 requires the response to be refused ordinary persistence and admission as evidence, by the typed nonsecret incident record and any sanitized derivative that section states, which preserve the fact, attribution, and lineage of the response without persisting the secret material.

**WP2-MODEL-015.** A provider response of the kind WP2-MODEL-014 preserves **MUST NOT** silently alter simulation state, silently become an accepted response, or silently change any recorded simulation resolution.

**WP2-MODEL-016.** A no-call resolution **MUST** record its reason and its deciding authority and resolve a logical model request that has zero local dispatch attempts and zero provider attempts.

**WP2-MODEL-017.** Where a provider-accepted or provider-completed boundary of a provider attempt is not observable, the provider stage fact record **MUST** state that the boundary is unobserved or unavailable, together with the reason where one is known, as WP2-TIME-010 requires, rather than carry a stage fact inferred in any of the ways WP2-TIME-011 prohibits.

**WP2-MODEL-018.** A conforming laboratory **MUST NOT** join, attribute, or resolve any record of the register in Section 12.3 to another record, or to an entity of the Section 6 register, by wall-clock proximity, arrival order, or temporal adjacency, otherwise than as a provider-behavior observation records, under the registered policy WP2-MODEL-050 requires it to identify, which requests and attempts an observed window in fact reached, and otherwise than as WP2-TIME-015 governs a comparison of timing records across hosts.

**Rationale.** These invariants state the record-model half of rules whose other halves are already established, and they redefine none of them: WP2-MODEL-012 bounds the resolution count in the way WP2-ID-009 bounds the authoritative accepted outcome of a planned run — at most one, possibly never reached — and the engine ownership WP2-MODEL-013 protects is the authority charter Section 14.8 assigns, which Section 11.4 already keeps logical time under; WP2-MODEL-017 states what the record holds and leaves the timing rules themselves to WP2-TIME-010 and WP2-TIME-011; and WP2-MODEL-018 applies to request records the join discipline WP2-ISO-014 states between isolation units and WP2-TIME-016 states across hosts. WP2-MODEL-014 and WP2-MODEL-015 divide the late-response rule into its preservation half and its prohibition half so that each states one obligation on one subject: the late response is evidence — of provider behavior, of transport delay, and of the resolution race it lost — and evidence that changed simulation state silently would contradict the resolution record the engine already made authoritative. A registered validation policy may define the combination of verdicts, or the conflict-resolution rule, under which it permits acceptance, but the validation basis WP2-MODEL-054 requires is one or more response-validation records, and an empty basis is never a sufficient one, however the policy is written. The engine-owned lifecycle facts Section 5.6 records about the current repository — the engine's resolved-request registry recording accepted, expired, and superseded resolutions — are evidence that such a resolution model is representable, not a requirement, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps them from being read as one.

### 12.6 Complete attribution

**WP2-MODEL-019.** Every logical model request, local dispatch attempt, provider attempt, provider response or failure result, response validation, simulation resolution, incurred usage or charge record, and budget authorization, settlement, or other budget event **MUST** be attributable, through explicitly recorded lineage, as applicable, to the study identity and version; the registration identity and version; the experiment identity and version; the condition and treatment identities; the planned-run identity; the execution-attempt identity; the laboratory-worker identity; the assignment-authority grant in exercise of which the operation was carried out, together with whether that grant was current for that operation, as the recorded authority facts of the Section 9.6 record model establish it; the originating cognitive episode or decision, and a specific decision where one exists; the decision authority; the prompt and context identity or version; the exact provider-facing rendering or payload identity or digest, for a provider attempt, every record beneath it, and every simulation acceptance whose validation basis WP2-MODEL-054 states; the requested model identity; the configured provider, serving-provider, and route identities; the observed upstream provider and returned model identity, where observable; a nonsecret provider-project or account-scope identity, where applicable; the nonsecret credential reference and version used, for a provider attempt and every recorded access to secret material; the registered concurrency-profile identity and version; the applicable time-domain records; and every budget scope to which the operation is charged — naming each of those that applies and stating the absence of any that does not.

**WP2-MODEL-020.** The requested model identity, the configured provider identity, the configured serving-provider identity, the route identity, the observed upstream provider identity, and the returned model identity **MUST** remain separately recorded, distinct identities wherever each of them is recorded, none of them conflated with, substituted for, or inferred from another, and none of them conflated with the decision authority that WP2-SCOPE-009 keeps distinct from serving provider identity and route identity.

**WP2-MODEL-021.** A mismatch or substitution among the requested, configured, observed, and returned identities of a model request **MUST** be recorded as a typed deviation, fallback, refusal, or violation, typed under the registered policy applicable to it and naming that policy by identity and version where one applies, and typed as an unregistered divergence naming the absence of any applicable registered policy where none does.

**WP2-MODEL-022.** A mismatch or substitution of the kind WP2-MODEL-021 records **MUST NOT** silently rewrite the registered treatment, the registered condition, or any other part of the registered design of the study in which it occurred.

**WP2-MODEL-023.** Every join this section requires among the records of the register in Section 12.3, and between any of them and an entity of the Section 6 register, **MUST** be recorded explicitly, at the time it is established, as a join by recorded identity that can be read from the record rather than reconstructed from naming, ordering, storage location, or timing.

**Rationale.** WP2-MODEL-019 completes the record-placement half of WP2-ID-016, which fixes what a model request has to be attributable to — the study, the planned run, the execution attempt, and the deciding episode or decision — while assigning where each identity is written to this section; the answer given here is that placement is not fixed. Nothing in WP2-MODEL-019 or WP2-MODEL-023 requires any of the twenty identities to reside on the request envelope itself: the WP2-SCOPE-009 rationale asks which record carries each identity and how those records are joined, and the answer is that any record may carry any of them, provided the join is explicit, identity-based, and recorded at establishment, in exactly the discipline WP2-ID-023 states for lineage and WP2-SCHED-045 states for the concurrency-profile join. The absence-stating close follows WP2-TIME-020 and WP2-SCHED-035, because an inapplicable identity and an omitted one are different facts and only the first is checkable — a model request whose provider attempt returned no observed upstream provider identity, because the route reported none, states that absence rather than leaving the field to be guessed at. WP2-MODEL-021 and WP2-MODEL-022 apply to request-level identity divergence the same two-sided rule WP2-SCHED-042 and WP2-SCHED-043 apply to concurrency-profile divergence: recorded and typed on one side, powerless to amend the registered design on the other. Section 5.6 records that no current wire artifact can be joined to a study, a planned run, an execution attempt, or a budget scope without an external mapping; that gap is what this section closes, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps the current mechanism from being read as the answer.

### 12.7 Duplicate delivery, retries, fallback, and idempotency

**WP2-MODEL-024.** A duplicate delivery of one logical model request carrying its one immutable request specification **MUST NOT** create a second logical model request, a second immutable request specification, or a second request identity, every further presentation of an already-known request identity receiving the typed presentation disposition WP2-MODEL-055 requires at an authority boundary that is atomic, or safe against competing presentations of one logical model request identity to an equivalent degree, so that no interleaving of competing presentations of one request identity can dispatch, charge, or resolve two of them as though no authoritative terminal simulation resolution of that request stood at that boundary, and at which the outcome of a race between competing presentations is never inferred from wall-clock arrival order.

**WP2-MODEL-025.** An idempotent replay — a further presentation of a request identity whose logical model request an authoritative terminal simulation resolution has already resolved, as that resolution stands at the authority boundary at which the presentation is resolved, with matching request content — **MUST** be answered from the recorded result that authoritative resolution accepted or recorded, creating no further provider attempt, no further charge or incurred usage, and no further simulation resolution.

**WP2-MODEL-026.** A presentation of an already-known request identity with conflicting request content **MUST** be refused as a typed identity/content conflict whose record preserves both content identities — the recorded one and the conflicting one — rather than answered, merged, or dispatched.

**WP2-MODEL-055.** Every further presentation of an already-known logical model request identity **MUST** receive exactly one current typed, preserved presentation disposition, of exactly one of the kinds this requirement states, under a registered presentation-disposition policy identified by identity and version — idempotent replay under WP2-MODEL-025, where an authoritative terminal simulation resolution of the request stands at that boundary and the content matches; identity/content conflict refusal under WP2-MODEL-026, where the content conflicts; and, where the content matches and no authoritative terminal simulation resolution stands, coalescing with or attachment to the in-flight request, deferral, refusal as a duplicate preserving the original request, or a transport or delivery retry that policy authorizes — only the authorized transport-retry disposition creating a further local dispatch attempt, no disposition creating a provider attempt, a charge, or a second simulation resolution otherwise than as WP2-MODEL-028 and Section 12.8 permit of an authorized retry, and every disposition being decided at the authority boundary WP2-MODEL-024 requires.

**WP2-MODEL-027.** A transport or delivery retry **MUST** retain the logical model request identity and the immutable request specification of the request whose delivery it repeats, arise for a matching presentation of an unresolved request only as the disposition the registered policy WP2-MODEL-055 names authorizes, and receive its own local dispatch attempt record where it is in fact dispatched.

**WP2-MODEL-028.** A further provider call for one logical model request **MUST** be a distinct provider attempt carrying its own provider-attempt identity, made only under a registered retry or fallback policy whose identity and version the retry and fallback lineage record names, and recorded distinguishably from a transport or delivery retry of the same request.

**WP2-MODEL-029.** A new cognitive decision, and a materially changed request, **MUST** receive a new logical model request identity, rather than being recorded, reported, or dispatched as a transport or delivery retry, a provider retry, or a fallback of an earlier request.

**WP2-MODEL-030.** A fallback **MUST** preserve the originating logical model request only where it continues to serve the same registered cognitive operation that request serves and its retry and fallback lineage record names every changed model, provider, serving-provider, route, prompt, context, and policy identity, and the changed provider-facing rendering or payload identity or digest where the fallback changes it.

**WP2-MODEL-031.** A fallback or a retry **MUST NOT** conceal, absorb, or silently apply an unregistered treatment change.

**WP2-MODEL-032.** Duplicate, late, losing, rejected, and superseded provider responses, and every simulation-resolution operation that does not become the authoritative terminal resolution of its logical model request, **MUST** remain preserved with recorded, distinguishable non-authoritative dispositions, rather than deleted, overwritten, merged into another record, or silently promoted to authoritative, a response that Section 16.8 requires to be refused ordinary persistence and admission as evidence being preserved instead through the typed nonsecret incident record and any sanitized derivative that section states.

**WP2-MODEL-033.** A process restart, a restart of a laboratory worker's execution agent, a restart of any component that serves model requests for more than one isolation unit, or the loss or reset of a provider session **MUST NOT** reset idempotency state, erase or truncate request or response history, or free any request identity for reuse.

**Rationale.** These requirements answer the idempotency question the WP2-ISO-015 rationale assigns here — which rule applies to a genuine repeat of one request — and they answer it in three typed families rather than one, each of them a delivery repetition inside the Section 3.1 term rather than outside it, the difference being how the presentation resolves, every further presentation of an already-known request identity receiving the typed presentation disposition WP2-MODEL-055 requires at the race-safe authority boundary WP2-MODEL-024 fixes: a repeat with matching content of a request an authoritative terminal simulation resolution has already resolved is answered from that recorded result as an idempotent replay under WP2-MODEL-025, dispatching nothing; a repeat whose content conflicts is an identity/content conflict refused under WP2-MODEL-026, this section applying to that refusal the same evidence-preserving discipline WP2-ISO-031 and WP2-ISO-032 apply to the refusals WP2-ISO-030 requires, rather than adding a condition to WP2-ISO-030; and a repeat with matching content of a request no authoritative terminal simulation resolution has yet resolved receives whichever of the dispositions WP2-MODEL-055 states the registered presentation-disposition policy provides — coalescing with or attachment to the in-flight request, deferral, refusal as a duplicate preserving the original request, or an authorized transport or delivery retry — no one of which this specification prefers, only the authorized transport retry among them leading to a further delivery, and that further delivery remaining recorded by the further local dispatch attempt record it produces under WP2-MODEL-027 rather than by the disposition record alone. What separates a retry from a new request is the decision served, not the transport history: WP2-MODEL-029 keeps a new decision from hiding inside an old identity, WP2-SCOPE-006 already prohibits recording a transport or delivery retry interchangeably with a registered replacement or a process restart, and WP2-SCHED-005 resolves the analogous repetition for assignment delivery. The fallback rules keep the treatment honest: a fallback that changes a model, route, or prompt within the registered policy is recorded lineage under WP2-MODEL-030, and one that would change what the study tests is an unregistered treatment change that WP2-MODEL-031 prohibits concealing, with WP2-SCHED-042 and WP2-SCHED-043 governing the divergence record where one arises. The disposition vocabulary for the preserved non-authoritative responses WP2-MODEL-032 requires is assigned to Section 14. WP2-MODEL-033 states the restart half of the durability rule, and WP2-MODEL-042 states the accounting half; Section 5.6 records that the repository's current per-run idempotency and budget state is process-local, evictable, and reset at every attempt boundary, which is a current limitation and, under WP2-SCOPE-010 and Section 5.14 item 5, establishes neither a requirement nor a permitted design.

### 12.8 Budget and usage contract

The budget and usage contract distinguishes ten value or record kinds, stated in the table below and made binding by WP2-MODEL-034.

| Value or record | What it distinguishes |
| --- | --- |
| Registered budget policy and scope identity/version | The registered policy, and the identified and versioned scope — per-request accounting, a per-planned-run cap, a per-study cap, an acknowledged worst-case exposure, or any further registered provider-project or account scope, as the study's registered design declares it under charter Section 14.3, which lists call and failure budgets among what a registration identifies — under which every authorization, charge, refusal, and settlement is decided. Which scopes a study registers, and every cap value, belong to that registered design; no scope set is fixed here, and no cap, quota, or budget value is selected, WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibiting this specification from selecting one. |
| Requested or estimated exposure | What a dispatch is expected or estimated to consume, before any authorization is decided. |
| Authorized or reserved exposure | What the authorization boundary in fact authorized or reserved, per applicable scope. |
| Known incurred usage | What a provider attempt is known to have consumed, as far as it is known. |
| Provider-reported usage | The provider's own report of usage, recorded as the provider's. |
| Locally derived usage | What the laboratory derived from its own records, recorded as a local derivation. |
| Billed or reconciled usage, where available | What billing or a later reconciliation established, recorded with its source. |
| Released unused authorization | Authorization returned unused through a recorded release event. |
| Unresolved or disputed exposure | Exposure whose acceptance, charge, or amount is unknown, unconfirmed, or contested. |
| Budget exhaustion or refusal | The typed refusal an exhausted or unavailable scope produces in place of a dispatch. |

**WP2-MODEL-034.** A conforming laboratory **MUST** keep each of the ten value or record kinds of the table in this section, wherever that kind exists for a scope, request, attempt, or unit, separately recorded and distinguishable from every other of them, no one of them standing in for, being reported as, or overwriting another, and any value of one kind that a laboratory derives from another recorded together with the kind or kinds it was derived from.

**WP2-MODEL-035.** Every provider dispatch — the making of a provider attempt, whether the first for its logical model request or a further one under a registered retry or fallback policy — **MUST** be authorized against every applicable registered budget scope, by a budget authorization or reservation recorded before that provider attempt is made.
