**WP2-MODEL-036.** The authorization or reservation of a provider dispatch **MUST** be decided at an authorization boundary that is atomic, or safe against competing authorization operations to an equivalent degree, spanning every registered budget scope applicable to that dispatch, every scope whose cap it could consume among them, so that no interleaving of concurrent authorization operations can oversubscribe the cap of any such scope or authorize the dispatch against some of those scopes but not others, at which the outcome of a race between competing authorization operations is never inferred from wall-clock arrival order, and at which every authorization operation that loses or that proceeds from an authorization state no longer current is preserved as a distinguishable rejection recorded by the authority that decided the authorization operation and naming the dispatch and the scopes it attempted to authorize, the authority that attempted it, and the reason it did not prevail, without deleting, overwriting, truncating, or rendering unverifiable any recorded authorization, incurred usage, settlement, or exposure record.

**WP2-MODEL-037.** Every provider attempt, each provider retry and each fallback attempt among them, **MUST** be accounted for separately in every applicable value kind of the table in this section, even where several provider attempts serve one logical model request.

**WP2-MODEL-038.** Where it is unknown whether a provider accepted or charged a dispatch, the exposure of that dispatch **MUST** be represented as unresolved or disputed exposure, rather than counted — silently or otherwise — as zero or as one settled call, charge, or usage value.

**WP2-MODEL-039.** Calls, input tokens, output tokens, total tokens, monetary cost, failures, retries, and fallbacks **MUST** remain separately attributable, before any aggregation, to the entities and scopes WP2-MODEL-019 requires the records carrying them to be attributable to.

**WP2-MODEL-040.** Provider-reported, locally derived, and billed or reconciled usage values **MUST** remain distinguishable wherever more than one of them exists for one provider attempt, scope, or interval, each carrying the provenance of its source.

**WP2-MODEL-041.** A conforming laboratory **MUST NOT** represent an unknown call count, token count, monetary cost, or other usage or exposure value as zero, or as any other definite value.

**WP2-MODEL-042.** A process restart, a restart of a laboratory worker's execution agent, a restart of any component that serves model requests for more than one isolation unit, or the loss or reset of a provider session **MUST NOT** reset, erase, duplicate, or re-derive from absence any budget authorization, incurred usage, settlement, or exposure record.

**WP2-MODEL-043.** Exhaustion of any applicable registered budget scope **MUST** produce a distinguishable, preserved, typed refusal — identifying the exhausted scope and the registered budget policy identity and version applied, and attributed to the study, planned run, and execution attempt concerned as WP2-ISO-016 requires — in place of the provider dispatch refused, no dispatch charged to that scope proceeding except one the registered budget policies applicable to it authorize.

**WP2-MODEL-044.** A cancellation issued at one of the scopes WP2-LIFE-027 states, and affecting a study, a planned run, an execution attempt, or a logical model request beneath it, **MUST** prevent further provider dispatches under the cancelled authority, while preserving the representation of any in-flight exposure and of every usage actually incurred.

**WP2-MODEL-045.** Unused budget authorization **MUST** be released only through a recorded release or reconciliation event, and never by the disappearance, eviction, or reset of process-local state.

**WP2-MODEL-046.** An in-flight or unresolved exposure **MUST** remain represented until a recorded settlement or reconciliation event reconciles or dispositions it.

**WP2-MODEL-047.** A fallback attempt **MUST** proceed only where the registered retry or fallback policy applicable to its request and the remaining authorized capacity of every applicable registered budget scope authorize it.

**WP2-MODEL-048.** Unused capacity of a registered budget scope **MUST** be shared with, transferred to, or reserved for another scope or unit only under a registered, versioned policy whose recorded transfer or reservation lineage names the source and destination scopes explicitly, without merging the evidence, the attribution, or the identities of any isolation unit or registered budget scope, and without changing any registered treatment silently.

**Rationale.** These requirements answer what the WP2-ISO-015 and WP2-ISO-016 rationales assign here — how budgets are reserved and refused, the budget algorithms, exhaustion behavior, and refusal typing — while selecting none of the mechanisms those words might suggest: no reservation algorithm, quota arbitration, pricing source, currency rule, or accounting store is chosen, and Section 12.10 records each as unselected. The authorization boundary WP2-MODEL-036 requires is the budget counterpart of the boundaries WP2-LIFE-034 requires for scalar transitions, WP2-SCHED-052 requires for grant operations, and WP2-SCHED-030 requires for authoritative acceptance: without it, two concurrent dispatches could each observe remaining capacity and both proceed, oversubscribing a cap that each respected individually, and a boundary spanning fewer than all applicable scopes could admit a dispatch that one scope authorized and another refused. Honest uncertainty is the second discipline: WP2-MODEL-038, WP2-MODEL-041, and WP2-MODEL-046 keep an unknown from becoming a zero, because a laboratory that counts unconfirmed dispatches as free spends past its acknowledged exposure exactly when transport is least reliable, and charter Section 12.10 lists cost and token accounting among the authoritative evidence of the operational diagnostics that support interpretation and reproducibility. WP2-MODEL-043 applies the typed, evidence-preserving refusal discipline of WP2-ISO-030 and WP2-ISO-031 to exhaustion, and its scope attribution follows WP2-ISO-016, whose rationale already permits a shared or hierarchical ceiling provided every charge retains its own attribution; WP2-MODEL-048 states the condition under which such sharing extends to unused capacity, with lineage in place of merging. Section 5.6 records the current arrangement — per-run and process-total caps enforced inside one process, reset at every attempt boundary, with no durable, shared, or atomic reservation across processes or studies — and Section 5.14 item 5 carries it forward as a limitation that establishes no requirement; WP2-SCOPE-013 separately prohibits trading request and outcome attribution or evidence completeness for execution throughput, WP2-ISO-033 keeps the isolation-level budget attribution WP2-ISO-016 requires from being relaxed for the same reason, and WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting any numeric cap or budget value.

### 12.9 Provider behavior and operational exposure

**WP2-MODEL-049.** A conforming laboratory **MUST** record a separately identified provider-behavior observation, as the provider-behavior observation entry of the register in Section 12.3 states it, for each of the following phenomena that in fact occurs and is observable on its request path, taken separately: provider queueing and latency; rate-limit responses; outages and transport failures; correlated provider or route failure windows; provider rejection; timeout; model, serving-provider, or route substitution; fallback exposure; retry exposure; late completion; and returned-model or observed-provider mismatch.

**WP2-MODEL-050.** Every provider-behavior observation **MUST** identify the affected logical model requests and provider attempts; the provider and route identities concerned and the nonsecret provider-project or account-scope identity of the path observed, carried in the nonsecret alias, digest, or registered identity WP2-SCHED-047 requires wherever a raw provider-project or account identifier would expose secret material; the applicable time domain, identified under WP2-TIME-001, and for a timeout the domain WP2-TIME-005 requires; the observing authority; and the identity and version of the registered policy under which the observation was interpreted — naming each of those that applies and stating the absence of any that does not, or that it was not observable.

**WP2-MODEL-051.** Provider behavior, provider queueing, or provider latency **MUST NOT** alter logical simulation time, simulated psychology, simulated causality, decision eligibility, or any registered treatment, otherwise than through the explicitly registered, engine-owned treatment rule WP2-TIME-004 permits, WP2-TIME-003 remaining the governing prohibition for every non-logical delay.

**WP2-MODEL-052.** A divergence between the registered provider, model, serving-provider, route, or provider-behavior expectations of a study and what execution observed **MUST NOT** amend, rewrite, or reinterpret the registration, the registered treatment, or the registered concurrency profile of that study after the fact, the divergence being recorded as evidence, attributed to the study, planned runs, execution attempts, and model requests affected, and retained for the disposition Section 14 assigns.

**Unselected option.** Detection algorithm and threshold. What tells a laboratory that a rate-limit window, an outage, a correlated failure window, a substitution, or a late completion occurred — the detection algorithm, the observation window, and every threshold — is deliberately unselected, and no numeric threshold or window value is stated anywhere in this section, WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibiting this specification from selecting one. WP2-MODEL-049 binds only the phenomena that in fact occur and are observable on a conforming laboratory's request path, and the registered policy WP2-MODEL-050 requires each observation to identify is where a registered design records its own detection choices.

**Rationale.** This subsection states the obligation once and the identification set once, and the register's provider-behavior observation entry carries both into the record model, so that the eleven phenomena share one record type rather than eleven separately identified ones. The phenomena are the operational exposures charter Section 14.5 requires to be observable where relevant — provider queueing, rate limits, correlated failure windows, fallback, timeout, and the rest — and charter Section 12.10 lists operational diagnostics of the same family — provider and local latency, failures, fallbacks, queue delay, and supersession or rejection among them — that support interpretation and reproducibility without defining believable behavior; the observed-provenance categories of Section 10.5 record the same exposures at the concurrency-profile level, and WP2-MODEL-050's identification set is what lets the two be joined explicitly rather than by timing. WP2-MODEL-051 keeps operational exposure out of the simulation by citation rather than restatement: WP2-TIME-003 and WP2-TIME-004 already state the prohibition and its one registered exception, and what this requirement adds is that no provider behavior reaches the simulated world, its psychology, its causality, its decision eligibility, or its treatment through any other route. What WP2-MODEL-051 prohibits is an effect on the registered treatment of a study, not the recording of identities that in fact differed: a fallback undertaken under a registered policy records every changed model, provider, serving-provider, route, prompt, context, and policy identity, and any changed provider-facing rendering or payload identity or digest, under WP2-MODEL-030, and WP2-MODEL-022 separately prohibits any such divergence from rewriting what the study registered. WP2-MODEL-052 is the request-level counterpart of WP2-SCHED-038, WP2-SCHED-042, and WP2-SCHED-043: a registration is a statement made before execution, and observed provider behavior that contradicts it is evidence of divergence, never grounds for editing the registered record to match what happened.

### 12.10 Options deliberately left unselected

**Unselected option.** Request-record placement and encoding. The records of the register in Section 12.3 may be carried on the entities they describe, in separate request-accounting records, in an event stream, in an index, or in more than one of those, under any encoding. WP2-MODEL-019 and WP2-MODEL-023 state the attribution and join properties; no schema, field structure, serialization, wire format, or store is selected, no payload format, hashing scheme, prompt representation, or wire encoding is selected for the immutable semantic content identity or the provider-facing rendering or payload identity or digest WP2-MODEL-053 keeps distinct, and Section 13 rather than this section decides how evidence is written.

**Unselected option.** Idempotency realization. The idempotency resolution this section requires may be realized by a durable record store, a keyed index, an authority-issued reservation, or another arrangement, under any key scheme and any replay-answer mechanism satisfying WP2-ISO-015, WP2-MODEL-024 through WP2-MODEL-026, and WP2-MODEL-055. No store, key scheme, or mechanism is selected; no cache, wait strategy, coalescing or attachment mechanism, deferral arrangement, or duplicate-refusal rule is selected; and no disposition of WP2-MODEL-055 is preferred over another by this specification.

**Unselected option.** Retry and fallback realization. How many retries, in what order, against which fallback identities, and under what policy representation are the registered design's decisions, carried by the registered retry or fallback policy identity and version this section requires to be named; this specification selects no retry count, fallback order, or policy encoding, and WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting a retry count.

**Unselected option.** Budget authorization realization. The atomic or equivalently safe authorization boundary WP2-MODEL-036 requires may be realized by a database transaction, a conditional or compare-and-set write, a single-writer arrangement, a consensus protocol, an authority-issued token, or another mechanism — the same range Section 9.12 records for the authoritative-commit boundary — and the reservation algorithm, any quota arbitration, the pricing source, every currency rule, and the accounting store remain unselected. WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits this specification from selecting a numeric cap, quota, or budget value, and none is selected here.

**Unselected option.** Usage measurement source. Known incurred usage may be measured from provider reports, from local derivation, from billing or reconciliation records, or from more than one of those, kept distinguishable as WP2-MODEL-040 requires. No source is preferred, and none is treated as authoritative by this specification.

**Unselected option.** Provider-behavior detection. The algorithms, observation windows, and thresholds by which the phenomena of Section 12.9 are detected are unselected, as the Unselected option of that subsection records, and no monitoring product, detector, or telemetry arrangement is selected here.

**Unselected option.** Dispatch path. The path by which a local dispatch attempt reaches a provider may be a direct call, a queued or brokered arrangement, a pooled connection, or another arrangement, echoing the queue and delivery option Section 9.12 records for scheduling; the events of Section 12.4 are recorded where each in fact occurs on whichever path exists. No transport, broker, or path is selected.

### 12.11 Boundaries assigned elsewhere

This section states which model-request, accounting, and provider-behavior records exist, what each of them names, how the three lifecycle layers stay distinct, how repeated and duplicated activity resolves, how budgets authorize and account for provider dispatch, and how provider behavior is recorded without reaching the simulation. It deliberately defines none of the following, each of which the staged instructions assign elsewhere: the evidence formats, inventory contents, replay treatment, and retention of the records this section requires, which belong to Section 13; the failure-class taxonomy, the typed disposition vocabulary behind the refusals, non-authoritative results, and unresolved exposures this section requires, and what counts as a material provider deviation, which belong to Section 14; the effect of refused, unresolved, or deviating requests and budgets on study completeness and reporting, which belongs to Section 15; secret custody, the provider-dispatch secret boundary, the secret-material half of the immutable request specification's content rule, and the refusal treatment of any provider response or other record of this section that is offered for ordinary persistence or for admission as evidence and in which live secret material is detected, which Section 16 states, the treatment of such material detected in a record already persisted or admitted being assigned onward as Section 16.10 states; the conformance checks, fault injections, and pass conditions for every requirement stated here, which belong to Section 17 and to the conformance document; and the implementation handoff and the ranking of the options this section leaves unselected, which belong to Section 18.

## 13. Evidence roots, replay, finalization, and sealing

### 13.1 What this section establishes

This section defines the implementation-neutral evidence contract of a conforming laboratory: the evidence-record model under which run and study evidence roots are populated, closed, and preserved; the authority layers that fix what each kind of record can establish; the completeness and applicability discipline for every evidence class; replay; finalization; inventories, run-level seals, sealed packages, and their verification; study-level sealing; evidence-size and retention policies; and the treatment of live secret material discovered in a record already persisted or admitted as evidence. It completes, as normative contract, the evidence terms Section 3.1 defines — *run evidence root*, *study evidence root*, *finalization*, *UI replay*, *reducer/ledger replay*, and *sealing* — and it leaves *aggregation* to Section 15. It is implementation-neutral: it names records, roles, boundaries, and properties, and it selects no archive format, storage system, serialization, digest or hash algorithm, canonicalization scheme, replay engine, UI path, retention duration, capacity value, compression, deletion mechanism, quarantine or redaction mechanism, transaction primitive, store, or numeric parameter, the selections WP2-SCOPE-015 enumerates remaining prohibited throughout this document.

It answers the questions the earlier sections expressly assign here. Sections 6.14 and 6.18 assign evidence formats, inventory contents, packaging, sealing procedure, and verification, which Sections 13.3 and 13.9 state. The Section 7.11 rationale assigns the question whether a diagnostic record is evidence at all and what evidentiary weight it carries, which Section 13.4 and the logs-and-diagnostics class of Section 13.6 answer. The Section 7.12 rationale assigns the evidence contract for finalization, which Section 13.8 states. Sections 7.14 and 7.21 assign inventory contents, sealing procedure, and verification, which Section 13.9 states. Section 8.16 assigns evidence formats, inventory contents, replay verification, the finalization evidence contract, packaging, and sealing, stated across Sections 13.3 and 13.6 through 13.9, and the Section 8.6 *Evidence validity determined* entry assigns the evidence rules against which an execution attempt's evidence is determined valid, which Sections 13.6 and 13.8 state, the determination vocabulary of that dimension remaining assigned to Section 14 as Section 13.14 records. Section 9.13 assigns evidence formats, inventory contents, replay verification, packaging, and sealing, stated across those same subsections, together with retention, which Section 13.11 states, and the WP2-SCHED-034 rationale assigns retention rules, evidence-size budgets, and what may be discarded after a defined retention period, which Sections 13.11 and 13.13 state. Section 10.13 assigns the evidence formats, inventory contents, packaging, sealing, and retention of concurrency-profile and observed-provenance records, which the classes of Section 13.6 together with Sections 13.9 and 13.11 carry. Section 11.13 assigns the evidence format, inventory, replay, and retention of timing records, which Sections 13.6, 13.7, 13.9, and 13.11 carry, and the WP2-TIME-019 rationale assigns how an impossible timing record is preserved within an evidence root, which the correction rule of the Section 13.3 evidence-item entry carries. Section 12.11 assigns the evidence formats, inventory contents, replay treatment, and retention of Section 12's records, which Sections 13.3, 13.6, 13.7, 13.9, and 13.11 carry. Section 16.10 assigns the evidence format, retention, and sealing treatment of incident records, audit records, and sanitized derivatives, which Sections 13.6, 13.9, and 13.11 carry, together with the evidentiary treatment of live secret material detected in a record already persisted or admitted as evidence, which Section 13.12 states — the incident typing, remediation, and disposition of that same discovery remaining assigned to Section 14, exactly as Section 16.10 states. Wherever one of those assignments names evidence formats, the formats themselves remain unselected, as Section 13.13 records.

Every substantive statement here is a Required future invariant carrying exactly one `WP2-EVID-###` identifier, a Design rationale introduced by a **Rationale.** lead-in, or an Unselected option introduced by an **Unselected option.** lead-in, in the Section 4.3 sense, or else a register entry of Section 13.3, its introducing statements included, whose normative force flows through WP2-EVID-001 rather than through an identifier of its own, an entry of the attribute table in Section 13.2, which states how the register is read and carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements that cite it, the definitional statement in Section 13.2 stating how the registered evidence contract is named in this section, which carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements that cite it, the coordination statement that opens Section 13.12, which records where the Section 16.10 assignment lands and carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements of that subsection, or a boundary statement in Section 13.14. The register in Section 13.3 is the one structured block of this section that carries normative clauses, and WP2-EVID-001 is its single incorporation requirement; no other table of this section carries a hidden or unbound obligation. Nothing in this section is a Current fact or a Current limitation; those belong to Section 5, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps a current mechanism from being read as a requirement. Every defined term used here carries the meaning Section 3.1 assigns it, as WP2-SCOPE-005 requires; every lifecycle term carries the meaning Section 8.3 assigns it, as WP2-LIFE-002 requires; and every secret-handling term carries the meaning Section 16.2 assigns it, as WP2-SEC-001 requires.

### 13.2 How the evidence-record register is read

Each entry of the register in Section 13.3 states the same seven attributes in the same order. The register supplies the normative evidence-record definitions of this section, which WP2-EVID-001 incorporates and makes binding clause by clause, except for the specific question an entry expressly assigns to a later section.

Throughout this section, the *registered evidence contract* of a study is the registered, versioned design object by which that study's registration fixes the evidence classes its units account for, the replay verification it requires, the inventory, sealing, and packaging boundaries it requires, and any further registered evidence class of WP2-EVID-018 — identified by identity and version wherever a requirement of this section names it, in the way the registered scheduling, cancellation, liveness, authoritative-commit, and budget policies of Sections 8, 9, and 12 are identified. Naming it names a registered design object and selects no schema, format, representation, or store, and charter Section 14.3 governs the registration that identifies it.

| Attribute | What the entry states |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | The domain within which the record's identity distinguishes one instance of the record type from every other instance of that record type. |
| Recording authority | The role answerable for creating the record or for recording the fact it states. |
| Required parent and source references | The identities the record has to name. |
| Cardinality | The counts that hold between records of this type and the records and entities they reference or are referenced by. |
| Point of existence | The point from which the record exists and can be referenced. |
| Correction or supersession rule | What may and may not be done with the record and its identity after it has been recorded. |
| May it exist for an attempt that produced no execution evidence | Whether an instance of the record type can exist for an execution attempt, or a study, whose produced execution evidence is empty. |

**WP2-EVID-001.** Every evidence record of a record type of the register in Section 13.3 that a conforming laboratory creates, records, references, or relies upon **MUST** conform to every clause of the evidence-record register in Section 13.3 — its introducing statements and, for the applicable entry, its identity scope, recording authority, required parent and source references, cardinality, point of existence, correction or supersession rule, and rule on existence for an attempt that produced no execution evidence — except only the specific question or clause that the entry expressly assigns to a later section, an exemption that reaches no other clause of that attribute or entry.

**Rationale.** An authority named in the register is a role — the party or process answerable for creating a record or recording a fact — and never a component, service, store, or product, exactly as Section 6.2 keeps every authority in the entity register a role, and WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting a mechanism throughout this document. WP2-EVID-001 is the single requirement that incorporates the register and makes every non-deferred clause binding, in the way WP2-ID-027 binds the entity register, WP2-LIFE-001 binds the lifecycle models, WP2-SCHED-051 binds the assignment-authority record model, WP2-MODEL-001 binds the model-request record register, and WP2-SEC-017 binds the credential-authority event model, so that no individual table cell carries a requirement identifier of its own, and a cell that expressly assigns a question to a later section states a boundary rather than an answer, consistent with Section 4.3. Three of the entries below concern identities the Section 6.7 register already registers — the run evidence root, the study evidence root, and the sealed package — and those entries reference the Section 6.7 entries and do not edit them, in exactly the device the Section 12.3 logical-model-request entry uses for the Section 6.6 identity: each adds no second identity, revises no clause of the Section 6.7 entry, which continues to bind through WP2-ID-027, and states only the evidence-contract clauses this section adds.

### 13.3 The evidence-record register

One register entry follows for each of the fourteen record types of the evidence contract — the finalization record with its completion record stated in one entry and the evidence-size and retention policy with its dispositions in another — in the order in which this section states them, from the two evidence roots, through the records an attempt's and a study's evidence accumulates — the ordinary evidence-intake-closure record standing immediately before the attempt evidence-closure record among them — to the inventory, seal, verification, integrity-basis, package, and retention records that enumerate, authenticate, and govern them, the attempt-integrity-basis record standing immediately after the seal-verification record among those. Each entry answers the seven attributes of Section 13.2 concretely, and a cell that expressly assigns a question to a later section defers exactly that question and nothing else.

**Run evidence root.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | The identity scope the Section 6.7 run-evidence-root entry states in full: every run evidence root in the laboratory, across all studies and all concurrent execution. This entry adds no second identity for the run evidence root and revises no clause of the Section 6.7 entry, which continues to bind through WP2-ID-027; what this entry adds is the evidence-contract clauses below. |
| Recording authority | The laboratory authority that creates or reserves the root for its execution attempt, as the Section 6.7 entry states. Every write into the root before its boundary is closed carries the attribution WP2-EVID-012 requires. |
| Required parent and source references | Exactly one execution-attempt identity, and through it the planned-run and study identities, as the Section 6.7 entry states; from the attempt's terminal closure onward, the attempt evidence-closure record of this register — a reference the root acquires through the closure record's admission as the last record of its boundary, co-accepted with the attempt's terminal value at the same authority boundary under WP2-EVID-014, never through a write into an already-closed boundary. |
| Cardinality | One run evidence root belongs to exactly one execution attempt, and one execution attempt has exactly one run evidence root, as the Section 6.7 entry states. One root holds zero or more evidence-item or evidence-source records, holds the ordinary evidence-intake-closure records of its attempt's finalization operations where any finalization began, carries exactly one current attempt evidence-closure record once its attempt is terminal, and is authenticated over time by zero or more run-level seals and sealed packages, each a distinct record of this register. |
| Point of existence | Created or reserved as part of its execution attempt's admission, as WP2-LIFE-009, WP2-EVID-007, and the Section 6.7 entry state, within the all-or-none admission boundary WP2-SCHED-013 states; the root's identity may therefore exist before any execution activity begins. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never adopted by, transferred to, or shared with another execution attempt, as the Section 6.7 entry states. A correction before closure is an additional identified record under WP2-EVID-013, never a mutation; Section 3.1 and the Section 6.7 entry record that the root is immutable once its attempt reaches a terminal state, the closure WP2-EVID-014 requires being co-accepted at that same terminal authority boundary so that a terminal root's boundary is already closed; after the boundary closes under WP2-EVID-014, nothing inside it is added, altered, or removed, later facts being recorded outside it under WP2-EVID-015. Creation, collision handling, resume, and recovery remain governed by WP2-ISO-026 through WP2-ISO-029, which WP2-EVID-011 preserves by citation rather than restatement, and the root's storage location remains an attribute rather than its identity, under WP2-ID-018 and WP2-EVID-010. |
| May it exist for an attempt that produced no execution evidence | Yes. The root of an admitted execution attempt cancelled or failed before execution activity began retains its identity with no execution-produced evidence, as WP2-EVID-008 requires, and its closure records an explicitly empty execution-produced evidence subset under WP2-EVID-017. A planned run for which no execution-attempt identity was ever admitted has no run evidence root, under WP2-EVID-009. |

**Study evidence root.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | The identity scope the Section 6.7 study-evidence-root entry states in full: every study evidence root in the laboratory, an identity-bearing logical namespace or registered set under WP2-ID-019. This entry adds no second identity and revises no clause of the Section 6.7 entry, which continues to bind through WP2-ID-027; what this entry adds is the evidence-contract clauses below. |
| Recording authority | The laboratory authority that creates or reserves the root for its study, as the Section 6.7 entry states. |
| Required parent and source references | Exactly one study identity and version, as the Section 6.7 entry states; the registered evidence contract identity and version under which its membership is fixed; and the explicit, identity-based membership WP2-EVID-053 requires — every run evidence root of the study's execution attempts and every study-level record the registered evidence contract requires. |
| Cardinality | One study evidence root belongs to exactly one study and contains or references the run evidence root of every execution attempt belonging to that study, together with zero or more sealed packages, as the Section 6.7 entry states; it is attested over time by zero or more study-level seals, each a distinct record of this register. |
| Point of existence | Created or reserved under the study's registration, as the Section 6.7 entry states; it may exist before any execution attempt of the study has run. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never adopted by or transferred to another study, as the Section 6.7 entry states. A membership or content error is corrected by a further identified record in the WP2-ID-025, WP2-ID-026, and WP2-LIFE-017 discipline, and a later study-level package or seal never rewrites an earlier one or the run evidence it references, under WP2-EVID-058. |
| May it exist for an attempt that produced no execution evidence | Yes, entirely. It exists for a study whose every execution attempt produced no execution evidence, and for a study with no admitted execution attempt at all, containing or referencing then exactly the study-level records that in fact exist. |

**Evidence-item or evidence-source record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every evidence item or evidence source the laboratory records within the boundary of a run evidence root or of a study evidence root, across all studies and all concurrent execution — one identified item or source per record, carrying an identity that a storage path or location alone does not supply, in the discipline WP2-ID-018 states for the roots that hold it. |
| Recording authority | The authority under whose recorded authority the write occurred, as WP2-EVID-012 requires — the owning laboratory worker for evidence produced by execution activity, the finalizing authority for finalization-produced records, and otherwise the authority the applicable contract of Sections 8 through 12 and 16 names for the record concerned. |
| Required parent and source references | The run evidence root or study evidence root whose boundary holds it, and through that root its execution-attempt, planned-run, and study lineage; the evidence class of WP2-EVID-018 it belongs to; and, for a derived item, its exact authoritative sources and derivation identity and version, as WP2-EVID-005 requires. |
| Cardinality | One boundary holds zero or more evidence-item or evidence-source records, and each such record belongs to exactly one boundary. An item referenced from more than one record keeps its one identity rather than acquiring one per reference. |
| Point of existence | At its recording into the boundary that holds it, before that boundary's closure; a fact arising after ordinary evidence intake closes for the execution attempt it concerns — the recorded closure the ordinary evidence-intake-closure record of this register states, as WP2-EVID-071 requires — and not among the records and derived artifacts that attempt's finalization itself produces, or arising after the closure of the run evidence root it concerns, is recorded outside that boundary, in the discipline WP2-EVID-015 and WP2-EVID-016 state, as a record of whatever open boundary holds it, with an explicit reference to the root it concerns. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never mutated in place and never removed from a closed boundary. A correction is an additional identified record under WP2-EVID-013, explicitly naming what it corrects; an impossible or contradictory record — an impossible timing record under WP2-TIME-017 among them — is preserved exactly as recorded and surfaced under the applicable integrity rules rather than normalized or discarded. |
| May it exist for an attempt that produced no execution evidence | Yes, for the record classes that do not presuppose execution activity — the attempt's admission, assignment, transition, and closure records among the facts its evidence accounts for — and no execution-produced item exists for an attempt that produced none, the empty execution-produced evidence subset being recorded explicitly under WP2-EVID-017 rather than represented by fabricated items. |

**Ordinary evidence-intake-closure record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every ordinary evidence-intake closure the laboratory records: the closure of ordinary evidence intake for one execution attempt — the distinct and earlier act WP2-EVID-030 states, closing the admission into that attempt's evidence boundary of any evidence other than the records and derived artifacts its finalization itself produces — across all studies, all planned runs, and all concurrent execution, each closure separately identified and its identity never reused. |
| Recording authority | The finalizing authority, ordinary evidence-intake closure being the finalization's earlier act under WP2-EVID-030. |
| Required parent and source references | The execution-attempt identity and its run-evidence-root identity; the registered evidence contract identity and version applied; the finalization-operation identity of the operation that recorded the closure — the explicit, never-reused identity WP2-EVID-072 requires every finalization undertaking to carry; the exact closed ordinary-input membership or snapshot, stated by identity; and the authority point at which the closure was accepted. |
| Cardinality | At most one ordinary evidence-intake-closure record is current per execution attempt, decided at the boundary WP2-EVID-071 states, every losing or superseded closure operation preserved as that requirement states; a finalization operation whose closure operation did not become current never holds a current successful finalization result, as WP2-EVID-072 states. |
| Point of existence | At its acceptance at the race-safe boundary WP2-EVID-071 states, before strict validation begins and before the terminal closure of the attempt-local evidence boundary WP2-EVID-014 requires, recorded into that attempt's evidence boundary and therefore among the members a full-root inventory of that boundary enumerates under WP2-EVID-040; a closure operation that did not become current exists from its own recording, preserved as WP2-EVID-071 requires, recorded into that same evidence boundary and enumerated among its members like the current one. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never rewritten in place; corrected only by a further identified record in the discipline WP2-EVID-013 and WP2-LIFE-017 state, a correction never reopening closed ordinary evidence intake. |
| May it exist for an attempt that produced no execution evidence | Yes. An attempt whose finalization begins with no ordinary inputs receives an intake closure over an explicitly empty membership or snapshot, stated explicitly rather than inferred from absence; no ordinary evidence-intake-closure record exists for an attempt whose finalization never began. |

**Attempt evidence-closure record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every attempt evidence-closure record the laboratory records: the terminal closure of one execution attempt's attempt-local evidence boundary, across all studies, all planned runs, and all concurrent execution, each closure separately identified and its identity never reused. |
| Recording authority | The closing authority the laboratory names for the attempt's evidence boundary — the finalizing authority, for an attempt that reaches its terminal operational value through Finalizing; the authority the applicable Section 8.6 entry names as permitted to record the terminal transition, for an attempt cancelled or failed before or during execution activity. |
| Required parent and source references | The execution-attempt identity and its run-evidence-root identity; the attempt's terminal operational value and finalization state — the values accepted at the same authority boundary at which the closure itself is admitted, as WP2-EVID-014 requires — referenced as the Section 8.6 records state them rather than restated; the registered evidence contract identity and version applied; exactly what evidence the attempt produced, named by identity — or by an inventory recorded at the closure itself over the execution-produced evidence subset, naming that narrower subset explicitly as its boundary under WP2-EVID-040 and never standing in for the full-root inventory, a full-root inventory of the whole closed boundary being recordable only on or after that closure, no inventory of a run evidence root preceding that root's closure under WP2-EVID-014; and, for each expected class of WP2-EVID-018, its state in the vocabulary WP2-EVID-019 states, the produced, absent, unavailable, inapplicable, and refused outcomes WP2-EVID-017 requires being the present, expected-but-missing, unavailable-or-unobservable, inapplicable, and refused states of that vocabulary at terminal closure, and a class whose resolution is still outstanding at that closure recorded as not yet resolved rather than as any of them — an empty execution-produced evidence subset stated explicitly as a recorded fact of this record. |
| Cardinality | Exactly one current attempt evidence-closure record per terminal execution attempt, every terminal attempt receiving one — an attempt whose finalization failed, an attempt whose finalization never began, and an attempt cancelled before execution activity among them — and none for a nonterminal attempt. |
| Point of existence | At the attempt's terminal evidence closure, recorded, as WP2-EVID-014 requires, at the same atomic or equivalently safe authority boundary at which the attempt's terminal operational value — and, where the attempt leaves Finalizing, the compound outcome WP2-LIFE-033 requires — is accepted, admitted as the last record of the boundary it closes, becoming current together with that terminal value or not at all, so that the Section 3.1 immutability of a terminal run evidence root holds over a boundary already closed. The closure record follows the completion record of a successful finalization and is not a record of that finalization for the purposes of WP2-EVID-034. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Corrected only by a correction record that explicitly names and supersedes it, in the WP2-LIFE-017 discipline; a correction never reopens the closed boundary, and a late or losing result arriving after closure is recorded through the separately identified post-closure record WP2-EVID-016 requires rather than through a revision of the closure. |
| May it exist for an attempt that produced no execution evidence | Yes — it exists for exactly such an attempt as for any other, recording the explicitly empty produced-evidence set WP2-EVID-017 requires — that attempt's empty execution-produced evidence subset, never its closed root boundary — never inferred from an absent location, an absent directory, or an omitted inventory. |

**Replay-verification record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every replay verification the laboratory records: one performance of one replay verification over one identified input set, across all studies and all concurrent execution — a reducer/ledger replay and a UI replay each recorded as its own separately identified verification with its own separately identified verdict, as WP2-SCOPE-007 requires wherever a UI replay surface exists, and the record naming which of the two Section 3.1 replay meanings it carries. |
| Recording authority | The replay-verifying authority the laboratory names; for a UI replay, the authority that records the operator-surface verdict, recorded as that surface's own verification rather than as the reducer/ledger one. |
| Required parent and source references | The execution attempt and run evidence root whose evidence it verifies; the exact registered initial state, the exact authoritative ledger or event set, and the exact engine, reducer, and version inputs it replayed, each by identity and digest, as WP2-EVID-021 and WP2-EVID-022 require; the replay implementation identity and version; the produced hashes or comparisons; the verdict; the verifying authority; and the time-domain facts of the record, each identifying its domain under WP2-TIME-001. |
| Cardinality | One execution attempt carries zero or more replay-verification records, and each record describes exactly one verification performance. Where the registered evidence contract requires replay, a successful finalization of the attempt names the applicable verification and its verdict, and a mismatch prevents one, under WP2-EVID-027. |
| Point of existence | When the verification is performed and its verdict recorded; an unavailable or inapplicable replay boundary exists as a record stating that unavailability or inapplicability under WP2-EVID-026, not as an inferred pass. |
| Correction or supersession rule | A verdict is never revised in place; a further verification is a further replay-verification record, and a material recording error is corrected under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026. |
| May it exist for an attempt that produced no execution evidence | Replay reconstructs recorded authoritative events, so no replay verification exists over an attempt that recorded none; for such an attempt the replay boundary is inapplicable and is recorded as inapplicable under WP2-EVID-026 rather than fabricated or inferred as a pass. |

**Finalization record and completion record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every finalization record the laboratory records — the record of one finalization undertaken for one execution attempt, each undertaking carrying the explicit, never-reused finalization-operation identity WP2-EVID-072 requires — and every completion record — the identified record whose writing, last, marks that attempt's successful finalization — across all studies and all concurrent execution. The completion record is a member record of its one finalization operation, not a second finalization and not a lifecycle transition. |
| Recording authority | The finalizing authority, as the Section 8.6 entries name it for the finalization dimension. |
| Required parent and source references | The identification set WP2-EVID-029 states — the exact execution attempt, the run-evidence-root identity, the registered evidence contract identity and version, the finalization policy or rules identity and version, the finalizing authority, and the assignment-authority history under which the evidence was produced and finalized; the finalization-operation identity of the undertaking, named by the finalization record, by every validation, by every candidate completion record, and by every derived artifact the operation produced, together with the operation's complete output membership, as WP2-EVID-072 requires; the ordinary evidence-intake-closure record whose closed ordinary-input membership or snapshot the operation validated, by identity — for the operation holding the current successful finalization result, the attempt's current intake-closure record under WP2-EVID-071; each evidence source validated and its verdict, as the Section 8.6 Finalization succeeded entry carries in provenance; the replay-verification records relied upon, where the registered evidence contract requires replay; and every derived artifact produced, with the lineage WP2-EVID-005 requires. |
| Cardinality | At most one current successful finalization result per execution attempt, under WP2-LIFE-033 and WP2-LIFE-034, every losing or stale finalization or completion operation preserved under WP2-LIFE-035, every interrupted or otherwise unfinished finalization or completion operation preserved with a recorded, distinguishable disposition in the vocabulary Section 14 states, and a failed finalization concluding through the accepted compound outcome WP2-LIFE-033 states; exactly one completion record per finalization operation that reaches its completion write, each operation identified as WP2-EVID-072 requires, written last under WP2-EVID-034; at most one current completion record per execution attempt, none current where the compound outcome was not accepted, the written record of a losing operation preserved under WP2-LIFE-035 and the written record of an interrupted or otherwise unfinished operation preserved with a recorded, distinguishable disposition in the vocabulary Section 14 states. |
| Point of existence | A finalization record exists from the recording of the finalization it describes; the completion record exists from its writing, which follows every other record and derived artifact of its own finalization operation, as WP2-EVID-034 states, and, where that operation's compound outcome is accepted, precedes the compound Finalization succeeded and Completed result becoming current, as WP2-EVID-037 requires; the record of an operation whose compound outcome was not accepted exists from its writing and is preserved as WP2-EVID-037 states for a rejected or losing operation and, for an interrupted or otherwise unfinished one, with the recorded, distinguishable disposition WP2-EVID-036 requires. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never rewritten in place, and never rewritten so as to convert a failed finalization into a successful one or a successful one into a failed one; a material recording error is corrected under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026, the correction record also satisfying WP2-LIFE-017 where it corrects a recorded transition's provenance. |
| May it exist for an attempt that produced no execution evidence | Yes, in the narrow sense that a finalization undertaken for an attempt whose produced-evidence set is empty is recorded like any other, strictly validating exactly the applicable sources that exist; no finalization record exists for an attempt whose finalization never began — one holding Finalization not started permanently under the Section 8.6 model — and no completion record of a finalization that did not succeed ever stands as one. |

**Inventory.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every inventory the laboratory records: one enumeration of the members of one identified evidence boundary at one recorded state, run-evidence-root inventories and study-level inventories among them, each inventory separately identified and its identity never reused. |
| Recording authority | The inventorying authority the laboratory names — the sealing authority, where the inventory is produced as part of a sealing operation, and otherwise the authority the registered evidence contract names. |
| Required parent and source references | The exact evidence boundary it enumerates, by identity — for a run-evidence-root inventory, the boundary named as the whole closed run-evidence-root boundary or as an explicitly named execution-produced evidence subset, as WP2-EVID-040 requires; its own inventory identity; the versioned inventory rules applied; and every member of the boundary by stable identity and content digest, with the omission-, duplication-, substitution-, and mutation-detecting metadata WP2-EVID-040 requires. |
| Cardinality | One inventory enumerates exactly one identified boundary. One boundary receives zero or more inventories over time, each a distinct record; each seal and each sealed package names the exact inventory it authenticates. |
| Point of existence | When the enumeration is recorded; for a run evidence root, on or after that root's closure under WP2-EVID-014, so that what it enumerates is the closed boundary rather than a moving one. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never edited in place. A further inventory over the same boundary is a further identified record carrying explicit correction or supersession lineage to the earlier one, which remains preserved, so that at most one inventory of one boundary stands as the current one — the current full-root inventory WP2-FAIL-026 requires of a cancellation-terminal attempt among them — and a disagreement between an inventory and the boundary it enumerates is a typed integrity failure under WP2-EVID-049, never an occasion to rewrite either. |
| May it exist for an attempt that produced no execution evidence | Yes. A full-root inventory — one whose boundary is the whole closed run-evidence-root boundary — enumerates every member of that boundary, the attempt evidence-closure record and every non-execution record among them, and therefore never enumerates zero members, the empty execution-produced evidence subset of such an attempt remaining the explicit fact its attempt evidence-closure record states; a zero-member enumeration over such a root exists only in a separately identified sub-inventory that explicitly names the execution-produced evidence subset as its narrower boundary and is never substituted for the full-root inventory or for the run-level seal that names it, as WP2-EVID-040 and WP2-EVID-043 require, a study-level inventory over a registered membership that holds nothing enumerating zero members as the study-level-seal entry of this register states. |

**Run-level seal.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every run-level seal the laboratory records: one authenticated content-digest record over one closed run-evidence-root boundary and the exact inventory it names, in the Section 3.1 sealing sense, across all studies and all concurrent execution. |
| Recording authority | The laboratory sealing authority, the role the Section 6.7 sealed-package entry names for a sealing operation. |
| Required parent and source references | The run-evidence-root boundary it authenticates; the exact full-root inventory it authenticates, as WP2-EVID-043 requires; the sealing rules and their version; its digest facts; the sealing authority; and the time-domain facts of the sealing operation, each identifying its domain under WP2-TIME-001. |
| Cardinality | One run-level seal authenticates exactly one run-evidence-root boundary and one inventory, as WP2-EVID-043 requires. One closed boundary is authenticated by zero or more run-level seals over time, a later seal a distinct record with explicit correction or supersession lineage to the earlier one, which remains preserved. |
| Point of existence | When the sealing operation commits, after the boundary's closure under WP2-EVID-014. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never recomputed, replaced, or rewritten under its same identity, as WP2-EVID-049 requires; supersession is a new seal with explicit lineage, and the sealed boundary itself is never reopened to make a seal verify. |
| May it exist for an attempt that produced no execution evidence | Yes. A run-level seal over the root of an attempt whose execution-produced evidence subset is empty authenticates the full closed run-evidence-root boundary and its full-root inventory, the attempt evidence-closure record among its members, the empty subset remaining the explicit fact that closure record states, as WP2-EVID-043 requires; it never claims a zero-member boundary where the closure record itself is a member. |

**Study-level seal.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every study-level seal the laboratory records: one seal attesting completeness and integrity over all evidence roots and all study-level evidence registered to one study, in the WP2-SCOPE-008 sense, across all studies and all concurrent execution. |
| Recording authority | The laboratory sealing authority. |
| Required parent and source references | The study evidence root and the study identity and version; the registered evidence contract identity and version fixing the registered membership it attests; the exact study-membership-and-completion ledger identity and its version or correction lineage as read for the seal and the study's current registered-membership-closure result as it stood when the seal was produced — for a complete study-level seal a current reached result whose completeness it attests, as WP2-EVID-055 requires, that result reached as WP2-AGG-016 requires; the exact study-level inventory it authenticates, enumerating every run evidence root and study-level record of that membership by identity and digest; the sealing rules and their version; and the sealing authority. It attests nothing outside the boundary it identifies, as WP2-ISO-022 requires of every seal. |
| Cardinality | One study-level seal seals exactly one study-level boundary. One study receives zero or more study-level seals over time, each distinct from every run-level seal and sealed package under WP2-EVID-056 and WP2-EVID-057. A study-level seal produced before the study's registered-membership closure is reached is labeled a partial or snapshot boundary and never stands as a complete study-level seal, as WP2-EVID-055 requires. Such a labeled partial or snapshot boundary identifies the study-membership-and-completion ledger snapshot and the not-yet-reached registered-membership-closure result WP2-EVID-055 requires it to name, together with each respect in which it is not a complete study-level seal. |
| Point of existence | When the study-level sealing operation commits. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never rewritten, and never rewriting an earlier study-level seal or package or the run evidence it references, under WP2-EVID-058; supersession is a new study-level seal with explicit lineage, and later lawful membership under a new registered boundary produces new inventory, seal, and lineage records without rewriting the earlier snapshot, as WP2-EVID-055 requires. |
| May it exist for an attempt that produced no execution evidence | Yes. A study-level seal attests the registered set whether it contains one root, several roots, or no execution-produced evidence, as WP2-EVID-055 requires, so a study whose attempts produced nothing, or that admitted no attempt, is sealable over exactly what its registered membership holds. |

**Seal-verification record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every seal verification the laboratory records: one read-only verification of one seal or sealed package against the inventory and boundary it names, across all studies and all concurrent execution. |
| Recording authority | The verifying authority the laboratory names, which need not be the sealing authority; both are roles, and naming them selects no component. |
| Required parent and source references | The exact package or seal verified; the exact inventory and evidence boundary it was verified against; the verification rules and their version; the verdict; the verifying authority; and the time-domain facts of the verification, each identifying its domain under WP2-TIME-001. |
| Cardinality | One record per verification performance; one seal or package is verified zero or more times, each performance a distinct record. Which verification is current for an execution attempt is fixed by that attempt's current attempt-integrity basis, which names the exact seal-verification record and verdict relied upon among the references the attempt-integrity-basis entry of this register requires of it, WP2-EVID-073 fixing which basis is current — never by arrival order or by a favorable outcome — and the current, successful verification the current basis names is among the eligibility facts WP2-EVID-051 requires before authoritative planned-run acceptance. |
| Point of existence | When the verification is performed and its verdict recorded. |
| Correction or supersession rule | A verdict is preserved, never revised in place; a further verification is a further record, and a failed verification is a typed integrity failure under WP2-EVID-049, never repaired by silently replacing what it verified. |
| May it exist for an attempt that produced no execution evidence | Yes. Verification of the run-level seal over the root of an attempt whose execution-produced evidence subset is empty is a verification like any other, and its verdict states what the full-root inventory that seal names enumerates, the empty subset remaining the explicit fact the attempt evidence-closure record states, as WP2-EVID-043 requires. |

**Attempt-integrity-basis record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every attempt-integrity basis the laboratory records: one identified selection, for one execution attempt, of the exact integrity evidence relied upon for that attempt, across all studies and all concurrent execution, each basis separately identified and its identity never reused; a basis is successful only where it names every member of the chain its required parent and source references state — the closed run-evidence-root boundary, the full-root inventory, the run-level seal, and the seal-verification record — and the seal-verification record it names preserved a successful verdict over that seal, inventory, and boundary, a basis lacking any member of the chain, or naming no seal-verification record, never being successful, and an empty set of named verdicts never standing as success, which is the sense in which WP2-EVID-051 and WP2-AGG-024 read a basis as current and successful, and in which WP2-AGG-018 reads the current basis whose typed outcome it requires. |
| Recording authority | The verifying or sealing authority the registered evidence contract names for integrity-basis recording — a role; naming it selects no component. |
| Required parent and source references | Exactly one closed run-evidence-root boundary; exactly one full-root inventory enumerating that whole closed boundary; exactly one run-level seal authenticating that inventory and boundary; exactly one seal-verification record evaluating that seal against that inventory and boundary under identified verification rules and version, together with the verdict it preserved; and the applicable authority point — each named by identity, none of them absent. WP2-EVID-073 states which basis is current for an execution attempt, how competing basis operations are decided, and which basis an acceptance names. |
| Cardinality | At most one attempt-integrity basis is current per execution attempt, competing basis operations decided race-safely with every losing operation preserved, as WP2-EVID-073 requires; one execution attempt carries zero or more historical bases through the explicit correction, supersession, and currentness lineage that requirement states. |
| Point of existence | When its selection is accepted at the boundary WP2-EVID-073 states, on or after the seal verification it names; arising after the closure of the run-evidence-root boundary it names, it lives outside that boundary with an explicit reference inward, in the discipline WP2-EVID-015 states. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Explicit correction, supersession, and currentness lineage under WP2-EVID-073, never rewritten in place; an integrity failure detected after an authoritative acceptance receives the preserved post-acceptance integrity-incident treatment WP2-EVID-049 states rather than an edit to any basis or to the accepted outcome. |
| May it exist for an attempt that produced no execution evidence | Yes. A basis for an attempt whose execution-produced evidence subset is empty satisfies the same chain as any other basis, through its nonempty full closed root — the attempt evidence-closure record and every other non-execution record among its members — its full-root inventory enumerating that whole closed boundary, its run-level seal authenticating that inventory and boundary, and a seal verification over that seal, inventory, and boundary together with the verdict it preserved, exactly as for any other attempt, its success turning on that verdict exactly as this entry's identity scope states for any other basis. |

**Sealed package.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | The identity scope the Section 6.7 sealed-package entry states in full: every sealed package the laboratory produces. This entry adds no second identity for the sealed package and revises no clause of the Section 6.7 entry, which continues to bind through WP2-ID-027; what this entry adds is the evidence-contract clauses below. |
| Recording authority | The laboratory authority that performs the sealing operation, as the Section 6.7 entry states. |
| Required parent and source references | The identity of the evidence boundary it seals, the inventory it authenticates, and the versioned packaging and sealing rules it applied, as the Section 6.7 entry states; and, for a later package over an already-packaged boundary, explicit correction or supersession lineage to the earlier package, as WP2-EVID-047 requires. |
| Cardinality | One sealed package seals exactly one identified evidence boundary, as the Section 6.7 entry and WP2-EVID-046 state; one boundary is sealed by zero or more sealed packages over time, every earlier package preserved, as charter Section 14.9 requires of an original package when a packaging defect is corrected in a later version. A study-level package produced before its study's registered-membership closure is reached is labeled a partial or snapshot boundary and never stands as a complete study-level boundary, identifying the study-membership-and-completion ledger snapshot it read, the not-yet-reached registered-membership-closure result standing when it was produced, and each respect in which it is not complete, the label itself being what WP2-EVID-055 requires of it. |
| Point of existence | When the sealing operation commits, as the Section 6.7 entry states. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never reused and never rewritten, as the Section 6.7 entry states; a later package over the same boundary is a distinct sealed package identifying its own boundary and inventory, and packaging never mutates a run evidence root, under WP2-EVID-050. |
| May it exist for an attempt that produced no execution evidence | Yes. The Section 6.7 entry records that a boundary whose inventory holds no evidence produced by an execution attempt can still be sealed; such a package authenticates the full-root inventory of its closed boundary, the explicitly empty execution-produced evidence subset remaining the recorded fact of the attempt evidence-closure record under WP2-EVID-017, never a zero-member boundary claim, as WP2-EVID-043 requires. |

**Evidence-size and retention-policy record or disposition.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every evidence-size and retention policy the laboratory applies — a registered, versioned policy record — and every retention disposition recorded under one: one policy-authorized retention action or determination over identified evidence, each policy and each disposition separately identified. |
| Recording authority | For the policy, the registration or governance authority of the study's registered design; for a disposition, the authority the policy names for the action or determination concerned. |
| Required parent and source references | For the policy, the evidence classes it governs and the studies, evidence roots, packages, and records to which it applies, as WP2-EVID-059 requires; for a disposition, the policy identity and version that authorized it, the exact evidence concerned by identity, the action or determination recorded, the authority, the reason, and the time-domain facts of the record under WP2-TIME-001. |
| Cardinality | One study identifies every evidence-size and retention policy applicable to it; one disposition records exactly one retention action or determination; and no retention action affecting evidence under an active sealed package, a registered claim dependency, an audit, or a required review proceeds unrecorded, under WP2-EVID-063. |
| Point of existence | The policy from its establishment under the study's registered design; a disposition from its recording, no retention action it authorizes preceding the record that authorizes it. |
| Correction or supersession rule | A policy change is a new policy version rather than an edit, in the charter Section 14.2 discipline; a disposition is corrected by a further identified record naming what it supersedes, and no disposition makes an incomplete or unavailable evidence set appear complete or replayable, under WP2-EVID-062. |
| May it exist for an attempt that produced no execution evidence | Yes. The policy exists independently of any execution, and a disposition over an explicitly empty or pre-start-terminal root is recorded like any other, against exactly the records that exist. |

### 13.4 Evidence authority layers

**WP2-EVID-002.** Canonical engine events and canonical engine state **MUST** be treated as authoritative for the simulated world truth and the committed consequences of their own independent simulated world, consistent with charter Section 14.8's rule that the deterministic engine is final authority over objective world state, legal affordances, and committed consequences.

**WP2-EVID-003.** A provider response **MUST** be treated as authoritative evidence of what was received from the provider attempt that produced it, and of nothing more — never as evidence of simulated world truth or of a committed consequence.

**WP2-EVID-004.** Request, provider, timing, budget, concurrency, diagnostic, and authority records **MUST** establish only the recorded facts of their own record types, no one of them silently becoming, supplying, or standing in for a canonical world event.

**WP2-EVID-005.** Every derived artifact **MUST** identify its exact authoritative sources and the derivation identity and version that produced it.

**WP2-EVID-006.** A model prose passage, a log line, a report, an aggregate, or a later narrative **MUST NOT** retroactively establish that an unrecorded simulated event occurred.

**Rationale.** These five requirements fix the evidentiary weight of each layer of records so that no later reader has to guess which record was entitled to say what. They answer the question the Section 7.11 rationale assigns here: a log or diagnostic record is evidence — of its own recorded facts, attributable under WP2-ISO-017 and accounted for as the logs-and-diagnostics class of WP2-EVID-018 — and it is never canonical world truth, which charter Section 14.8 reserves to the deterministic engine, charter Section 12.10 separately confining diagnostics to interpretation and reproducibility rather than to defining behavior. The provider-response rule restates no clause of Section 12: WP2-MODEL-013 already keeps simulation acceptance engine-owned, and what WP2-EVID-003 adds is the evidentiary consequence — a response proves receipt from one provider attempt, and nothing about the simulated world. WP2-EVID-006 is the retroactivity half of the same authority rule, against every narrative surface at once: charter Section 14.8 denies model prose the standing to prove that an event occurred, and an aggregate or report that could establish an unrecorded event would let a derived artifact outrank its own sources, which WP2-EVID-005 makes visible by requiring every derived artifact to name exactly what it derived from.

### 13.5 Run-evidence-root lifecycle, boundary, and closure

**WP2-EVID-007.** The run-evidence-root identity of every execution attempt **MUST** be created or reserved as part of that attempt's admission, consistent with WP2-LIFE-009, the Section 6.7 run-evidence-root entry, and the all-or-none admission boundary WP2-SCHED-013 states, before and independently of any execution activity carried out under the attempt.

**WP2-EVID-008.** An admitted execution attempt cancelled or failed before execution activity began under it **MUST** retain its run-evidence-root identity, even where that root contains no execution-produced evidence.

**WP2-EVID-009.** A planned run for which no execution-attempt identity was ever admitted **MUST NOT** have, acquire, or be assigned a run evidence root.

**WP2-EVID-010.** The identity of a run evidence root and the storage location or locations holding its contents **MUST** remain two separately recorded facts, every boundary, closure, sealing, and verification rule of this section attaching to the root's identity rather than to any location, consistent with WP2-ID-018.

**WP2-EVID-011.** Every creation or reservation of a run evidence root, every handling of a collision over one, and every resume or recovery operation acting on one **MUST** satisfy WP2-ISO-026 through WP2-ISO-029 exactly as Section 7.17 states them, no record or operation of this section restating, narrowing, or widening what those requirements make a collision or permit to be acted on.

**WP2-EVID-012.** Every write into a run evidence root before that root's boundary is closed **MUST** be attributable, from the record of the write itself, to the authority under which it occurred.

**WP2-EVID-013.** A correction to evidence already recorded in a run evidence root **MUST** be recorded as an additional identified record that explicitly names what it corrects — in the discipline WP2-LIFE-017 states for lifecycle records — rather than as a mutation, replacement, or removal of the corrected record.

**WP2-EVID-014.** The attempt-local evidence boundary of an execution attempt **MUST** be closed through the attempt evidence-closure record of the register in Section 13.3, recorded at the same atomic or equivalently safe authority boundary at which the attempt's terminal operational value — and, where the attempt leaves Finalizing, the compound outcome WP2-LIFE-033 requires — is accepted, admitted as the last record of the boundary it closes, becoming current together with that terminal value or not at all, so that the Section 3.1 immutability of a terminal run evidence root holds over a boundary already closed and no interval exists in which that root is both terminal and open to a further write.

**WP2-EVID-015.** A late response, an acceptance operation, a replacement decision, an exclusion, a seal verification, or a study-level record arising after an execution attempt's terminal evidence closure **MUST NOT** mutate that attempt's run evidence root, every record of such a later fact living outside the closed boundary and referencing it explicitly.

**WP2-EVID-016.** A late or losing result arriving after ordinary evidence intake closes for the execution attempt it concerns, or after the closure of the run evidence root it concerns, **MUST** remain preserved through a separately identified post-closure record, and through any later package or study-level boundary that includes that record, without the run evidence root being reopened or rewritten.

**WP2-EVID-017.** Every terminal execution attempt — an attempt whose finalization failed, an attempt whose finalization never began, and an attempt cancelled before execution activity among them — **MUST** receive an explicit evidence-closure result identifying exactly what evidence the attempt produced and which expected classes were absent, unavailable, inapplicable, or refused, an empty produced-evidence set being represented explicitly rather than inferred from an absent location, an absent directory, or an omitted inventory.

**Rationale.** These requirements give the run evidence root its lifecycle in evidence terms without changing what Sections 6, 8, and 9 already fixed: WP2-EVID-007 through WP2-EVID-009 carry the admission-time creation, pre-start retention, and no-root-without-an-attempt consequences that WP2-LIFE-009, WP2-LIFE-011, WP2-ID-007, and the Section 6.7 entry state, restating none of them in a way that changes meaning, and WP2-EVID-011 keeps the collision, adoption, resume, and recovery rules exactly where Section 7.17 put them. The boundary discipline is the load this section adds: closure is an explicit recorded act, accepted at the same atomic or equivalently safe authority boundary at which the attempt's terminal value is accepted, so that the Section 3.1 immutability of a terminal root attaches to a boundary already closed, and everything later — an acceptance under Section 9, a replacement decision under Section 14, an exclusion, a seal verification, a study-level record — lives outside the closed boundary with an explicit reference inward, which is what lets WP2-SCHED-029's preserved losing results, WP2-MODEL-014's preserved late responses, and WP2-MODEL-032's non-authoritative dispositions all exist without any of them reopening a sealed root. The closure result is stated against every terminal attempt because charter Section 14.4 requires failed, interrupted, invalid, excluded, and replacement attempts to remain recorded evidence with typed dispositions, and an attempt that never started is the case a concurrent laboratory loses first; Section 5.9 records that the repository's current per-execution seal already authenticates an explicitly empty set for an interruption that preceded any evidence creation, which is evidence that the representation is achievable, not a requirement, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps it from being read as one.

### 13.6 Evidence completeness and applicability

**WP2-EVID-018.** Every execution attempt's evidence **MUST** account explicitly, under the registered evidence contract applicable to it, for every applicable class among the following: the specification, study, registration, experiment, condition, treatment, world or scenario, and analysis identities and versions; the planned-run, execution-attempt, replacement, laboratory-worker, assignment-authority, dispatch-wave, and concurrency lineage; the registered concurrency profile and the observed concurrency provenance; the canonical initial state, the canonical events, the final canonical state, and the logical-time records; the model requests, immutable request specifications, provider-facing renderings, provider attempts, responses, validations, simulation resolutions, idempotency dispositions, retries, fallbacks, budget authorizations, incurred usage, and provider-behavior observations of Section 12; the local and provider timing records of Section 11; the logs and diagnostics of WP2-ISO-017; the cancellation, failure, refusal, rejection, correction, and non-authoritative-result records of the applicable sections; the secret-boundary audit records, detected-secret incident records, and sanitized-derivative lineage of Section 16; the replay results; the finalization results; the inventories, seals, packages, and verification results; and any other registered evidence class the study's registered evidence contract requires.
