**WP2-EVID-019.** For every class of WP2-EVID-018, the record accounting for that class in an execution attempt's evidence **MUST** distinguish present, inapplicable, unavailable or unobservable, refused, expected but missing, and not yet resolved, wherever those states apply to that class.

**WP2-EVID-020.** The absence of a record for an evidence class **MUST NOT** be interpreted, recorded, or reported as that class's inapplicability or as a success.

**Rationale.** The class list enumerates, at evidence level, exactly the records the approved contracts of Sections 6 through 12 and 16 already require to exist, so it creates no new record type and selects no format; what it adds is that an attempt's evidence answers for each class rather than leaving silence ambiguous. The six states follow the discipline WP2-TIME-010, WP2-SCHED-035, and WP2-MODEL-019 already apply in their own sections — an inapplicable category is stated, an unobservable boundary is stated with its reason, and an absence is a different fact from either — because only a stated fact is checkable, and a missing record that reads as success is the failure mode charter Section 14.9 requires to be treated as explicit rather than concealed. The produced, absent, unavailable, inapplicable, and refused outcomes WP2-EVID-017 names and the six states WP2-EVID-019 distinguishes are one vocabulary under two statements — produced is present, absent is expected but missing, and unavailable is unavailable or unobservable — and a class whose resolution is still outstanding at terminal closure, an unresolved budget exposure represented under Section 12.8 among them, is recorded as not yet resolved and dispositioned under Section 14. This subsection carries the evidence form of the timing records Section 11.13 assigns here, of the concurrency records Section 10.13 assigns here, of the Section 12 records its Section 12.11 boundary assigns here, and of the incident, audit, and sanitized-derivative records Section 16.10 assigns here, in each case as classes of this contract rather than as revisions of those sections' record models.

### 13.7 Replay

**WP2-EVID-021.** Every reducer/ledger replay **MUST** reconstruct canonical state from the exact registered initial state, the exact authoritative ledger or event set, and the exact engine, reducer, and version inputs that its replay-verification record names, and from nothing else.

**WP2-EVID-022.** Every replay-verification record **MUST** record the input identities and digests it replayed, the replay implementation identity and version, the produced hashes or comparisons, the verdict, the verifying authority, and the time-domain facts of the verification, each identifying its domain under WP2-TIME-001.

**WP2-EVID-023.** A replay of the authoritative simulated consequences already recorded for an execution attempt **MUST NOT** require a provider call, regenerated model prose, or a renewed cognitive decision.

**WP2-EVID-024.** Where a UI replay surface exists, the UI replay verdict **MUST** be recorded separately from the reducer/ledger replay verdict, as WP2-SCOPE-007 requires, and never substituted for reducer/ledger replay.

**WP2-EVID-025.** The absence of a UI replay surface **MUST NOT** be recorded, reported, or treated as a nonconformity of an otherwise conforming laboratory or as a missing evidence class of its execution attempts.

**WP2-EVID-026.** An unavailable or inapplicable replay boundary **MUST** be recorded as unavailable or inapplicable, in the discipline WP2-TIME-010 states for an unobservable timing boundary, rather than inferred, recorded, or reported as a passed verification.

**WP2-EVID-027.** A replay mismatch, an impossible reconstruction, a missing replay input, or a contradictory replay result **MUST** remain preserved as a typed failure, in the vocabulary Section 14 states, and prevent a successful finalization of the execution attempt concerned wherever the registered evidence contract requires that replay.

**WP2-EVID-028.** Replay and replay verification **MUST** be read-only with respect to the evidence and the simulated state they inspect.

**Rationale.** The two replay meanings are the Section 3.1 rows, preserved exactly: reducer/ledger replay is the independent reconstruction of final canonical state by folding the reducer over the recorded authoritative event ledger, compared by hash, and UI replay is the operator-surface verification whose verdict is read from the user interface, each recorded as its own verification with its own verdict wherever both exist, under WP2-SCOPE-007. Requiring no provider call and no regenerated prose is the evidentiary consequence of charter Section 14.8 and WP2-EVID-002: the authoritative consequences are already in the ledger, so reconstructing them needs the recorded inputs and the reducer, and a replay that needed a provider would be measuring the provider rather than verifying the record. WP2-EVID-025 keeps WP2-SCOPE-007's conditional construction intact — a laboratory with no UI replay surface satisfies that requirement vacuously and loses nothing here. The honest-boundary and typed-failure rules carry the discipline of WP2-TIME-010 and charter Section 14.9 to replay: an unavailable verification recorded as a pass is a concealed evidence gap, and a mismatch is exactly the signal strict finalization exists to stop, so it blocks success where the registered evidence contract requires the replay rather than being dispositioned quietly. No replay engine, UI path, hash algorithm, serialization, or canonicalization mechanism is selected, and Section 13.13 records each as unselected.

### 13.8 Finalization

**WP2-EVID-029.** Every finalization **MUST** identify the exact execution attempt, the run-evidence-root identity, the registered evidence contract identity and version applicable to the attempt, the finalization policy or rules identity and version applied, the finalizing authority, and the assignment-authority history under which the evidence it finalizes was produced and under which it finalizes.

**WP2-EVID-030.** A finalization **MUST** close ordinary evidence intake for its execution attempt — the admission into that attempt's evidence boundary of any evidence other than the records and derived artifacts the finalization itself produces — before strict validation begins, that closure being a distinct and earlier act than the terminal closure of the attempt-local evidence boundary WP2-EVID-014 requires, recorded through the ordinary evidence-intake-closure record of the register in Section 13.3 as WP2-EVID-071 states, and a fact concerning the attempt that arises after that intake closure and is not one of those records or derived artifacts being preserved outside that evidence boundary with an explicit reference to it, in the discipline WP2-EVID-015 and WP2-EVID-016 state for a fact arising after the terminal closure.

**WP2-EVID-071.** Every ordinary evidence-intake closure **MUST** be recorded through an identified ordinary evidence-intake-closure record of the register in Section 13.3 — naming the finalizing authority that closed the intake, the registered evidence contract identity and version applicable to the execution attempt, the exact closed ordinary-input membership or snapshot by identity, and the authority point at which the closure was accepted — accepted at an authority boundary that is atomic, or safe against competing closure operations to an equivalent degree, at which at most one intake closure becomes current per execution attempt, a write racing that boundary being either included in the closed snapshot, or refused through a distinguishable preserved refusal record of the register in Section 14.2 naming the write it refused and the closure boundary at which it was refused, or preserved outside the attempt's evidence boundary as a post-intake fact in the discipline WP2-EVID-030 states — a late or losing result taking the separately identified post-closure record WP2-EVID-016 requires — never ambiguously omitted, and every losing or superseded closure operation remaining preserved as a distinguishable record.

**WP2-EVID-031.** A finalization **MUST** strictly validate every applicable evidence source of its execution attempt, reconciling among sources only where more than one applicable source exists, as the Section 3.1 finalization definition states.

**WP2-EVID-032.** A contradiction among present applicable sources, a missing required source, an unresolved required validation, or a failure of a replay the registered evidence contract requires **MUST** be treated as a failed finalization rather than as a degraded, partial, or qualified success.

**WP2-EVID-033.** A finalization **MUST** produce every derived artifact the registered evidence contract and the applied finalization rules require of it, each carrying the source and derivation lineage WP2-EVID-005 requires and naming the finalization operation that produced it, as WP2-EVID-072 requires.

**WP2-EVID-034.** The identified completion record of a successful finalization **MUST** be written last among the records of its own finalization operation, after every other record and derived artifact that operation produced, under the finalization-operation identity WP2-EVID-072 requires.

**WP2-EVID-035.** A conforming laboratory **MUST NOT** hold current a successful finalization result whose finalization operation's required derived artifacts, source validations, or completion record are only partially committed, the completeness of that commitment being assessed against the one producing operation's validated source snapshot and recorded output membership, as WP2-EVID-072 states.

**WP2-EVID-036.** The finalization of one execution attempt **MUST** be safe against competing finalizers and competing completion operations, at most one current successful finalization result existing per attempt, decided at the boundaries WP2-LIFE-033 and WP2-LIFE-034 state, with every losing or stale operation preserved under WP2-LIFE-035, every validation, derived artifact, finalization record, and candidate completion record that competing operations interleave in the still-open root remaining attributable, as WP2-EVID-072 requires, to the finalization operation that produced it, every interrupted or otherwise unfinished finalization or completion operation preserved with a recorded, distinguishable disposition in the vocabulary Section 14 states, and a failed finalization concluding through the accepted compound outcome WP2-LIFE-033 states rather than through a preserved rejection.

**WP2-EVID-072.** Every finalization undertaking **MUST** carry an explicit finalization-operation identity that is never reused, every validation, derived artifact, finalization record, and candidate completion record it produces naming the one finalization operation that produced it, the complete output membership of each finalization operation being recorded, the output membership recorded for an operation interrupted before it recorded its own being the membership observed at its recorded disposition and stated as such rather than as a complete membership, the validated source snapshot of a finalization operation being the exact closed ordinary-input membership or snapshot its own ordinary evidence-intake-closure record names, no finalization operation whose intake closure is not the current one for its execution attempt under WP2-EVID-071 holding a current successful finalization result, the successful operation's validated source snapshot and required output membership being coherent and fully committed before that operation's success becomes current, and the outputs of every losing, stale, interrupted, or rejected finalization operation remaining preserved and distinguishable without becoming required outputs of the winning operation and without silently changing the source snapshot the winning operation validated.

**WP2-EVID-037.** The completion record of a successful finalization **MUST** exist, naming under WP2-EVID-072 the one finalization operation whose completion it records, before the compound outcome WP2-LIFE-033 requires — the operational value Completed together with the Finalization succeeded result the Completed transition names — becomes current for the execution attempt — a completion record whose compound outcome is not accepted remaining preserved under WP2-LIFE-035 as a record of that rejected or losing operation rather than becoming the completion record of a successful finalization — as an additional evidence-side precondition of that compound outcome's acceptance that revises no clause of the Section 8.6 entries, no member of the compound outcome, and no rule of WP2-LIFE-033.

**WP2-EVID-038.** A finalization operation **MUST NOT** record, supply, or alter the authoritative-outcome state of any planned run, an execution attempt's result becoming the authoritative outcome of its planned run only through the acceptance operation Section 9 governs.

**WP2-EVID-039.** A failed or cancelled execution attempt **MUST NOT** be given a fictitious successful finalization in order to make its evidence sealable, its terminal evidence closure and any seal over its root covering exactly what exists and stating the failed or incomplete finalization honestly.

**Rationale.** This subsection is the evidence contract the Section 7.12 rationale and the Section 8.6 entries assign here, and it leaves the lifecycle model untouched: the Section 3.1 definition fixes what finalization comprises — strict validation of every applicable evidence source, reconciliation among sources where more than one applies, derived-artifact production, and a completion record written last — the Section 8.6 entries fix which values the finalization dimension takes and who records them, and WP2-LIFE-033 fixes how the operational and finalization dimensions resolve as one coherent compound outcome. Intake closure under WP2-EVID-030 is the earlier of this contract's two closures: the finalization's own derived artifacts, its finalization record, and its completion record are admitted into the attempt's evidence boundary after ordinary intake closes and before the terminal closure WP2-EVID-014 requires, which is why the Section 13.3 evidence-item entry names the finalizing authority as a recording authority for finalization-produced records. That earlier closure is itself a recorded act: the ordinary evidence-intake-closure record of the Section 13.3 register gives it an identity, the finalizing authority that closed the intake, an exact closed ordinary-input membership or snapshot, and a race-safe currentness boundary, as WP2-EVID-071 states, so a write racing the closure has only recorded fates — included in the closed snapshot, refused through a distinguishable preserved refusal record, or preserved outside the boundary as a post-intake fact — and never an ambiguous omission. What WP2-EVID-037 adds is strictly evidence-side: the compound Completed and Finalization succeeded outcome already names a separately recorded success result, and this section requires the identified completion record of that success to exist before the compound outcome becomes current, so that the record whose absence signals an interrupted commit is in place before any current status asserts success. WP2-EVID-036 adds no second acceptance boundary — the competing-finalizer race is decided exactly where WP2-LIFE-033 and WP2-LIFE-034 already decide it, and this requirement makes the finalization operation's safety against that race a testable evidence property, with losers preserved under WP2-LIFE-035. Below that lifecycle boundary, the finalization-operation identity WP2-EVID-072 requires keeps the race legible in the evidence itself: two finalizers can interleave their records and derived artifacts in the still-open root, and because every validation, derived artifact, finalization record, and candidate completion record names its own operation and each operation's complete output membership is recorded, the winning operation's success is checkable against exactly its own validated source snapshot and required outputs, every losing, stale, interrupted, or rejected operation's outputs remaining preserved and distinguishable rather than silently absorbed. WP2-EVID-038 states the operation-side counterpart of WP2-LIFE-006 and WP2-SCHED-053: finalizing is not accepting, and no finalizer, sealer, or verifier acquires commit authority by finishing its own work. WP2-EVID-039 closes the remaining honesty gap — sealing covers what exists, so a failed attempt needs no fictitious success to be preserved, which is exactly the separation WP2-EVID-017's closure result and WP2-EVID-043's sealing of a closed root whose execution-produced evidence subset is empty make representable. Section 5.7 records that the repository's two current finalization paths follow different contracts and that its commit ordering guards against interruption rather than against a competing writer; both are observations about the current system, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps them from being read as requirements. No transaction primitive, store, or staging arrangement is selected here, and WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting one.

### 13.9 Inventory, run-level sealing, packages, and verification

**WP2-EVID-040.** Every inventory **MUST** identify the exact evidence boundary it enumerates, its own inventory identity, the versioned inventory rules applied, and every member of the boundary by stable identity and content digest, together with metadata sufficient to detect omission, duplication, substitution, and mutation — a full-root inventory, one whose boundary is a closed run-evidence-root boundary, enumerating every member of that boundary, the attempt evidence-closure record and every non-execution record among them, and an inventory over the execution-produced evidence subset naming that narrower subset explicitly as its boundary rather than the root.

**WP2-EVID-041.** Inventory membership **MUST** be determined by the identified boundary and the registered evidence contract, and not by a selection of whichever files or records happen to be present, favorable, or convenient.

**WP2-EVID-042.** A missing required member, an unexpected member the registered evidence contract requires to be accounted for, a duplicate member identity, or a conflicting digest **MUST** prevent a clean completeness verdict for the inventory concerned.

**WP2-EVID-043.** One run-level seal **MUST** authenticate exactly one closed run-evidence-root boundary in its entirety and the exact full-root inventory it names, never claiming a zero-member boundary where the attempt evidence-closure record is itself a member, the root of an execution attempt whose execution-produced evidence subset is empty being sealable like any other enumerated boundary — that empty subset remaining the explicit fact the attempt evidence-closure record states under WP2-EVID-017 — and a sub-inventory over that empty subset never being substituted for the full-root inventory or the seal.

**WP2-EVID-044.** Every seal record **MUST** identify the sealing rules and their version, the sealing authority, the inventory it authenticates, the evidence boundary it authenticates, and its digest facts, residence inside the boundary it authenticates being required of no seal record.

**WP2-EVID-045.** A seal or an inventory **MUST NOT** exclude itself, or any seal, package, or verification record, from the boundary it authenticates or enumerates through an unstated self-reference convention, the authenticated or enumerated boundary and every excluded seal, package, or verification record being identified explicitly.

**WP2-EVID-046.** A sealed package **MUST** seal exactly one identified evidence boundary, consistent with the Section 6.7 sealed-package entry and WP2-ID-020.

**WP2-EVID-047.** A boundary already sealed by a package **MUST** receive any later package only under a new sealed-package identity carrying explicit correction or supersession lineage to the earlier package, which remains preserved, as charter Section 14.9 requires of an original package.

**WP2-EVID-048.** Every seal verification **MUST** be read-only, carry its own verification identity — the identity by which an attempt-integrity-basis record names it under the attempt-integrity-basis entry of the register in Section 13.3 — name the exact package or seal, inventory, boundary, and verification rules and version it verified, and produce a preserved verdict.

**WP2-EVID-049.** A post-seal mutation, a missing member, an extra member, a digest mismatch, an inventory mismatch, or a package corruption **MUST** be recorded as a typed integrity failure, in the vocabulary Section 14 states, and never repaired by silently replacing, recomputing, or rewriting the original seal or package under its same identity — an integrity failure detected after an authoritative acceptance never rewriting the accepted outcome, remaining instead a preserved post-acceptance integrity incident whose study-completion, aggregation, validity, and claim consequences are recorded through the records of Sections 14 and 15.

**WP2-EVID-050.** A packaging or seal-verification operation **MUST NOT** mutate a run evidence root.

**WP2-EVID-051.** A completed, successfully finalized execution attempt **MUST NOT** be considered for, or become eligible for, authoritative planned-run acceptance until one exact attempt-integrity basis exists for it — an attempt-integrity-basis record of the register in Section 13.3, naming exactly one closed run-evidence-root boundary, the attempt's own; exactly one full-root inventory enumerating that whole closed boundary; exactly one run-level seal authenticating that inventory and boundary; and exactly one seal-verification record evaluating that seal against that inventory and boundary under identified verification rules and version, together with the successful verdict it preserved — none of them absent — that basis current under WP2-EVID-073 and successful in the sense the attempt-integrity-basis entry states, the acceptance that later relies on it naming it and verifying it current and successful at the authoritative-commit boundary, as WP2-EVID-073 states — an additional evidence-integrity eligibility condition standing alongside the conditions WP2-SCHED-025 states — necessary and not sufficient, exactly as the Section 8.6 Finalization succeeded entry frames WP2-SCHED-025's conditions — transferring no authoritative-commit authority to the finalizing, sealing, or verifying authority, the authoritative-commit authority remaining the authority the registered authoritative-commit policy names, which WP2-SCHED-053 keeps distinct from the assignment authority under which a result is produced and finalized.

**WP2-EVID-073.** The attempt-integrity basis of one execution attempt **MUST** be decided race-safely — at most one attempt-integrity-basis record of the register in Section 13.3 being current per execution attempt, currentness decided at an authority boundary that is atomic, or safe against competing basis operations to an equivalent degree, every losing or superseded basis operation preserved as a distinguishable record, and every correction, supersession, and currentness relation among bases remaining explicit — every acceptance record of the kind WP2-SCHED-053 names, naming the exact basis on which it relied, that basis verified current and successful at the authoritative-commit boundary, no basis being selected by arrival order, by favorable outcome, or by the mere existence of some historical successful verification, and a current basis over whose named closed run-evidence-root boundary, full-root inventory, or run-level seal a later recorded verification or integrity result that is not successful stands ceasing to be current from the recording of that result, a further basis becoming current only through a further basis operation decided over the complete recorded results.

**Rationale.** An inventory answers what a boundary holds, a seal authenticates that answer, a package carries the authenticated boundary, and a verification checks all three read-only — four records, each named by identity, in the layering WP2-SCOPE-008 requires to be reported distinctly and WP2-ID-020 requires each seal to identify. The boundary each of them names is exact in both directions: a full-root inventory — one whose boundary is a closed run-evidence-root boundary — enumerates every member of that boundary, the attempt evidence-closure record and every non-execution record among them, so a closed root is never enumerated as empty merely because its execution-produced evidence subset is, that emptiness remaining the explicit fact the closure record states, and a sub-inventory over that subset names its narrower boundary explicitly rather than standing in for the full-root inventory or the seal, as WP2-EVID-040 and WP2-EVID-043 state. The self-reference rule exists because a seal cannot digest itself: the honest resolution is to state the authenticated or enumerated boundary and every excluded seal, package, or verification record explicitly, so that an auditor can tell an excluded-by-convention record from an omitted member, which WP2-EVID-042 makes a completeness defect. WP2-EVID-051 is the eligibility half of this section's relationship with Section 9: WP2-SCHED-025 already makes Completed and Finalization succeeded necessary and not sufficient for consideration, WP2-SCHED-026 already verifies identity, lineage, authority currency, treatment, cancellation state, and the absence of a prior outcome, and this requirement adds the evidence-integrity facts to what eligibility takes — one exact current, successful attempt-integrity basis naming the attempt's closed run-evidence-root boundary, one full-root inventory enumerating that whole closed boundary, one run-level seal authenticating that inventory and boundary, and one seal-verification record that preserved a successful verdict over them, none of them absent — without moving the acceptance operation, its authority, or its boundary, which WP2-SCHED-030 and WP2-SCHED-053 continue to govern. That unconditional chain is compatible with the register's zero-or-more cardinalities because those cardinalities describe a root's history rather than an acceptance: a closed root holds zero seals before any sealing operation, after a failed one, or where its attempt never approaches authoritative acceptance, while WP2-EVID-051 constrains only the exact current basis of an attempt considered for authoritative acceptance, which names one complete successful chain — and an attempt that cannot produce a member of that chain remains represented through the applicable typed failure or blocker, a record of the packaging, inventory, seal, or seal-verification failure class the Section 14.4 taxonomy states, the typed integrity failure WP2-EVID-049 states among them, or, for a cancellation-terminal attempt, which is not a completed, successfully finalized attempt and is untouched by this rule, the typed sealing blocker WP2-FAIL-026 permits, and never becomes eligible through an empty set of verdicts. The attempt-integrity basis is what makes current and successful checkable rather than selectable: one root accumulates inventories, seals, and verification performances over time, and WP2-EVID-073 fixes by identity which of them an acceptance relied upon — decided race-safely, never by arrival order or by favorable outcome — while an integrity failure detected after an authoritative acceptance rewrites nothing, remaining the preserved post-acceptance integrity incident WP2-EVID-049 states, whose study-completion, aggregation, validity, and claim consequences are recorded through the records of Sections 14 and 15. Section 5.9 records the current sequence-root packaging transaction and its confirmed archive-format defect, including that the receipt attests a digest over the committed archive rather than its container format; that history is why verification here names its rules and version and why a later package is a new identity with the original preserved, and under WP2-SCOPE-010 it remains evidence rather than a requirement, this section deliberately not settling whether a future packaging version is independently format-verified — the later question Section 5.14 item 4 records — and requiring of the verification an applicable requirement of this section names — the seal verification an acceptance-supporting attempt-integrity basis names among them — only, on that question, that it be performed under identified rules and version and its verdict preserved, the further conditions WP2-EVID-048 and WP2-EVID-051 state of such a verification, the successful verdict an acceptance-supporting basis names included, standing unaffected. No archive format, digest algorithm, inventory encoding, seal representation, or verification schedule is selected, and Section 13.13 records each as unselected.

### 13.10 Study evidence root and study-level sealing

**WP2-EVID-052.** The study evidence root of every study **MUST** identify or contain every run evidence root belonging to that study and every study-level record the registered evidence contract requires.

**WP2-EVID-053.** The complete registered membership of a study evidence root **MUST** be explicit and identity-based, never reconstructed from directory layout, storage location, timestamps, or a count of successful runs.

**WP2-EVID-054.** A study-level record created after an execution attempt's run evidence root closes **MUST** remain outside that closed attempt root while retaining explicit lineage to it.

**WP2-EVID-055.** A study-level seal **MUST** attest completeness and integrity over all evidence roots and all study-level evidence registered to its study, whether the registered set contains one root, several roots, or no execution-produced evidence — naming the exact study-membership-and-completion ledger identity and its version or correction lineage it read and the study's current registered-membership-closure result as it stood when the seal was produced, a complete study-level seal — one attesting a closed registered roster rather than a snapshot of an open one — being recorded only against a current reached registered-membership-closure result whose completeness it attests, reached as WP2-AGG-016 requires, whose roster cannot lawfully acquire a further execution attempt or replacement under the current registration, a study-level seal or study-level package produced before that closure being labeled a partial or snapshot boundary and never recorded or reported as a complete study-level seal, and later lawful membership under a new registered boundary producing new inventory, seal, and lineage records without rewriting the earlier snapshot or the records it names.

**WP2-EVID-056.** A sealed package over one evidence root **MUST NOT** be recorded or reported as a study-level seal merely because the study's registered evidence happens to lie within that one root.

**WP2-EVID-057.** Study-level sealing, run-level sealing, sealed packaging, study aggregation, and analysis **MUST** remain distinct operations producing distinct records, no one of them performed, recorded, or reported as another.

**WP2-EVID-058.** A later study-level package or study-level seal **MUST NOT** rewrite an earlier study-level package or seal, or the run evidence either of them references.

**Rationale.** The study evidence root is the Section 6.7 identity — an identity-bearing logical namespace or registered set under WP2-ID-019 — and these requirements give it the completeness contract WP2-SCOPE-008 presupposes: a study-level seal is precisely the seal attesting completeness and integrity over all the evidence roots registered to one study, so its membership has to come from the registration rather than from what storage happens to contain, and WP2-ISO-022 continues to prohibit any seal from attesting past the boundary it names. The registration's roster reaches the seal as an identified snapshot: WP2-EVID-055 names the exact study-membership-and-completion ledger identity and version or correction lineage it read and the study's registered-membership-closure result as it stood when the seal was produced, a complete study-level seal being recorded only against a current reached result whose completeness it attests, so a seal produced while a planned run of the study can still lawfully admit a further execution attempt or replacement is a labeled partial or snapshot boundary rather than a complete study-level seal, and later lawful membership under a new registered boundary produces new inventory, seal, and lineage records with the earlier snapshot preserved. WP2-EVID-056 addresses the coincidence Section 3.1's sealing row records: where a study registers all its evidence into one root, the run-root package and the study boundary coincide in content without the package establishing that they do, and the study-level attestation remains a distinct record that names the registered membership it attests. WP2-EVID-057 extends the WP2-SCOPE-008 reporting distinction to the operations themselves and adds analysis, so that aggregation under WP2-ID-021 and Section 15, sealing at either level, and packaging can never be discharged by one another's records. The outside-the-closed-root rule is WP2-EVID-015 seen from the study side: study-level facts accumulate after attempt roots close, and they do so outside those roots with explicit lineage inward, which is what keeps a study assemblable without reopening anything. No study-root realization, membership representation, or sealing procedure is selected, and WP2-SCOPE-015 continues to prohibit selecting one. The partial or snapshot label WP2-EVID-055 requires names no further record type and is not the partial-study record of Section 15.7, whose identification set WP2-AGG-027 fixes for study-level completion and membership records.

### 13.11 Evidence-size and retention

**WP2-EVID-059.** Every evidence-size and retention policy a conforming laboratory applies **MUST** be versioned and identify the evidence classes it governs and the studies, evidence roots, packages, and records to which it applies.

**WP2-EVID-060.** Capacity or evidence-size exhaustion **MUST** produce a typed, evidence-preserving refusal or failure, never a silent truncation, drop, overwrite, compression into a different unidentified representation, or omission of required evidence.

**WP2-EVID-061.** A process restart, an orchestrator restart, or the loss of a laboratory worker **MUST NOT** reset evidence-size accounting or retention state.

**WP2-EVID-062.** Every policy-authorized retention disposition **MUST** be explicit, attributable, recorded under the policy identity and version that authorized it, and incapable of making an incomplete or unavailable evidence set appear complete or replayable.

**WP2-EVID-063.** Evidence required by an active sealed package, a registered claim dependency, an audit, or a required review **MUST NOT** be lost through an unrecorded retention action.

**Rationale.** Retention is where an evidence contract quietly fails years after execution, which is why the policy is a versioned registered object, the disposition is a recorded act under it, and exhaustion is a refusal rather than an accommodation: WP2-ISO-030 through WP2-ISO-032 state the refusal discipline, WP2-MODEL-033 and WP2-MODEL-042 state the restart half for idempotency and budget state, and WP2-EVID-061 states the same for size accounting and retention state, because a laboratory that forgot how much evidence it holds whenever a process died would rediscover capacity by destroying something. The honesty clause of WP2-EVID-062 is the retention counterpart of WP2-EVID-020: a disposition can remove what its policy authorizes it to remove, and what it can never do is leave records claiming that the removed material is still present, complete, or replayable. This subsection states what the WP2-SCHED-034 rationale assigns here — retention rules, evidence-size budgets, and what may be discarded after a defined retention period — as properties of recorded policy and disposition, and it selects no retention duration, storage technology, compression, deletion mechanism, or capacity value, each recorded as unselected in Section 13.13, the selections WP2-SCOPE-015 enumerates remaining prohibited throughout this document.

### 13.12 Secret material already persisted or admitted

Section 16.8 states the refusal contract for live secret material detected in candidate output — output not yet persisted or admitted as evidence. Section 16.10 assigns onward the case that contract cannot reach: live secret material detected in a record already persisted or admitted as authoritative evidence, whose evidentiary treatment lands here and whose incident typing, remediation, and disposition belong to Section 14. This subsection coordinates with Section 16 by citation and revises no clause of it.

**WP2-EVID-064.** Discovery of live secret material in a record already persisted or admitted as evidence **MUST** create a typed, nonsecret incident record carrying, for the affected record, the identification WP2-SEC-014 requires of a detected-secret incident — its source event, the surface on which the material was detected, the recording authority, the registered secret-handling rule identity and version under which the detection was made, and every affected entity that requirement enumerates — and record every affected run evidence root, inventory, seal, package, and study-level surface as affected or compromised through that incident record and any further separately identified record outside every closed or sealed boundary concerned, each naming the affected surface explicitly in the WP2-EVID-015 discipline, no affected surface being left recorded as clean and none of them being altered in place.

**WP2-EVID-065.** A discovery WP2-EVID-064 records **MUST** block every new clean-sealing claim over the affected material, the authoritative acceptance of every execution attempt whose evidence includes it, and every clean-aggregation claim over it, until an explicit disposition record of Section 14 dispositions that material under the registered policy applicable to it, or, where none applies, under the absence that record states.

**WP2-EVID-066.** A discovery WP2-EVID-064 records **MUST** trigger the registered credential-revocation or rotation path of Section 16.6, wherever the affected material concerns a credential that path governs.

**WP2-EVID-067.** The handling of a discovery WP2-EVID-064 records **MUST NOT** copy the secret material into any new incident record, log, package, aggregate, or report.

**WP2-EVID-068.** Quarantine, access restriction, or removal of affected evidence **MUST** proceed only through an explicit security and evidence disposition that preserves the nonsecret identity, digest, source-event lineage, and mutation lineage of what it affects.

**WP2-EVID-069.** Every sanitized derivative produced from affected evidence **MUST** be a new identified derivative, explicitly identified as sanitized and carrying the nonsecret lineage WP2-SEC-016 requires of a sanitized derivative of refused candidate output, without retaining the secret material.

**WP2-EVID-070.** The disposition of affected evidence **MUST NOT** rewrite an existing seal, or record, report, or claim that a sanitized derivative is the original evidence.

**Rationale.** The already-persisted case differs from Section 16.8's in exactly one respect: the bytes are inside boundaries this section makes immutable, so the two obligations that never trade against each other — WP2-SEC-002's prohibition on persisted secrets and charter Section 14.9's prohibition on concealed evidence loss or mutation — meet an existing seal instead of a refusable candidate. The resolution keeps both: the affected surfaces are recorded as affected from outside every closed or sealed boundary rather than silently cleaned, new clean claims stop until an explicit disposition record of Section 14 dispositions the affected material under the registered policy applicable to it, or, where none applies, under the absence that record states, and any quarantine, restriction, or removal is an explicit security and evidence disposition whose nonsecret lineage records exactly what changed, so the evidence set never claims a completeness or an originality it no longer has — WP2-EVID-062's honesty rule reaching this disposition as it reaches a retention one. Nothing here requires the ordinary retention of usable secret bytes merely for scientific preservation: what is preserved is the nonsecret record of what existed and what was done, and the affected seal stands as the historical record of what it authenticated, recorded as affected from outside itself rather than rewritten. The incident taxonomy, the remediation path, and the dispositions belong to Section 14, exactly as Section 16.10 assigns them; the credential consequences are Section 16.6's own, reached here by citation; and no quarantine, redaction, deletion, or secret-management mechanism is selected, as Section 13.13 records.

### 13.13 Options deliberately left unselected

**Unselected option.** Archive and serialization technology. The representation of an inventory, seal, package, closure record, ordinary evidence-intake-closure record, or attempt-integrity-basis record may use any archive or serialization technology. WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting one, and the properties any selection has to satisfy are stated in WP2-EVID-040 through WP2-EVID-050, in WP2-EVID-071 for the ordinary evidence-intake-closure record, and in WP2-EVID-073 for the attempt-integrity-basis record.

**Unselected option.** Digest and hash algorithm. The content digests of WP2-EVID-040, the digest facts of WP2-EVID-044, and the hashes or comparisons of WP2-EVID-022 may rest on any algorithm and any canonicalization scheme the registered evidence contract adopts. No algorithm, canonicalization scheme, length, or encoding is selected.

**Unselected option.** Inventory encoding and seal representation. An inventory may be one record, a manifest, an index, or another arrangement, and a seal may be represented in any form that identifies what WP2-EVID-044 requires. No encoding, file layout, or representation is selected.

**Unselected option.** Partial or snapshot boundary labeling. How the partial or snapshot label WP2-EVID-055 requires of a pre-closure study-level seal or study-level package is carried — on the seal record, in a separate labeling record, or in another explicit form — is unselected. WP2-EVID-055 and the study-level-seal and sealed-package entries of Section 13.3 state the properties any form has to satisfy; no representation is selected.

**Unselected option.** Verification scheduling. When and how often seal verification runs — at packaging, before acceptance eligibility under WP2-EVID-051, on resume, periodically, or on another schedule — is unselected, provided the verification an applicable requirement names exists and its verdict is preserved. No schedule or trigger is selected.

**Unselected option.** Replay engine and UI path. The replay implementation WP2-EVID-022 requires to be identified, and any UI replay surface, may be realized by any engine, harness, or surface. No engine, browser, headless split, or invocation path is selected, and WP2-EVID-025 keeps the absence of a UI surface from being a nonconformity.

**Unselected option.** Post-closure record placement. The separately identified post-closure records of WP2-EVID-016, and the study-level records of WP2-EVID-054, may live in a study evidence root, a separate record store, an event stream, or another arrangement outside the closed boundary they reference. No placement or store is selected.

**Unselected option.** Retention realization. Retention durations, storage technologies, compression, deletion mechanisms, capacity values, and the realization of evidence-size accounting are unselected. WP2-EVID-059 through WP2-EVID-063 state the recorded-policy, refusal, and preservation properties; WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting a duration or capacity value.

**Unselected option.** Quarantine and redaction mechanism. The explicit disposition WP2-EVID-068 requires may be realized by quarantine, access restriction, removal to controlled custody, or another mechanism, under the registered policy applicable to it, or, where none applies, under the absence that disposition states. No quarantine, redaction, deletion, or secret-management mechanism is selected, as Section 16.9 records for the neighboring boundary.

**Unselected option.** Evidence-contract representation. How a study's registered evidence contract is represented, carried, and joined to its registration is unselected. The definitional statement of Section 13.2 names the role it plays; no schema, format, or store is selected.

### 13.14 Boundaries assigned elsewhere

This section states which evidence records exist, what each of them names, how a run evidence root is populated, closed, and preserved, how replay and finalization are evidenced, how inventories, seals, packages, and verifications authenticate boundaries, how study-level sealing attests registered membership, how evidence-size and retention are governed, and how already-persisted secret material is treated without concealment. It deliberately defines none of the following, each of which another section or the conformance document states: the failure-class taxonomy, the typed disposition vocabulary that the typed failures, refusals, and integrity problems of this section carry, and the determination vocabulary of the Section 8.6 evidence-validity dimension whose evidence rules Sections 13.6 and 13.8 supply, which Section 14 states; the incident typing, remediation, and dispositions of the discovery WP2-EVID-064 records, which Section 14 states, as Section 16.10 assigns; the completion and aggregation consequences of closures, seals, missing evidence, dispositions, and invalidity for a study's result, which Section 15 states; the conformance checks, fault injections, and pass conditions for every requirement stated here, which belong to Section 17 and to the conformance document; and the implementation handoff and the ranking of the options this section leaves unselected, which belong to Section 18.

## 14. Failure, cancellation, retry, resume, and replacement

### 14.1 What this section establishes

This section defines the implementation-neutral failure and disposition contract of a conforming laboratory: the disposition-record register whose eight record types carry every incident, refusal, disposition, and decision this contract produces; the failure-class taxonomy under which those records classify what happened; the rules that keep an incident's cause, observed class, lifecycle consequence, evidence consequence, and research disposition separate; the distinctions among the seven operations that repetition, interruption, and recovery produce; the cancellation dispositions; the admission of further execution attempts and registered replacements; and the containment of failure propagation and the study-invalidity decision. It answers what the earlier sections assign here: the disposition vocabulary whose absence Section 5.10 records and assigns to Section 14 — a transport or delivery retry, a laboratory-worker restart, a cancellation, a lease expiry, stale laboratory-worker output, containment of sibling units, and a partially dispositioned wave among the cases the current taxonomy cannot name; the failure and disposition taxonomy behind the typed refusals WP2-ISO-031 requires, which Section 7.21 assigns here; the failure-class taxonomy, the typed disposition vocabulary, replacement eligibility, sample-size treatment, and the containment of sibling units after a failure, which Section 8.16 assigns here; the determination vocabulary of the artifact-or-evidence-validity dimension, which the Section 8.4 dimension table and the Section 8.6 *Evidence validity determined* entry assign to Sections 13 and 14 and which the artifact-invalidity class of Section 14.4 carries, the evidence rules themselves remaining Section 13's; the taxonomy and disposition vocabulary behind the refusals and non-authoritative results of Section 9, the disposition of superseded output among them, which Section 9.13 assigns here; what counts as a material divergence and what disposition a deviation or violation receives, which Section 10.13 assigns here; the typed vocabulary of the timing-integrity problems WP2-TIME-019 requires to be surfaced, which Section 11.13 assigns here; the failure-class taxonomy, the typed disposition vocabulary behind the refusals, non-authoritative results, and unresolved exposures of Section 12, and what counts as a material provider deviation, which Section 12.11 assigns here; the failure and disposition vocabulary that Section 13's typed failures use, which Section 13.14 assigns here; and the incident taxonomy and typed dispositions of a detected-secret incident, a boundary violation, or a refusal, together with the incident typing, remediation disposition, and consequence of live secret material detected in a record already persisted or admitted as evidence, which Section 16.10 assigns here and whose evidentiary treatment Section 13 states. It selects no mechanism: no error-code set, incident schema, monitoring product, classification algorithm, retry count, replacement count, eligibility threshold, or numeric operating parameter is selected anywhere in it, and the selections WP2-SCOPE-015 enumerates remain prohibited throughout this document.

Section 14.2 states how the disposition-record register is read, carries the requirement that makes it binding, and holds the register itself. Section 14.3 keeps cause, class, consequence, and disposition separate. Section 14.4 states the failure-class taxonomy. Section 14.5 states preservation and correction. Section 14.6 distinguishes retry, restart, recovery, and resume. Section 14.7 states cancellation dispositions. Section 14.8 governs further execution attempts and registered replacements. Section 14.9 governs failure propagation and study invalidity. Section 14.10 records options deliberately left unselected, and Section 14.11 states the boundaries assigned elsewhere.

Every substantive statement here is a Required future invariant carrying exactly one `WP2-FAIL-###` identifier, a Design rationale introduced by a **Rationale.** lead-in, or an Unselected option introduced by an **Unselected option.** lead-in, in the Section 4.3 sense, or else a register entry of Section 14.2, its introducing statements included, whose normative force flows through WP2-FAIL-001 rather than through an identifier of its own, an entry of the attribute table in Section 14.2, which states how the register is read and carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements that cite it, an entry of the failure-class taxonomy table in Section 14.4, its introducing statements included, whose normative force flows through WP2-FAIL-005 rather than through an identifier of its own, or a boundary statement in Section 14.11. Nothing in this section is a Current fact or a Current limitation; those belong to Section 5, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps a current mechanism from being read as a requirement. Every defined term used here carries the meaning Section 3.1 assigns it, as WP2-SCOPE-005 requires, and every lifecycle term carries the meaning Section 8.3 assigns it.

### 14.2 How the disposition-record register is read, and the register

Each register entry states the same eight attributes in the same order. The register supplies the normative disposition-record definitions of this section, which WP2-FAIL-001 incorporates and makes binding clause by clause, except for the specific question an entry expressly assigns to a later section. Where an entry names a refusal, rejection, cancellation, disposition, or non-authoritative result that an approved requirement of Sections 6 through 12 or Section 16 already requires, the entry gives that record its common record model and revises nothing in the requirement that creates it.

| Attribute | What the entry states |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | The domain within which the record's identity distinguishes one instance of the record type from every other instance of that record type. |
| Recording or deciding authority | The role answerable for recording the record or for making the decision it states. |
| Affected entities | The identified entities the record names as affected or concerned, and how an absence is stated. |
| Required source evidence | The records and facts the record names as the basis on which it was recorded or decided. |
| Governing policy identity and version | The registered policy, where one applies, under which the record was recorded or the decision was made, identified by identity and version. |
| Applicable time-domain facts | The time-domain facts Section 11 makes applicable to the record. |
| Correction or supersession rule | What may and may not be done with the record and its identity after it has been recorded. |
| Effect on lifecycle state, evidence preservation, authoritative acceptance, replacement eligibility, aggregate inclusion, and study validity | What the record does, and does not do, to each of those six surfaces. |

**WP2-FAIL-001.** Every record of a record type of the disposition-record register in Section 14.2 that a conforming laboratory creates, records, references, or relies upon **MUST** conform to every clause of that register — its introducing statements and, for the applicable entry, its identity scope, recording or deciding authority, affected entities, required source evidence, governing policy identity and version, applicable time-domain facts, correction or supersession rule, and effect or absence of effect on lifecycle state, evidence preservation, authoritative acceptance, replacement eligibility, aggregate inclusion, and study validity — except only the specific question or clause that the entry expressly assigns to a later section, an exemption that reaches no other clause of that attribute or entry.

**Rationale.** An authority named in the register is a role — the party answerable for detecting an incident, deciding a disposition, or refusing an operation — and never a component, service, monitor, or store, exactly as Section 6.2 keeps every authority in the entity register a role, and naming a role selects no mechanism, which WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting. WP2-FAIL-001 is the single requirement that incorporates the register and makes every non-deferred clause binding, in the way WP2-ID-027 binds the entity register, WP2-LIFE-001 binds the lifecycle models, WP2-SCHED-051 binds the assignment-authority record model, WP2-MODEL-001 binds the model-request record register, and WP2-SEC-017 binds the credential-authority event model, so that no individual table cell carries a requirement identifier of its own, and a cell that expressly assigns a question to a later section states a boundary rather than an answer, consistent with Section 4.3. The entries below reference requirements of the approved sections in the way the Section 12.3 logical-model-request entry references the Section 6.6 identity: the reference adds the eight-attribute record model and edits nothing, and each governing requirement continues to state its own conditions and contents.

One register entry follows for each of the eight record types, in the order in which this section states them, from the incident and cancellation records, through the refusal, unit-disposition, exclusion, and repetition decisions, to the replacement and study-invalidity decisions.

**Failure or integrity incident record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every failure or integrity incident record the laboratory records, across all studies, all planned runs, all execution attempts, all laboratory workers, all shared infrastructure, and all concurrent execution, each incident record separately identified within that scope. One event that gives rise to records of several register types or several classes remains one event with several explicitly linked records, as WP2-FAIL-004 requires. |
| Recording or deciding authority | The role that detected the incident or determined that it occurred — the refusing, verifying, observing, finalizing, or auditing role the applicable approved section names for the fact concerned. |
| Affected entities | Every study, planned run, execution attempt, laboratory worker, dispatch wave, assignment-authority grant or lease, model request, provider attempt, evidence root, seal, package, or other identified entity the incident in fact affected, named by identity, naming each of those that applies and stating the absence of any that does not; an incident of infrastructure scope that affects no registered unit states that fact rather than borrowing a unit. |
| Required source evidence | The records that establish that the incident occurred — the refusal, rejection, divergence, timing, budget, provider-behavior, replay, finalization, seal-verification, or detected-secret records concerned, named by identity — with an unavailable or unobservable source stated honestly, in the WP2-TIME-010 discipline, rather than inferred. |
| Governing policy identity and version | The registered policy under which the incident was detected or classified, identified by identity and version where one applies; where none applies, the record states that absence, and the classification stands as an unregistered divergence in the sense WP2-MODEL-021 states for its own records. |
| Applicable time-domain facts | The time-domain facts Section 11 makes applicable. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never rewritten in place. A correction or reclassification is a new identified record under WP2-FAIL-013 and WP2-FAIL-014, in the discipline WP2-LIFE-017, WP2-ID-025, and WP2-ID-026 state. |
| Effect on lifecycle state, evidence preservation, authoritative acceptance, replacement eligibility, aggregate inclusion, and study validity | None by itself, on any of the six surfaces. The incident record states what happened and its observed class; every consequence is recorded through an explicit disposition record naming the registered policy applicable to it, or the absence of any, as WP2-FAIL-008 and WP2-FAIL-009 require, and no incident record reduces evidence preservation. |

**Cancellation incident record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every cancellation the laboratory records — each cancellation operation issued at study scope, planned-run scope, or execution-attempt scope in the sense WP2-LIFE-027 states, together with the recorded consequence for each unit it in fact reached. This entry gives the cancellation record its common model and revises neither WP2-LIFE-027 nor WP2-LIFE-028. |
| Recording or deciding authority | The issuing authority WP2-LIFE-027 requires the cancellation to identify. Where the cancellation races an acceptance or another authority operation, the resolution is decided under the registered policy WP2-SCHED-031 requires, at the recorded authority boundary, never from wall-clock arrival order. |
| Affected entities | The one unit at whose scope the cancellation was issued, each unit whose recorded status it changed, and the affected lineage below the issued scope, each named by identity; a sibling or unrelated unit is never among them, as WP2-ISO-023 and WP2-LIFE-028 keep it. |
| Required source evidence | The reason WP2-LIFE-027 requires, together with the incident, policy, or authority records that ground it, named by identity. |
| Governing policy identity and version | The registered cancellation policy identity and version WP2-LIFE-027 requires and, where a race arose, the registered authority policy identity and version WP2-SCHED-032 requires the resolution record to identify. |
| Applicable time-domain facts | The time-domain facts Section 11 makes applicable, the applicable logical and wall-clock facts among them, each domain identified as WP2-TIME-001 requires. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never rewritten in place; corrected under WP2-FAIL-013 and WP2-FAIL-014. |
| Effect on lifecycle state, evidence preservation, authoritative acceptance, replacement eligibility, aggregate inclusion, and study validity | The recorded status of each unit whose recorded status the cancellation changed, as WP2-LIFE-027 requires the cancellation to identify and as the transitions Sections 8.5 and 8.6 permit; beyond that, what WP2-FAIL-022 through WP2-FAIL-027 state: further operations under the cancelled authority are prevented; evidence and accounting already produced are preserved; every execution attempt the cancellation makes terminal receives the terminal evidence closure Section 13 requires of every terminal execution attempt and, over its actual closed run-evidence-root boundary, the current full-root inventory and exactly one current recorded sealing outcome WP2-FAIL-026 requires — a run-level seal over that inventory with a preserved seal-verification verdict, or a typed, preserved record of the packaging, inventory, seal, or seal-verification failure class the Section 14.4 taxonomy states, recording the failed or unperformable inventory, seal, or seal-verification operation and the condition that blocked it — the registered evidence contract governing the rules and representations applied without making the full-root inventory or the sealing outcome inapplicable; and replacement eligibility, aggregate inclusion, and study validity are unchanged by the cancellation itself, changing only through an explicit disposition record naming the registered policy applicable to it, or the absence of any, as WP2-FAIL-009 requires. |

**Refusal and rejection record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every typed refusal, every preserved rejection, and every recorded non-authoritative result the approved sections require, each separately identified — the refusals WP2-ISO-030 and WP2-ISO-031 require; the transition refusals WP2-LIFE-030 and the race rejections WP2-LIFE-035 require; the conflicting-delivery refusals WP2-SCHED-006, the race-resolution rejections WP2-SCHED-032, the grant-operation rejections WP2-SCHED-052, and the non-authoritative results WP2-SCHED-029 require; the identity/content conflict refusals WP2-MODEL-026, the preserved non-authoritative responses and resolutions WP2-MODEL-032, and the typed budget refusals WP2-MODEL-043 require; the intake-closure race refusals WP2-EVID-071 requires; and the detected-secret refusals WP2-SEC-013 requires and the losing credential-lifecycle operations preserved under WP2-SEC-017 — among them. This entry gives those records one common record model and revises none of the requirements that create them; each continues to record every fact its own governing requirement states. |
| Recording or deciding authority | The role that decided the refusal, or the role at whose authority boundary the rejection was decided. |
| Affected entities | The refused or rejected operation, the authority that attempted it, and each entity the attempted operation named, with such owning lineage as the operation demonstrated recorded, and the absence stated where none was demonstrated, as WP2-ISO-031 states for the refusals it governs. |
| Required source evidence | Each condition the refused operation met, the competing operation or current record that prevailed where the record is a rejection, and the facts demonstrated at the deciding boundary, named by identity. |
| Governing policy identity and version | The governing requirement concerned and, where one applies, the registered policy identity and version under which the refusal or rejection was decided, with the absence of any applicable registered policy stated where none does. |
| Applicable time-domain facts | The time-domain facts Section 11 makes applicable. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never rewritten in place; corrected under WP2-FAIL-013 and WP2-FAIL-014. |
| Effect on lifecycle state, evidence preservation, authoritative acceptance, replacement eligibility, aggregate inclusion, and study validity | None by itself beyond stopping the refused operation, which its governing requirement already states. A refusal or rejection establishes no failure class beyond the one it records, no artifact or study invalidity, no exclusion, no replacement eligibility, and no aggregate omission, as WP2-FAIL-008 requires, and it never reduces evidence, as WP2-ISO-032, WP2-LIFE-031, and WP2-LIFE-035 keep it. |

**Unit disposition record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every unit disposition the laboratory records — the recorded operational, evidence, or research disposition of exactly one study, planned run, or execution attempt, or of one separately identified output of an execution attempt, the dispositions of superseded, late, losing, duplicate, stale, and non-authoritative output among them — each disposition record separately identified. |
| Recording or deciding authority | The role the applicable approved section or registered policy names as entitled to record or decide the disposition concerned — the finalizing authority, the scheduling authority, the authority the registered authoritative-commit policy names, or the registered study authority, as the surface concerned provides. |
| Affected entities | Exactly one owning unit or separately identified output, named by identity, together with the planned-run and study lineage above it. |
| Required source evidence | The incident, refusal, rejection, race-resolution, validation, replay, finalization, or seal-verification records on which the disposition rests, named by identity. |
| Governing policy identity and version | The registered policy identity and version under which the disposition was decided, where one applies, with the absence stated where none does. |
| Applicable time-domain facts | The time-domain facts Section 11 makes applicable. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never rewritten in place; corrected under WP2-FAIL-013 and WP2-FAIL-014. |
| Effect on lifecycle state, evidence preservation, authoritative acceptance, replacement eligibility, aggregate inclusion, and study validity | Exactly what it states under the registered policy it names, or, where none applies, under the absence it states: the unit disposition record is the explicit disposition record through which WP2-FAIL-009 requires a consequence to be recorded. A lifecycle value changes only through the transitions Sections 8 and 9 permit, recorded by the entitled authorities at the boundaries WP2-LIFE-034 requires, and no disposition erases evidence or history. |

**Exclusion decision record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every exclusion decision the laboratory records — the decision, under a registered exclusion rule, that one identified unit or its evidence does not count toward its study's registered result, recorded in the study-validity dimension Section 8.4 keeps separate. |
| Recording or deciding authority | The registered study authority the applicable registered exclusion rule names. |
| Affected entities | The excluded unit or evidence, named by identity, and the study result surface affected. |
| Required source evidence | The records establishing the facts on which the registered exclusion criteria were met, named by identity. |
| Governing policy identity and version | The preregistered exclusion rule identity and version. An exclusion decided under no registered rule is not a record of this entry; the attempted exclusion is recorded as a failure or integrity incident of this register, naming the absence of any applicable registered exclusion rule in the discipline WP2-MODEL-021 states for its own records, classified under the unauthorized-operation limb of the assignment-authority, stale-lease, or unauthorized-operation failure class of Section 14.4, an operation attempted without the authority a registered exclusion rule would supply, together with any further class of that table that in fact applies to it, and it excludes nothing. |
| Applicable time-domain facts | The time-domain facts Section 11 makes applicable. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never rewritten in place; corrected under WP2-FAIL-013 and WP2-FAIL-014. |
| Effect on lifecycle state, evidence preservation, authoritative acceptance, replacement eligibility, aggregate inclusion, and study validity | The unit's study-disposition value, recorded as Sections 8.5 and 8.6 provide, and nothing else by itself. The excluded unit, its identity, and its evidence remain preserved under WP2-LIFE-012, and its denominator, missingness, and reporting treatment belongs to Section 15. |

**Retry, recovery, and resume decision record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every recorded decision that one of the repetition, recovery, or re-entry operations WP2-FAIL-016 distinguishes proceed — that a further provider attempt proceed under a registered retry or fallback policy; that a transport or delivery retry proceed as the authorized presentation disposition WP2-MODEL-055 states; that recovery or completion of already-produced evidence and packaging work proceed; or that a study or planned-run workflow be re-entered under exact identity and provenance verification — each decision separately identified and naming which operation it decides. The admission of a new execution attempt is decided and recorded as Sections 8 and 9 state and as Section 14.8 requires, not by a record of this entry. |
| Recording or deciding authority | The role holding the deciding authority the applicable registered policy names. |
| Affected entities | The logical model request, provider attempt, execution attempt, planned run, study, or evidence concerned, named by identity, naming each of those that applies and stating the absence of any that does not. |
| Required source evidence | The interruption, incident, refusal, or verification records on which the decision rests, and, for a re-entry, the exact identity and provenance verification WP2-ISO-029 requires, named by identity. |
| Governing policy identity and version | The registered retry, fallback, presentation-disposition, recovery, or continuation policy identity and version. |
| Applicable time-domain facts | The time-domain facts Section 11 makes applicable. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never rewritten in place; corrected under WP2-FAIL-013 and WP2-FAIL-014. |
| Effect on lifecycle state, evidence preservation, authoritative acceptance, replacement eligibility, aggregate inclusion, and study validity | None on any terminal disposition already recorded, and the decision itself creates or changes no execution attempt, no planned run, and no authoritative outcome. What a recovery so decided may handle is bounded by WP2-FAIL-020 and WP2-FAIL-021, and the decision has no effect by itself on evidence preservation, replacement eligibility, aggregate inclusion, or study validity. |

**Replacement-eligibility and replacement-authorization decision record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every replacement-eligibility determination and every replacement-authorization decision the laboratory records, the two remaining distinct records under WP2-FAIL-036, each separately identified. |
| Recording or deciding authority | The authority the preregistered replacement policy names for the decision concerned. |
| Affected entities | The planned run and the specific predecessor execution attempt concerned — named explicitly, in the discipline WP2-ID-011 and WP2-ID-012 state — and, for an authorization, no execution-attempt identity, run-evidence-root identity, or replacement-lineage record for the replacement it authorizes, that absence stated in the record because none of the three exists before the all-or-none admission that creates them, as WP2-FAIL-045 states. |
| Required source evidence | The typed class and disposition records of the predecessor execution attempt on which eligibility rests, named by identity; the behavioral favorability of any trajectory is never among the grounds, as WP2-FAIL-035 states. |
| Governing policy identity and version | The preregistered replacement policy identity and version, in the sense Section 3.1 gives *registered replacement* and consistent with charter Section 14.4. |
| Applicable time-domain facts | The time-domain facts Section 11 makes applicable. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never rewritten in place; corrected under WP2-FAIL-013 and WP2-FAIL-014. |
| Effect on lifecycle state, evidence preservation, authoritative acceptance, replacement eligibility, aggregate inclusion, and study validity | Eligibility establishes eligibility alone, and authorization authorizes admission alone, the admission that relies on an authorization naming it explicitly, as WP2-FAIL-045 states; admission, execution, and later authoritative acceptance remain the further distinct decisions WP2-FAIL-036 keeps separate. No predecessor execution attempt's record or evidence changes, as WP2-LIFE-026 keeps it, and sample-unit and denominator treatment belongs to Section 15. |

**Study-invalidity decision record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every study-invalidity decision the laboratory records, each deciding validity for the registered claim of exactly one study. |
| Recording or deciding authority | The registered authority entitled to decide the study's validity for its registered claim under the study's registered validity rules. |
| Affected entities | The one study concerned and its affected claim boundary. Each unit below the study keeps its own recorded dispositions, which the decision does not rewrite. |
| Required source evidence | The exact evidence supporting the decision, named by identity, as WP2-FAIL-042 requires. |
| Governing policy identity and version | The registered validity rule identity and version supporting the decision, as WP2-FAIL-042 requires. |
| Applicable time-domain facts | The time-domain facts Section 11 makes applicable. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never rewritten in place; corrected under WP2-FAIL-013 and WP2-FAIL-014. |
| Effect on lifecycle state, evidence preservation, authoritative acceptance, replacement eligibility, aggregate inclusion, and study validity | The study-validity surface alone. Whether execution may continue for diagnostic or exploratory purposes is stated in the decision itself, as WP2-FAIL-042 requires; no deletion, erasure, or favorable rerunning is authorized, as WP2-FAIL-044 states; and the completion and aggregation consequences belong to Section 15. |

### 14.3 Cause, class, consequence, and disposition kept separate

**WP2-FAIL-002.** For every incident, cancellation, refusal, rejection, deviation, exclusion, invalidity, or observed outcome a conforming laboratory records under this section, the cause, the observed class, the lifecycle consequence, the evidence consequence, and the research disposition **MUST** remain five separately recorded facts, each readable from a record that identifies which of the five it states.

**WP2-FAIL-003.** A conforming laboratory **MUST NOT** collapse two or more of those five facts into one generic failure code, one combined status word, or one mutable scalar value.

**WP2-FAIL-004.** Where one event gives rise to records of more than one register type or more than one class, each such record **MUST** name the shared triggering event or incident identity explicitly, so that the linkage among the records is read from the records themselves rather than reconstructed from naming, ordering, storage location, or timing.

**Rationale.** These three requirements are the disposition counterpart of WP2-LIFE-003 and WP2-LIFE-004: the five facts vary independently, and a single value that answers all five at once cannot represent an operationally failed attempt whose evidence is valid, a cleanly completed attempt excluded by a registered rule, or a cancelled attempt whose planned run remains schedulable. Charter Section 14.4 requires operational failure, artifact invalidity, study invalidity, and behaviorally unfavorable outcomes to remain distinct, and Section 5.10 records that the repository already keeps a pipeline verdict and a scientific verdict as two separately recorded values on one execution record — evidence that the separation is representable, not a requirement, as WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps it. WP2-FAIL-004 applies to linked disposition records the join discipline WP2-ID-023 and WP2-MODEL-023 state for lineage and request records, so that one event with several categories stays one auditable event rather than several coincidences.

### 14.4 The failure-class taxonomy

The taxonomy below names the classes this contract keeps distinct. A laboratory records what happened under the applicable class or classes; what follows from a classification is governed by Sections 14.3 and 14.5 through 14.9 and by the identified registered policies. The table states, for each class, what it records and what it does not by itself establish; WP2-FAIL-005 makes its clauses binding.

| Class | What it records | What it does not by itself establish |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Operational failure | A unit of work that failed for an operational reason inside the laboratory's own execution — a crash, an interruption, a resource loss, a stalled or stopped process, or a comparable operational cause. | Artifact invalidity, study invalidity, exclusion, replacement eligibility, or any disposition of a sibling unit. |
| Provider or transport failure | A failed, rejected, timed-out, or undeliverable interaction with a provider or a transport path, recorded against the local dispatch attempt or provider attempt concerned. | A new cognitive decision or logical model request, a treatment change, the exhaustion of a budget scope, or the failure of the execution attempt the request serves. |
| Assignment-authority, stale-lease, or unauthorized-operation failure | An operation attempted under an assignment-authority grant or lease that was not current, or without demonstrable authority or owning lineage — the conditions WP2-ISO-030, WP2-SCHED-021, and WP2-LIFE-029 govern. | The invalidity of evidence produced earlier under then-current authority, or the terminal disposition of the unit affected. |
| Identity, namespace, or root collision | A duplicate identity, a namespace collision, or an existing-root collision in the WP2-ISO-026 sense, met by the refusal WP2-ISO-030 requires. | Which colliding party owns the contested identity or location, or any unit's terminal disposition. |
| Request attribution or idempotency failure | A record that cannot be attributed as WP2-MODEL-019 requires, an identity/content conflict refused under WP2-MODEL-026, or a presentation that cannot receive the disposition WP2-MODEL-055 requires. | The failure of the deciding episode, of the execution attempt, or of the study the request serves. |
| Budget exhaustion, refusal, or unresolved exposure | A typed budget refusal under WP2-MODEL-043, or exposure that remains unresolved or disputed under WP2-MODEL-038 and WP2-MODEL-046. | A settled usage value, the failure of the affected unit, or authority to exceed, transfer, or reallocate any registered budget scope. |
