| Timing-integrity failure | An impossible, contradictory, or unattributable timing record of the kinds WP2-TIME-017 through WP2-TIME-019 require to be surfaced and preserved. | The incorrectness of any other timing record, or the disposition of the unit whose timing records are affected. |
| Concurrency-profile or provider-behavior divergence | A divergence between registered and observed execution recorded under WP2-SCHED-042, or between a study's registered provider expectations and observed provider behavior recorded under WP2-MODEL-052, its materiality fixed as WP2-FAIL-010 requires. | Any amendment of the registered design, which WP2-SCHED-043 and WP2-MODEL-052 prohibit, or the invalidity of the units executed. |
| Artifact invalidity | Evidence determined invalid against the applicable evidence rules, recorded in the evidence-validity dimension Section 8.4 keeps separate. | Study invalidity, exclusion, operational failure, or anything about the behavioral quality of the outcome. |
| Replay failure | A mismatch, an impossible reconstruction, a missing input, or a contradictory result of a replay verification, as Section 13 states replay. | The invalidity of evidence outside the replay boundary concerned, or any successful finalization. |
| Finalization failure | A finalization that failed under the Section 3.1 definition — a contradiction among present applicable sources, a missing required source, an unresolved required validation, or a required replay failure among its causes — recorded through the Finalization failed result the Section 8.6 model states. | The authoritative acceptance of any competing execution attempt, replacement eligibility, or study invalidity. |
| Packaging, inventory, seal, or seal-verification failure | A failed inventory or completeness verdict, a failed seal or package operation, a failed seal verification, or a post-seal mutation, omission, substitution, or digest mismatch, as Section 13 states those operations. | The loss or invalidity of the underlying evidence, or the disposition of the unit whose evidence was being sealed. |
| Secret-boundary or detected-secret incident | A detected-secret refusal and its typed nonsecret incident under WP2-SEC-013 and WP2-SEC-014, a secret-boundary violation under Section 16, or live secret material discovered in a record already persisted or admitted as evidence — the case whose incident typing and remediation disposition Section 16.10 assigns here and whose evidentiary treatment Section 13 states. | A clean seal, a clean aggregation, or an authoritative acceptance over affected material; deletion authority; or the invalidity of unaffected records. |
| Cancellation | A cancellation issued at one of the three scopes WP2-LIFE-027 states, recorded through the cancellation incident record of Section 14.2. | The erasure of anything already produced, the cancellation of any sibling unit, or the invalidity of the study. |
| Exclusion | The exclusion of one unit or its evidence from its study's registered result under a registered exclusion rule, recorded through the exclusion decision record of Section 14.2. | Operational failure, artifact invalidity, erasure of the excluded unit, or removal from any registered denominator, whose treatment Section 15 states. |
| Study invalidity | The explicit decision that one study is invalid for its registered claim, recorded through the study-invalidity decision record of Section 14.2. | The operational failure of any unit, deletion authority, or any authorization of favorable rerunning, which WP2-FAIL-044 prohibits. |
| Behaviorally unfavorable, repetitive, incoherent, or otherwise poor outcome | An observed research outcome of the registered design, recorded as WP2-FAIL-006 requires. | An operational failure, an artifact invalidity, an eligibility for replacement, or an authorization to rerun, as WP2-FAIL-007 states. |

**WP2-FAIL-005.** Every record of the register in Section 14.2 that classifies a failure, incident, deviation, refusal, rejection, cancellation, exclusion, invalidity, or observed outcome **MUST** classify it under the failure-class taxonomy table in Section 14.4, conforming to every clause of that table — its introducing statements and, for each applicable class entry, the class named, what the class records, and what it does not by itself establish — naming each applicable class separately where more than one applies rather than merging, substituting, or generalizing them, and treating no two classes of that table as interchangeable, except only the specific question or clause that an entry expressly assigns to a later section, an exemption that reaches no other clause of that entry.

**WP2-FAIL-006.** An outcome of the behaviorally unfavorable, repetitive, incoherent, or otherwise poor class **MUST** be recorded as an observed research outcome of the unit that produced it, attributed and preserved as evidence in the same way as any other observed outcome of the registered design.

**WP2-FAIL-007.** A conforming laboratory **MUST NOT** record, report, or treat an outcome of that class as, by itself, an operational failure, an artifact invalidity, an eligibility for replacement, or an authorization to rerun, retry, or replace any unit.

**WP2-FAIL-008.** A conforming laboratory **MUST NOT** derive from the recording of one failure class, by itself, membership in another class, a study invalidity, an exclusion, a replacement eligibility, an aggregate omission, or any other lifecycle, evidence, acceptance, or reporting consequence.

**WP2-FAIL-009.** Every lifecycle, evidence-preservation, authoritative-acceptance, replacement-eligibility, aggregate-inclusion, or study-validity consequence that a conforming laboratory draws from a classified failure, incident, deviation, cancellation, or outcome **MUST** be recorded through an explicit disposition record of the register in Section 14.2 that records that consequence and names the registered policy applicable to it by identity and version where one applies, stating the absence where none does, and never derived from the classification alone, the obligations the approved sections of this specification impose of themselves continuing to hold without any registered policy.

**WP2-FAIL-010.** What makes a provider, route, model, concurrency, timing, budget, or evidence divergence material **MUST** be fixed by a registered policy whose identity and version every record classifying such a divergence as material or immaterial identifies, the materiality question WP2-SCHED-042 and Section 10.13 leave to this section being answered by that registered policy rather than by this specification.

**WP2-FAIL-011.** A materiality rule, and any interpretation of one, **MUST NOT** be adopted, changed, or applied retroactively after observing whether the affected trajectory or result was favorable.

**Rationale.** The taxonomy is a floor rather than a ceiling — a registered design may distinguish finer classes, and Section 14.10 records the realization as unselected — and its seventeenth class is the one a concurrent laboratory is most tempted to misuse. Charter Section 14.4 requires behaviorally unfavorable outcomes to remain distinct from operational failure, artifact invalidity, and study invalidity, and prohibits retrying an attempt merely to obtain a preferred trajectory; WP2-FAIL-006, WP2-FAIL-007, and WP2-FAIL-035 carry that rule into the record model, and WP2-FAIL-011 closes the quieter route to the same abuse, in which a materiality judgment is invented after the outcome is known so that an unfavorable unit becomes disposable. Section 5.10 records that the current taxonomy has no name for a transport or delivery retry, a laboratory-worker restart, a cancellation, a lease expiry, stale laboratory-worker output, containment of sibling units, or a partially dispositioned wave; the classes above supply those names — the provider or transport, assignment-authority, cancellation, and operational classes among them — without adopting any current class set, and the refusal conditions WP2-ISO-030 states classify under the collision, attribution, and authority classes without WP2-ISO-030 itself being revised. WP2-FAIL-008 and WP2-FAIL-009 keep classification descriptive and consequence explicit, which is what lets an auditor distinguish what happened from what a registered policy decided about it.

### 14.5 Preservation and correction

**WP2-FAIL-012.** Every failed, interrupted, invalid, excluded, cancelled, late, losing, superseded, replacement, and non-authoritative path taken by any unit or record this specification governs **MUST** remain recorded and attributable, with the original event, incident, execution attempt, evidence root, and disposition history preserved.

**WP2-FAIL-013.** A later correction or reclassification of a recorded incident, class, consequence, or disposition **MUST** be recorded as a new identified record carrying explicit predecessor or corrected-record lineage, in the discipline WP2-LIFE-017, WP2-ID-025, and WP2-ID-026 state.

**WP2-FAIL-014.** A correction or reclassification **MUST NOT** rewrite the original event, the recorded lifecycle history, the evidence, or the prior disposition in place.

**WP2-FAIL-015.** Failure handling **MUST NOT** erase sibling evidence, terminate an isolation unit unrelated to the failure, merge the budget or evidence lineage of any two isolation units, or cause one unit's result to be adopted by another, consistent with WP2-ISO-023 and WP2-ISO-024.

**Rationale.** Charter Section 14.4 requires failed, interrupted, invalid, excluded, and replacement attempts to remain recorded evidence with typed dispositions, and charter Section 14.9 requires evidence loss, mutation, collision, adoption, or incomplete finalization to be treated as an explicit failure rather than concealed. WP2-FAIL-012 states the preservation obligation once, across every losing path this contract names, so that no disposition path becomes the quiet exception; WP2-FAIL-015 answers the sibling-containment question Section 8.16 assigns here, extending to failure handling in general the confinement WP2-ISO-023 and WP2-ISO-024 state for cancellation and cleanup. The correction discipline is the one already established for identities, lifecycle records, and authority events: append and supersede, never rewrite, so that a reader who relied on the earlier record can see both the error and its correction.

### 14.6 Retry, restart, recovery, and resume distinctions

**WP2-FAIL-016.** A conforming laboratory **MUST** record, report, and disposition each of the following seven operations as a distinct operation, extending the discipline WP2-SCOPE-006 states, none of them recorded, reported, counted, or dispositioned as another or as interchangeable with another: a duplicate delivery or transport retry of one logical model request; a further provider attempt under a registered retry or fallback policy; a process restart, an orchestrator restart among them; recovery or completion of already-produced evidence and packaging work; re-entry into a study or planned-run workflow under exact identity and provenance verification, which is a resume in the Section 3.1 sense; the admission of a new execution attempt for the same planned run; and a registered replacement execution attempt.

**WP2-FAIL-017.** A process restart, an orchestrator restart among them, **MUST NOT** be recorded, reported, or dispositioned as creating a new logical model request, a new planned run, a new execution attempt, or a registered replacement, or as changing any unit's recorded status or disposition by itself, consistent with WP2-LIFE-018.

**WP2-FAIL-018.** A transport or delivery retry **MUST NOT** be recorded, reported, or dispositioned as a new cognitive decision or a new logical model request, the retained request identity WP2-MODEL-027 requires and the new-identity rule WP2-MODEL-029 states settling which of the two any repetition is.

**WP2-FAIL-019.** A further provider attempt undertaken under a registered retry or fallback policy **MUST** be dispositioned as a distinct provider attempt with its own provider-attempt identity serving the same logical model request, recorded distinguishably from a transport or delivery retry, as WP2-MODEL-028 requires.

**WP2-FAIL-020.** A recovery or completion operation over already-produced evidence — finalization, inventory, sealing, verification, or packaging work among its permitted forms — **MUST** proceed only under the exact verified identity and provenance of the evidence and unit concerned, as WP2-ISO-029 requires and as WP2-LIFE-019 requires of reconstruction after an orchestrator restart, and only where the authority under which it acts remains valid for that operation.

**WP2-FAIL-021.** Interrupted simulation execution **MUST NOT** be continued in place under the same execution-attempt identity by a recovery, a re-entry, a process restart, or any other operation of this section — further simulation execution for the same planned run proceeding only as a newly admitted execution attempt under WP2-LIFE-024 and WP2-SCHED-024 and the registered policies they name, consistent with WP2-ISO-005, WP2-LIFE-020, and WP2-LIFE-021.

**Rationale.** Section 3.1 defines a resume as re-entering an existing, identity-matched unit of work after an interruption, without continuing an interrupted execution attempt in place and without erasing what that attempt produced. Consistent with the identity and isolation rules of Sections 6 through 8, no operation of this section resumes an execution attempt: what can lawfully happen under the same execution-attempt identity after an interruption is identity-verified handling of evidence that attempt already produced — the recovery and completion operations WP2-FAIL-020 bounds — together with the operations that do not continue the interrupted simulated world in place, and any further simulation execution is a newly admitted execution attempt with its own identity and run evidence root. The seven-way distinction exists because each operation answers to a different authority and produces different records — a transport retry is a transport fact under WP2-MODEL-027, a provider retry spends budget under WP2-MODEL-028 and Section 12.8, a restart changes nothing under WP2-LIFE-018, a recovery touches only evidence that already exists, a resume re-enters a workflow, and an admission or replacement creates a new unit — and a laboratory that conflates any two of them miscounts exactly the events a study's interpretation depends on.

### 14.7 Cancellation dispositions

**WP2-FAIL-022.** A cancellation **MUST** prevent further release, execution activity, provider dispatch, finalization, authoritative acceptance, and aggregation operations under the cancelled authority, as the approved sections applicable to each such operation require — WP2-MODEL-044 for provider dispatches among them — from the point at which the cancellation becomes current under the applicable authority boundary.

**WP2-FAIL-023.** A cancellation **MUST NOT** erase, truncate, or render unverifiable any evidence, accounting record, or exposure representation already produced, consistent with WP2-ISO-024 and the preservation half of WP2-MODEL-044.

**WP2-FAIL-024.** In-flight provider exposure, late results, and losing operations affected by a cancellation **MUST** receive typed, preserved dispositions under the register in Section 14.2, in the preservation discipline WP2-MODEL-032, WP2-MODEL-046, and WP2-SCHED-029 state.

**WP2-FAIL-025.** A cancellation **MUST NOT** cancel, clean up, or disposition a sibling or unrelated isolation unit, or any unit that its explicitly recorded lineage does not place below the scope at which it was issued, consistent with WP2-ISO-023 and WP2-LIFE-028.

**WP2-FAIL-026.** Every execution attempt that a cancellation makes terminal, an admitted attempt cancelled before any execution activity among them, **MUST** receive the terminal evidence closure Section 13 requires of every terminal execution attempt, together with the run-level inventory, sealing, and seal-verification treatment Section 13.9 states over its actual closed run-evidence-root boundary — a root whose execution-produced evidence subset is empty among the boundaries so covered — that treatment producing for the attempt a current full-root inventory of that boundary and exactly one current recorded sealing outcome: either a run-level seal over that inventory with a preserved seal-verification verdict, or a typed, preserved sealing or integrity failure or blocker record — a record of the packaging, inventory, seal, or seal-verification failure class the Section 14.4 taxonomy states, recording the failed or unperformable inventory, seal, or seal-verification operation and the condition that blocked it — stating why a clean seal or verification could not be produced, the registered evidence contract applicable to the attempt governing the rules and representations applied while never rendering the full-root inventory or the sealing outcome inapplicable, and the recorded outcome remaining visible to the evidence-closure-and-integrity dimension of Section 15, whose reached result WP2-AGG-018 governs.

**WP2-FAIL-027.** The disposition of each operation defeated in a race between a cancellation and an authoritative acceptance, or between a cancellation and another authority operation, **MUST** record the resolution decided under the registered, versioned authority policy and at the recorded authority boundary the approved sections require — WP2-SCHED-031 through WP2-SCHED-033 for the acceptance race — and never a resolution taken from wall-clock arrival order.

**Rationale.** Cancellation is an authority event, not an evidence event: it removes the entitlement to continue and licenses nothing about what already exists, which is the same separation WP2-ISO-020 and WP2-SCHED-021 state for lapsed authority. WP2-LIFE-027 and WP2-LIFE-028 already make the scope of a cancellation a recorded fact; what this section adds is the disposition side — every affected in-flight exposure, late result, and losing operation ends in a typed record rather than in silence, and every attempt the cancellation makes terminal closes its evidence boundary honestly under Section 13, over whatever it in fact produced, including nothing. The same refusal of silence reaches sealing: under WP2-FAIL-026 the sealing outcome of a cancelled attempt is itself a recorded fact — a current full-root inventory and exactly one current recorded sealing outcome, a run-level seal over that inventory with a preserved seal-verification verdict or a typed, preserved failure or blocker record stating why a clean seal or verification could not be produced — because a registered evidence contract able to render the full-root inventory or the sealing outcome inapplicable would leave an omission standing where a sealing result belongs, and the recorded outcome stays visible to the study's evidence-closure-and-integrity dimension, whose reached result WP2-AGG-018 governs. Section 5.9 records that the repository's current per-execution seal already authenticates an explicit empty set for an interruption that preceded any evidence creation, which is evidence that the empty-set closure is representable, not a requirement, as WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps it. Which of a cancellation and an acceptance prevails stays a registered-policy question under WP2-SCHED-031, and Section 9.12 records that neither direction is preferred.

### 14.8 New attempts and registered replacements

**WP2-FAIL-028.** Every further execution attempt admitted for an existing planned run **MUST** be admitted as a new execution-attempt identity with its own run evidence root, consistent with WP2-SCHED-007 and, for a resumed planned run, WP2-LIFE-024, carried out under the same planned-run parent and under that planned run's exact registered condition and treatment, a different condition or treatment being executable only as a different planned run registered under its own planned-run identity in the registered design, never as a further execution attempt of the existing planned run.

**WP2-FAIL-029.** The admission decision for every further execution attempt **MUST** be recorded with the reason the attempt was admitted, the identity and version of the registered policy that permitted it, and the authority that admitted it.

**WP2-FAIL-030.** The admission of a further execution attempt **MUST NOT** change the recorded status, terminal disposition, or evidence of any earlier execution attempt of the same planned run, consistent with WP2-LIFE-026, the append-only relation records WP2-LIFE-032 governs adding to that attempt's lineage-relation collection without changing any of the three.

**WP2-FAIL-031.** A further execution attempt, a registered replacement among them, **MUST NOT** be recorded, reported, or counted as a second authoritative outcome of its planned run, as a new planned run, as a new independent run, or as a new registered sample unit of its study — the sample-size treatment Section 8.16 assigns here being that every further attempt remains an additional physical execution of its existing planned run, counted in the denominators Section 15 states.

**WP2-FAIL-032.** A registered replacement **MUST** be admitted only under a preregistered replacement policy, as charter Section 14.4 requires of every replacement and as Section 3.1 gives *registered replacement*, with its eligibility and authorization records naming that policy's identity and version and the typed reason and eligibility decision on which the authorization rests, and its authorization record additionally identifying the proposed admission facts WP2-FAIL-045 requires of it.

**WP2-FAIL-033.** The replacement-authorization decision record and the replacement lineage it authorizes **MUST** name explicitly the specific execution attempt being replaced, in the discipline WP2-ID-011 and WP2-ID-012 state and through the replacement-lineage entity the Section 6.4 register states.

**WP2-FAIL-034.** A registered replacement **MUST NOT** erase, overwrite, renumber, rename, or conceal the execution attempt it replaces or any evidence that attempt produced, consistent with WP2-ID-010 and WP2-LIFE-026.

**WP2-FAIL-035.** A further execution attempt or registered replacement **MUST NOT** be admitted, authorized, or made eligible on the ground that an earlier attempt's simulated trajectory or observed outcome was behaviorally unfavorable, repetitive, incoherent, or otherwise poor, consistent with charter Section 14.4's prohibition on retrying an attempt merely to obtain a preferred trajectory.

**WP2-FAIL-036.** Replacement eligibility, replacement authorization, replacement admission, replacement execution, and the later authoritative acceptance of any execution attempt's result **MUST** remain five distinct decisions with five distinct records, none of them standing in for, implying, or being inferred from another, the lineage between authorization and admission running in one direction only — the admission naming the exact authorization record on which it relies, and no authorization naming or creating the execution-attempt identity of the replacement it authorizes, as WP2-FAIL-045 states.

**WP2-FAIL-045.** Every replacement-authorization decision record **MUST** identify its own authorization identity, the planned run concerned, the specific predecessor execution attempt being replaced, the preregistered replacement policy identity and version under which it was decided, and the proposed admission facts on which the later admission decision is to rest, while creating and naming no execution-attempt identity, no run-evidence-root identity, and no replacement-lineage record for the replacement it authorizes, the predecessor execution attempt it names being one already admitted, whether or not that predecessor was itself a registered replacement — the later replacement admission, decided at the all-or-none admission boundary WP2-SCHED-013 states, naming the exact authorization record on which it relies and atomically creating the new execution-attempt identity, its run evidence root, and the replacement-lineage record of the Section 6.4 register.

**Rationale.** The identity half of this contract is already established: WP2-ID-010 through WP2-ID-012 make a registered replacement an additional execution attempt of the same planned run with explicit lineage, the Section 6.4 replacement-lineage entity carries that relation, WP2-LIFE-026 keeps the replaced attempt's own record untouched, and WP2-ID-009 bounds the planned run at one authoritative accepted outcome at most. What this section adds is the decision structure around it: why the attempt was admitted is a recorded fact rather than an inference, the five decisions from eligibility to acceptance stay separate so that none of them silently confers another — an eligible attempt is not authorized, an authorized attempt is not admitted, and an executed replacement is not accepted, each step remaining its own recorded decision under its own authority — and no step is ever grounded in the behavioral favorability of what came before, which is the exact rule that keeps a replacement from becoming a rerun-until-favorable mechanism. The authorization-to-admission lineage runs in one direction: an authorization identifies its own authorization identity and the proposed admission facts while creating and naming no execution-attempt identity, no run-evidence-root identity, and no replacement-lineage record for the replacement it authorizes — that replacement attempt is not yet admitted and cannot be named, while the predecessor it does name is already admitted, whether or not that predecessor was itself a registered replacement — and the admission, at the all-or-none admission boundary WP2-SCHED-013 states, atomically creates all three and names the exact authorization on which it relies, so that the lineage is read from the admission backward rather than promised by the authorization forward, as WP2-FAIL-045 states. WP2-FAIL-033's phrase *the replacement lineage it authorizes* describes the lineage record the later admission creates, not a record the authorization names or brings into existence, as WP2-FAIL-045 states. Condition invariance follows from the Section 6 identity model: one planned run is registered under exactly one condition and treatment, and every execution attempt is a physical realization of that same planned unit, so a different condition is executable only as a different planned run registered under its own planned-run identity. Because every execution attempt realizes the same registered planned run, a further attempt under another condition would instead be a different planned run with its own identity; no exception to that identity rule is inferred from the preregistered design. How many attempts or replacements a design permits is that design's registered decision: no retry count, replacement count, or eligibility threshold is selected here, WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting one, and Section 14.10 records the choice as unselected.

### 14.9 Failure propagation and study invalidity

**WP2-FAIL-037.** The failure of one execution attempt **MUST NOT** be recorded, propagated, or reported as, by itself, the failure or invalidity of its planned run, of its study, or of any sibling unit.

**WP2-FAIL-038.** The failure of one planned run **MUST NOT** be recorded, propagated, or reported as, by itself, establishing the invalidity of its study.

**WP2-FAIL-039.** Study invalidity **MUST** be recorded only as the explicit, versioned, evidence-linked study-invalidity decision of the register in Section 14.2, decided by the registered authority that entry names.

**WP2-FAIL-040.** A correlated provider, infrastructure, secret, or evidence failure **MUST** be contained to the units it in fact affected, with every shared window, dependency, or common cause recorded — through the provider-behavior observations WP2-MODEL-049 and WP2-MODEL-050 require and the divergence records WP2-SCHED-042 requires, where those apply — rather than propagated to units related to the failure only by shared infrastructure.

**WP2-FAIL-041.** Membership of a unit in the affected set of a correlated failure **MUST NOT** be inferred from temporal proximity, wall-clock adjacency, or shared infrastructure alone, consistent with WP2-ISO-014 and WP2-MODEL-018.

**WP2-FAIL-042.** Every study-invalidity decision record **MUST** identify the exact evidence and the registered rule identity and version supporting the decision, the affected claim boundary, and whether execution under the study may continue for diagnostic or exploratory purposes.

**WP2-FAIL-043.** An invalid study **MUST** remain represented and auditable, with its membership, dispositions, and evidence preserved, and with any closure it receives recorded as a further record rather than as a rewriting of what preceded it.

**WP2-FAIL-044.** A study-invalidity decision **MUST NOT** authorize the deletion of evidence, the erasure of history, or the admission of further execution attempts in order to obtain a more favorable result.

**Rationale.** Propagation is where a concurrent laboratory quietly manufactures conclusions: a shared provider outage that fails one attempt is evidence about that attempt, and treating it as evidence about every unit that ran the same hour would decide affected membership by wall-clock adjacency, which WP2-ISO-014 prohibits as an implicit join and WP2-MODEL-018 prohibits for request records. The correlated-failure record therefore names the units the failure in fact reached and the shared window or dependency it reached them through, and everything beyond that set stays undisturbed. In the other direction, invalidity is a decision rather than an accumulation — no count of failed attempts or planned runs establishes it, and only the registered authority's explicit, versioned, evidence-linked decision records it — which keeps a study from drifting into invalidity by inference or being talked into it after an unfavorable result. An invalid study remains a study: its record answers what was registered, what ran, what failed, and why the claim cannot be made, and charter Section 14.9's preservation discipline reaches it exactly as it reaches a valid one. What invalidity does to completion, denominators, and any aggregate surface is stated by Section 15.

### 14.10 Options deliberately left unselected

**Unselected option.** Taxonomy realization. A laboratory may realize the failure-class taxonomy as a closed enumeration, a hierarchical code set, a tagged record structure, or another representation, and a registered design may distinguish finer classes within any class of Section 14.4, provided every record remains classifiable under the classes stated there and WP2-FAIL-005 is satisfied. No error-code set, enumeration, schema, or encoding is selected.

**Unselected option.** Disposition-record placement. The records of the register in Section 14.2 may be carried on the entities they disposition, in separate disposition records, in an event stream, in an index, or in more than one of those, under any encoding. WP2-FAIL-001 and WP2-FAIL-004 state the record-model and linkage properties; no store, schema, or placement is selected, and Section 13 rather than this section states how these records are treated as evidence.

**Unselected option.** Incident-linkage representation. The explicit linkage WP2-FAIL-004 requires among the records of one event may be realized by a shared incident identity carried on each record, by a separate linking record, by an index, or by another arrangement readable from the record. No join representation is selected.

**Unselected option.** Retry, replacement, and eligibility parameters. How many further provider attempts a registered retry or fallback policy permits, how many further execution attempts or registered replacements a preregistered replacement policy permits, and every eligibility criterion and threshold are the registered design's decisions, carried by the policy identities and versions this section requires to be named. This specification selects no retry count, replacement count, eligibility threshold, or capacity, and WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting one.

**Unselected option.** Materiality-rule contents. Which divergences a registered materiality policy declares material, and by what criteria, are that policy's contents. WP2-FAIL-010 and WP2-FAIL-011 state the properties any such policy has to satisfy — identified, versioned, and never applied retroactively after observing favorability — and no criterion, threshold, or comparison window is selected.

**Unselected option.** Detection and classification mechanism. What detects an incident and assigns its class — monitors, validators, verification passes, authority review, or another arrangement — is unselected, as Section 16.9 already records for secret detection specifically. No monitoring product, detector, classification algorithm, or review procedure is selected.

### 14.11 Boundaries assigned elsewhere

This section states which failure, cancellation, refusal, disposition, and replacement records exist, what each of them names, which classes keep what happened distinct from what was decided about it, and how repetition, interruption, cancellation, replacement, and propagation are dispositioned without erasing anything. It deliberately defines none of the following, each of which the staged instructions assign elsewhere: the evidence format, retention, and sealing treatment of the records this section requires, which Section 13 states; the effect of failures, cancellations, exclusions, invalidity decisions, and replacement dispositions on study completion, denominators, missingness, and aggregate inclusion, which Section 15 states; the conformance checks, fault injections, and pass conditions for every requirement stated here, which belong to Section 17 and to the conformance document; and the implementation handoff and the ranking of the options this section leaves unselected, which belong to Section 18.

## 15. Study aggregation and completion

### 15.1 What this section establishes

This section defines how one study's membership, execution, evidence, accounting, analysis, validity, and aggregation reach recorded conclusions, and what a study-level derived result has to identify before it can be produced, corrected, or cited. It answers the questions the other sections expressly assign here: what makes a study aggregation complete, and what distinct record a study that produced no evidence receives, which the Section 6.7 study-aggregation entry, the Section 6.14 rationale, and Section 6.18 assign here; study-aggregation completion, which Section 7.21 assigns here; the rules that make a study complete, the treatment of undispositioned scheduled units, and the study-level consequences of study dispositions and of closure to further attempts, which the Section 8.4 through 8.6 model cells, Section 8.16, and Section 9.13 assign here; the effect of a recorded concurrency-profile deviation on study completeness and reporting, which Section 10.13 assigns here; the study-completion rules that fix a study's end boundary, which the Section 11.3 study-level elapsed-time entry and Section 11.13 assign here; the effect of refused, unresolved, or deviating requests and budgets on study completeness and reporting, which Section 12.11 assigns here; the completion and aggregation consequences of the evidence, finalization, sealing, and verification results of Section 13, which Section 13.14 assigns here; the completion and aggregation consequences of the failures, cancellations, exclusions, replacements, and study-invalidity decisions of Section 14, which Section 14.11 assigns here; and the consequences of a detected-secret incident, a refused admission, or a sanitized derivative for study completeness and for any aggregation surface, which Section 16.10 assigns here. Section 5.11 records that no general multi-sequence or multi-study aggregator exists in the repository — no aggregation identity, no ledger of source evidence roots, no partial-study lifecycle, no denominator or missingness model beyond one sequence, and no detection of scheduled units that remain undispositioned — and Section 5.14 item 9 carries that absence forward to this section. Those observations are evidence about the current system; this section states the future contract they leave unstated, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps the current mechanism from being read as any part of it.

Section 15.2 holds the completion-dimension model and the requirements that keep its seven dimensions separate. Section 15.3 holds the study-record register — the study-membership-and-completion ledger and the study aggregation record — and the requirements that fix the ledger's derivation and contents. Section 15.4 states the denominator and missingness requirements. Section 15.5 states the completion conditions. Sections 15.6 through 15.8 state the aggregation source-set, partial-record, and rebuild requirements. Section 15.9 states the zero-evidence-study requirements. Section 15.10 states the completion and claim boundaries. Section 15.11 records options deliberately left unselected, and Section 15.12 states the boundaries assigned elsewhere. This section selects no statistical method, inclusion threshold, missing-data method, aggregation algorithm, ledger or snapshot representation, store, or numeric parameter, and the selections WP2-SCOPE-015 enumerates remain prohibited throughout this document.

Every substantive statement here is a Required future invariant carrying exactly one `WP2-AGG-###` identifier, a Design rationale introduced by a **Rationale.** lead-in, or an Unselected option introduced by an **Unselected option.** lead-in, in the Section 4.3 sense, or else an entry of the completion-dimension model in Section 15.2, that model's introducing statements included, whose normative force flows through WP2-AGG-001 rather than through an identifier of its own, an entry of the study-record register in Section 15.3, its introducing statements included, whose normative force flows through WP2-AGG-007 rather than through an identifier of its own, an entry of the attribute table in Section 15.3, which states how that register is read and carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements that cite it, a definitional statement opening Section 15.6 or Section 15.7, each of which states how a term is used in this section and carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements that cite it, or a boundary statement in Section 15.12. Nothing in this section is a Current fact or a Current limitation; those belong to Section 5, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps a current mechanism from being read as a requirement. Every defined term used here carries the meaning Section 3.1 assigns it, as WP2-SCOPE-005 requires, and every lifecycle term carries the meaning Section 8.3 assigns it, as WP2-LIFE-002 requires — *Closed to further attempts* remains the planned-run operational value of Section 8.5 wherever it is named, and the closure of a completion dimension here, like the closure of an evidence boundary in Section 13, is ordinary description rather than a use of that value's name.

### 15.2 The seven completion dimensions

One word never carries a study's conclusion in this specification. Seven completion dimensions are kept separate for every study, and between them they answer separately what a single scalar "complete" would otherwise be asked to mean at once: whether the registered membership is closed, whether execution and disposition are closed, whether the evidence is closed and intact, whether the accounting is reconciled, whether the registered analysis can proceed, whether the study is valid for its registered claim, and whether a study-level derived result has in fact been produced. Each dimension holds, for one study, its own separately recorded result: when that study's study-membership-and-completion ledger is derived from its admitted registration, each of the seven dimensions receives an explicit initial unresolved result — an initialization record rather than a substantive determination of validity, readiness, integrity, or reconciliation — recorded as the dimension's initial result for the purposes of WP2-AGG-004; a later result is the reached, failed, or otherwise typed result recorded under the study's registered rules, superseding the record it names under WP2-AGG-004; the recording authority each entry below names being the authority for that dimension's later results, and what each entry below states an unresolved or failed result of its dimension to mean being what a later unresolved or failed result of it means, an initial unresolved result stating only that the dimension is not yet determined; the explicit initial unresolved result of every dimension being recorded by the laboratory authority answerable for study-level membership and completion record-keeping at the ledger's derivation, as WP2-AGG-004 states; and an absent dimension record is a missing or integrity-defective record, never a valid unresolved state. This section constrains when a reached or clean result may be recorded and selects no result encoding. A study is *operationally closed* where its registered-membership-closure result and its execution-and-disposition-closure result are both recorded as reached; the phrase is a reading convenience over the first two dimensions rather than an eighth dimension or a defined term of Section 3.1.

| Dimension | What it records | Recording authority | What its unresolved or failed result means |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Registered-membership closure** | Whether every registered planned run, every admitted execution attempt, every scheduled or admitted unit, and every replacement decision of the study is present in the study-membership-and-completion ledger by recorded identity, the closure binding one exact study-membership-and-completion ledger identity and its version or correction lineage, so that the study's membership is fixed against silent later addition and silent disappearance alike; its reached result exists only where every registered planned run has reached a status under the lifecycle models of Section 8 from which no further execution attempt may be admitted and the study's registered continuation and replacement rules permit no further admission under it, so that no further execution attempt or replacement can lawfully be admitted under that membership, as WP2-AGG-016 requires. | The laboratory authority answerable for study-level membership and completion record-keeping under the study's admitted registration. | The membership is not fixed: a registered or admitted unit is missing from the study-membership-and-completion ledger or unaccounted for, no dimension that reads that ledger can reach a clean result over an open membership, and the study remains validly represented while the result holds, in the pattern WP2-LIFE-005 states for a planned run. A post-closure admission attempt does not return the dimension to unresolved silently: it is refused and preserved, or proceeds only under a new registration or another registered-membership boundary identity or version change the governing research design expressly requires, the admission then belonging to that new membership boundary rather than to the closed one and its evidence reaching new inventory, seal, and lineage records as WP2-EVID-055 requires, never as a silent reopening of the closed membership, as WP2-AGG-016 states. |
| **Execution and disposition closure** | Whether every registered planned run, every admitted execution attempt, every scheduled or admitted unit, and every replacement decision of the study holds an explicitly recorded disposition — a terminal value in each applicable status dimension of Sections 8.5 and 8.6, or the recorded missingness or non-acceptance reason WP2-AGG-014 requires — under the models of Sections 8 and 9 and the disposition records Section 14 states. Its reached result is a separately identified record whose event identity can serve as the exact study-level end event the Section 11.3 study-level elapsed-time entry names, under WP2-TIME-012, where the study's registration or its study-level completion record — a record of the ledger of Section 15.3, distinct from the attempt completion record of Section 13.3 — identifies it as that end event. | The laboratory authority answerable for study-level membership and completion record-keeping, reading the recorded transitions and disposition records whose own recording authorities Sections 8, 9, and 14 name. | At least one registered or admitted unit remains undispositioned, the study is not operationally closed, and WP2-AGG-017 keeps the undispositioned unit visible rather than dropped. |
| **Evidence closure and integrity** | Whether every expected run evidence root and study-level record of the study is accounted for, every execution attempt of the study that reached a terminal operational value carries the evidence-closure result Section 13 requires of it, and every required inventory, run-level seal, study-level seal, sealed package, seal-verification, and attempt-integrity-basis result — the recorded sealing outcome WP2-FAIL-026 requires of every execution attempt a cancellation makes terminal and, for every execution attempt of the study for which WP2-EVID-051 requires one, the current attempt-integrity basis WP2-EVID-073 keeps race-safely decided among them — holds a current typed outcome under the study's registered evidence contract. | The laboratory authority answerable for study-level membership and completion record-keeping, reading the closure, inventory, sealing, packaging, verification, and attempt-integrity-basis results whose own recording authorities Section 13 names. | The study's evidence does not support a clean aggregate: WP2-AGG-024 prevents one over a source set with a non-verifying required seal, an omitted member the registered analysis requires, or an undispositioned affected-or-compromised marking, WP2-AGG-018 keeps this dimension from being recorded as reached while any expected root, closure result, or required inventory, seal, package, verification, sealing, or attempt-integrity-basis outcome is missing, and a study-level derived result remains producible, where the registered rules permit one at all, only as the explicitly labeled partial record of Section 15.7. |
| **Budget or accounting reconciliation** | Whether every budget authorization, incurred usage, settlement, release, and exposure of the study under the Section 12.8 contract holds a reconciled recorded disposition, with every unresolved or disputed exposure represented honestly under WP2-MODEL-046. A registered completion policy may state which unresolved accounting states block which completion or reporting surface. | The applicable reconciling authority under the study's registered budget policies, as the Section 12.3 settlement-or-reconciliation entry names it. | Unresolved or disputed exposure remains, WP2-AGG-019 keeps a fully-reconciled claim from being recorded while it does, and each reporting surface the registered completion policy names as blocked by that accounting state remains blocked as that policy states. |
| **Analysis readiness** | Whether the study's exact registered inclusion, exclusion, missingness, and replacement rules are resolved under their registered identities and versions, and the exact sealed source-evidence set of the registered analysis — together with, for that set, the exact attempt-integrity-basis or seal-verification records and verdicts relied upon — is fixed by identity, as WP2-AGG-020 requires. | The analysis authority governed by charter Sections 14.2 and 14.7. | No aggregation eligible for the study's registered claim can yet be produced; a partial aggregate under Section 15.7 remains producible where the registered rules permit one, labeled as WP2-AGG-027 requires. |
| **Study validity for the registered claim** | Whether the study is valid for its registered claim, held as the explicit, versioned, evidence-linked determination the study's registered validity rules require, an adverse determination being the study-invalidity decision whose record model Section 14 states, under the registered validity rules that determination names. | The study or analysis authority governed by charter Sections 14.2 and 14.7, deciding under the study's registered validity rules and, for an adverse determination, through the Section 14 record model. | The study supports no registered claim while this result is unresolved or failed. An invalid study remains auditable with every record preserved and its closure records permitted, and invalidity authorizes neither deletion nor a favorable rerun, as Section 14 states. |
| **Aggregation production status** | Whether a study aggregation or an explicitly labeled partial aggregate has been produced and recorded for the study, which one is current under the correction and supersession lineage of Section 15.3, and whether a registered deterministic rebuild reproduced it or failed as the typed rebuild failure WP2-AGG-033 states. | The analysis authority governed by charter Sections 14.2 and 14.7, as the Section 6.7 study-aggregation entry states. | No study-level derived result exists, or its production or rebuild failed as a typed failure. The absence completes nothing and conceals nothing: WP2-AGG-006 keeps aggregate production from standing in for any other dimension, and a study that produced no execution-attempt evidence receives no aggregation identity under WP2-AGG-037. |

**WP2-AGG-001.** Every study completion-dimension result that a conforming laboratory records, references, or relies upon **MUST** conform to every clause of the completion-dimension model in Section 15.2 — its introducing statements and, for the applicable entry, what the dimension records, the recording authority the entry names, and what the entry states an unresolved or failed result of it means — except only the specific question or clause that the entry expressly assigns to a later section, an exemption that reaches no other clause of that attribute or entry.

**WP2-AGG-002.** A conforming laboratory **MUST** record each of the seven completion dimensions of the model in Section 15.2 as a separately recorded result of the study it belongs to — the explicit initial unresolved result of each dimension recorded when that study's study-membership-and-completion ledger is derived from its admitted registration, as an initialization rather than a substantive determination, never as an unresolved state read from the absence of a record — at most one result being current per dimension for one study, the current one read through the supersession and correction linkage WP2-AGG-004 states rather than from wall-clock arrival order.

**WP2-AGG-003.** A conforming laboratory **MUST NOT** represent two or more of the seven completion dimensions as one combined value or one scalar "complete", infer the recorded result of one dimension from the recorded result of another, or treat the recorded result of any one dimension as standing for the completion of the study as a whole.

**WP2-AGG-004.** Every recording of a completion-dimension result of a study — an initial result, a later result, a reclassification, or a correction — **MUST** be accepted at an authority boundary that is atomic, or safe against competing recordings to an equivalent degree — at which at most one initial result of one dimension of one study, and at most one successor of any one current result, becomes current, two competing recordings never both becoming current and currentness never being inferred from wall-clock arrival order — the explicit initial unresolved result of each dimension being recorded at the ledger's derivation from the admitted registration by the laboratory authority answerable for study-level membership and completion record-keeping, as an initialization rather than a substantive determination of validity, readiness, integrity, or reconciliation, and every later result, reclassification, or correction by the dimension-specific recording authority the Section 15.2 model names — a later result, reclassification, or correction being recorded as a further record that explicitly names the record it supersedes or corrects and carrying its recording authority and reason in the discipline WP2-LIFE-016 and WP2-LIFE-017 state, with every losing or stale recording preserved as a distinguishable rejection or refusal record naming the result it attempted, the authority that attempted it, and the reason it was not accepted, without deleting, overwriting, truncating, or rendering unverifiable any recorded result, record, or evidence.

**WP2-AGG-005.** A conforming laboratory **MUST** keep representable, with every recorded result current in its own dimension and none suppressed to resolve the appearance of tension, a study that is operationally closed while its evidence closure and integrity, budget or accounting reconciliation, analysis readiness, or study validity remains unresolved or failed, and a study whose every registered planned run, admitted execution attempt, and scheduled or admitted unit is dispositioned and which is scientifically invalid.

**WP2-AGG-006.** The production of a study aggregation or of a partial aggregate **MUST NOT** be recorded, reported, or treated as reaching, resolving, or superseding any other completion dimension of its study.

**Rationale.** The seven dimensions apply to the study the same discipline that Section 8.4 applies to the planned run and the execution attempt with its five scalar status dimensions: a single status word that means at once "every unit is dispositioned", "the evidence verifies", "the accounting is settled", "the analysis can run", "the science holds", and "an aggregate exists" cannot represent a study that is operationally finished with an unresolved provider exposure, a study whose every unit is dispositioned and whose registered claim its own preregistered rules invalidate, or a study whose aggregate exists while a seal verification stands failed. Charter Section 14.4 requires operational failure, artifact invalidity, study invalidity, and behaviorally unfavorable outcomes to remain distinct, and the study-level results here read, and never restate or overrule, the per-unit records those distinctions live in. The explicit initial unresolved result each dimension receives when that study's study-membership-and-completion ledger is derived from its admitted registration applies to completion the same reasoning WP2-EVID-020 applies to evidence classes: an unresolved state carried by the absence of a record is indistinguishable from a result that was never initialized, was lost, was omitted, or failed to commit, so the legitimate unresolved state is itself a recorded initialization — not a substantive determination of validity, readiness, integrity, or reconciliation — and an absent dimension record is a missing or integrity-defective record rather than a valid unresolved state. Each dimension's later results are recorded determinations in the pattern of the Section 8.5 and 8.6 study-disposition entries — a further determination names the one it supersedes, consistent with WP2-LIFE-016, and a correction is made in the WP2-LIFE-017 form — and WP2-AGG-004 adds the acceptance boundary that linkage alone cannot supply, in the same family as WP2-LIFE-034, WP2-SCHED-052, and WP2-SEC-017, so that two competing recordings of one dimension's result can never both become current under concurrent operation. The end-boundary answer this model gives Section 11 is deliberately narrow: the Section 11.3 study-level elapsed-time entry names an end event identified by the study's registration or its completion record, and the execution-and-disposition-closure entry states which recorded result can be that event, named by event identity under WP2-TIME-012, without making any completion result a timing fact itself. No transaction, store, arbiter, or consensus primitive is selected by any of this, and WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting one.

### 15.3 The membership-and-completion ledger and aggregation records

The study-record register below states, in the register style of Section 6, the two record types this section establishes at study level: the study-membership-and-completion ledger and the study aggregation record. Each entry states the same seven attributes in the same order. Its entries carry no requirement identifier of their own; their normative force flows through WP2-AGG-007. Where an entry names the study-aggregation identity, it names the identity the Section 6.7 register entry states in full, adding no second identity for it and revising no clause of that entry, which continues to bind through WP2-ID-027 — the same device by which the Section 12.3 register names the Section 6.6 logical-model-request identity.

| Attribute | What the entry states |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | The domain within which the record's identity distinguishes one instance of the record type from every other instance of that record type. |
| Recording authority | The role answerable for creating the record and for recording the facts it states. |
| Required parent and source references | The identities the record has to name. |
| Cardinality | The counts that hold between records of this type and the records and entities they reference or are referenced by. |
| Point of existence | The point from which the record exists and can be referenced. |
| Correction or supersession rule | What may and may not be done with the record and its identity after it has been recorded. |
| May it exist for a study that produced no execution evidence | Whether an instance of the record type can exist for a study that produced no execution-attempt evidence. |

**WP2-AGG-007.** Every study-membership-and-completion ledger and every study aggregation record — a partial aggregate among the latter's kinds — that a conforming laboratory creates, records, references, or relies upon **MUST** conform to every clause of the study-record register in Section 15.3 — its introducing statements and, for the applicable entry, its identity scope, recording authority, required parent and source references, cardinality, point of existence, correction or supersession rule, and rule on existence for a study that produced no execution evidence — except only the specific question or clause that the entry expressly assigns to a later section, an exemption that reaches no other clause of that attribute or entry.

**Rationale.** An authority named in this register is a role — the party or process answerable for creating a record or recording a fact — and never a component, service, store, or product, exactly as Section 6.2 keeps every authority in the entity register a role; WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting the mechanism that discharges it. WP2-AGG-007 is the single requirement that incorporates the register and makes every non-deferred clause binding, in the way WP2-ID-027 binds the entity register, WP2-LIFE-001 binds the lifecycle models, WP2-SCHED-051 binds the assignment-authority record model, and WP2-MODEL-001 binds the model-request register, so that no individual table cell carries a requirement identifier of its own and a cell that expressly assigns a question elsewhere states a boundary rather than an answer, consistent with Section 4.3.

**Study-membership-and-completion ledger.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every study-membership-and-completion ledger the laboratory records, across all studies and all concurrent execution: the one identity-based record of one study's registered membership, admitted execution attempts, dispositions, expected study-level records and evidence roots, and completion-dimension results, derived from the study's admitted registration. |
| Recording authority | The laboratory authority answerable for study-level membership and completion record-keeping under the study's admitted registration. The explicit initial unresolved result of each completion dimension is recorded by that record-keeping authority at the ledger's derivation, as an initialization rather than a substantive determination; each later completion-dimension result the ledger carries is recorded by the dimension-specific authority the Section 15.2 model names for that dimension, and each disposition it carries names the recording authority of the underlying record under Sections 8, 9, 13, and 14; the ledger reads those records and never restates them under a different authority. |
| Required parent and source references | Exactly one study identity and version, and the registration identity and version from which the ledger derives; the contents WP2-AGG-009 enumerates, each named by recorded identity rather than discovered from storage. |
| Cardinality | One study has exactly one study-membership-and-completion ledger identity, read through the version or correction lineage its correction rule states, and one ledger belongs to exactly one study. The ledger names every registered planned run of the study, a planned run with zero admitted execution attempts included, and every admitted execution attempt of every planned run. |
| Point of existence | From its derivation from the study's admitted registration, which may precede the admission of any execution attempt, the explicit initial unresolved result of each of the seven completion dimensions recorded at that derivation among the ledger's initial contents; later membership entries, dispositions, and completion-dimension results are appended as the facts they record are recorded. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never rewritten in place. A correction, or a later membership entry or completion result, is a further record that explicitly names the record it supersedes or corrects, under WP2-ID-025, WP2-ID-026, and the WP2-AGG-004 discipline, and the resulting version or correction lineage is what a study aggregation names under WP2-AGG-022. |
| May it exist for a study that produced no execution evidence | Yes. A study that produced no execution-attempt evidence receives its ledger with every registered planned run recorded, zero admitted execution attempts recorded wherever none was admitted, and the missingness, completion, and invalidity results that in fact hold, as WP2-AGG-036 requires. |

**Study aggregation record.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity scope | Every study aggregation the laboratory produces, under the study-aggregation identity the Section 6.7 register entry states in full; this entry adds no second identity for it and revises no clause of that entry, which continues to bind through WP2-ID-027. A partial aggregate is a kind of study aggregation record, carrying the explicit labeling and identifications WP2-AGG-027 requires, with its own clauses inside this entry rather than a separate record type. |
| Recording authority | The analysis authority governed by charter Sections 14.2 and 14.7, as the Section 6.7 entry states. |
| Required parent and source references | The identification set WP2-AGG-022 states — the Section 6.7 entry's study identity and version, analysis identity and version, and exact source-evidence set named by identity among its members — and, for the partial kind, the further identifications WP2-AGG-027 requires. |
| Cardinality | One study carries zero or more study aggregations, and one study aggregation names exactly one study and exactly one analysis version, as the Section 6.7 entry states. A primary aggregate includes at most one authoritative accepted outcome per planned run, under WP2-AGG-025. |
| Point of existence | When the aggregation is produced and recorded, as the Section 6.7 entry states. No aggregation identity is reserved ahead of the evidence it names, and none comes into existence for a study that produced no execution-attempt evidence. |
| Correction or supersession rule | Never reused and never rewritten in place, as the Section 6.7 entry states. A corrected, extended, or superseding aggregate is a new aggregation identity, or names a new analysis version, with explicit source and supersession lineage under WP2-AGG-029 and WP2-AGG-035, and every earlier aggregate remains preserved. |
| May it exist for a study that produced no execution evidence | No. Section 3.1 defines aggregation as the derived, versioned combination of evidence from multiple execution attempts, and the Section 6.7 entry states that a study aggregation derives from a non-empty set of evidence that execution attempts produced; WP2-AGG-036 and WP2-AGG-037 state the records such a study receives instead. |

**WP2-AGG-008.** A conforming laboratory **MUST** derive the study-membership-and-completion ledger of every study from that study's admitted registration and from the explicitly recorded identities, lineage, and dispositions of Sections 6, 8, 9, 13, and 14, rather than from discovered files, directory contents, storage layout, wall-clock ordering, successful outcomes, or the set of units that happened to produce evidence.

**WP2-AGG-009.** The study-membership-and-completion ledger of a study **MUST** include every registered planned run of that study, a planned run with zero admitted execution attempts included; every admitted execution attempt of every planned run — first attempts, further attempts, and registered replacements alike; the exact recorded relationship of each execution attempt to its planned run and, where one exists, to its replacement predecessor, as the Section 6.4 replacement-lineage entry and WP2-ID-011 record it; every lifecycle, finalization, seal, failure, cancellation, invalidity, exclusion, and authoritative-outcome disposition required for interpretation, as Sections 8, 9, 13, and 14 record them; and every expected study-level record and evidence root under the study's registered evidence contract.

**Rationale.** The membership-and-completion ledger is derived from the registration rather than from what execution left behind, because the two diverge in exactly the cases a study's interpretation turns on: a planned run that never had an execution attempt admitted leaves no evidence to discover, a cancelled attempt may leave an empty run evidence root, and a filesystem walk finds what survived rather than what was registered. Charter Section 14.3 requires the registration governing an evidence-generating study to precede execution, so the registered membership exists before any evidence does and is the only honest source for it; WP2-ID-023 already requires lineage to be read from the record rather than reconstructed from ordering, paths, or names, and WP2-AGG-008 extends the same discipline to the study's roster. Section 5.11 records, as evidence about the current system only, that the repository's sequence evaluation reads exactly one root and fails rather than silently omitting a completed execution whose fingerprint is missing; the ledger stated here is the study-level generalization of that honesty, defined by registration rather than by discovery.

### 15.4 Denominators and missingness

**WP2-AGG-010.** A conforming laboratory **MUST** report the registered sample denominator of a study as the count of that study's registered planned runs as the study-membership-and-completion ledger records them, and as no other quantity.

**WP2-AGG-011.** Execution attempts **MUST** be reported in a separate operational denominator, never merged with, substituted for, or reported as the registered sample denominator.

**WP2-AGG-012.** A registered replacement, which WP2-ID-010 keeps an additional execution attempt of the same planned run, **MUST NOT** increase the registered sample denominator of its study, silently or otherwise.

**WP2-AGG-013.** The study-membership-and-completion ledger **MUST** record, for each planned run, zero or more admitted execution attempts and at most one authoritative accepted outcome, consistent with WP2-ID-007 and WP2-ID-009.

**WP2-AGG-014.** Where a planned run has no authoritative accepted outcome, the study-membership-and-completion ledger **MUST** record the exact missingness or non-acceptance reason — no execution attempt admitted, no admitted attempt started, cancelled, failed, invalid, excluded, no eligible successfully finalized attempt, or unresolved among them — without removing the planned run from the registered sample denominator.

**Rationale.** A denominator that can shrink is the quietest way a study lies about itself: dropping a planned run that produced nothing turns "eight of ten registered runs reached an outcome" into "eight of eight", and no later reader of the shrunken record can tell the difference. The registered sample denominator is therefore fixed by registration and never by outcome, the operational denominator carries the physical-execution story separately — the same separation Section 3.1 draws between the planned run and the execution attempt — and a registered replacement adds to the second without touching the first, because WP2-ID-010 makes it an additional attempt of an existing planned run rather than a new registered sample unit. The missingness reasons WP2-AGG-014 enumerates are recorded facts of Sections 8, 9, 13, and 14, so the reason a planned run contributes no outcome is read from its own dispositions rather than asserted at the study level, and a planned run with zero attempts stays visible exactly as WP2-ID-007 keeps it representable.

### 15.5 Completion conditions

**WP2-AGG-015.** A conforming laboratory **MUST NOT** declare any completion dimension of a study reached, or record a clean result in one, merely because a desired number of successful execution attempts or authoritative accepted outcomes exists.

**WP2-AGG-016.** Registered-membership closure **MUST** be recorded as reached for a study only where its reached result binds one exact study-membership-and-completion ledger identity and its version or correction lineage, every registered planned run, every admitted execution attempt, every scheduled or admitted unit, and every replacement decision of that study is present in that ledger by recorded identity, and every registered planned run has reached a status under the lifecycle models of Section 8 from which no further execution attempt may be admitted and the study's registered continuation and replacement rules permit no further admission under it, so that no further execution attempt or replacement can lawfully be admitted under that membership — an admission attempted after the reached result being refused and preserved, or proceeding only under a new registration or another registered-membership boundary identity or version change the governing research design expressly requires, the admission then belonging to that new membership boundary rather than to the closed one and its evidence reaching new inventory, seal, and lineage records as WP2-EVID-055 requires, never as a silent reopening of the closed membership — and execution and disposition closure only where each of them additionally holds an explicitly recorded disposition or result.

**WP2-AGG-017.** An undispositioned scheduled or admitted unit **MUST NOT** disappear from a study's ledger, denominators, completion results, or reported surfaces because it produced no evidence or no successful result.

**WP2-AGG-018.** Evidence closure and integrity **MUST** be recorded as reached for a study only where every expected run evidence root and study-level record of that study is accounted for, every execution attempt of that study that reached a terminal operational value carries the evidence-closure result Section 13 requires of it, and every required inventory, run-level seal, study-level seal, sealed package, seal-verification, and attempt-integrity-basis result — the recorded sealing outcome WP2-FAIL-026 requires of every execution attempt a cancellation makes terminal and, for every execution attempt of the study for which WP2-EVID-051 requires one, the current attempt-integrity basis WP2-EVID-073 keeps race-safely decided among them — holds a current typed outcome under the study's registered evidence contract.

**WP2-AGG-019.** A record of the budget-or-accounting-reconciliation dimension **MUST NOT** claim fully reconciled accounting for a study while any unresolved or disputed exposure of that study remains represented under WP2-MODEL-046.

**WP2-AGG-020.** Analysis readiness **MUST** be recorded as reached for a study only where that study's exact registered inclusion, exclusion, missingness, and replacement rules are resolved under their registered identities and versions and the exact sealed source-evidence set of the registered analysis — together with, for that set, the exact attempt-integrity-basis or seal-verification records and verdicts relied upon — is fixed by identity.

**WP2-AGG-021.** A recorded concurrency-profile or provider-behavior divergence, a typed refusal, a refused admission of candidate output, a sanitized derivative, a detected-secret incident, an unresolved or disputed exposure, or any other recorded deviation of Sections 10 through 14 and 16 **MUST** reach a study's completion results, denominators, membership-and-completion ledger, and reported surfaces only through an explicitly recorded result of the applicable completion dimension under the study's registered rules, never through the silent omission of a unit, the silent shrinking of a denominator, or the silent degradation or upgrade of any recorded result.

**Rationale.** These conditions answer, dimension by dimension, the assignments the other sections make here. The undispositioned-unit rule answers Sections 8.16 and 9.13: a unit that was scheduled or admitted and then neither ran, finished, failed, nor was cancelled is exactly the unit a concurrent laboratory loses track of, and WP2-AGG-016 and WP2-AGG-017 make its absence from the disposition record a fact that blocks closure rather than a gap that closure papers over. The evidence condition answers Section 13.14 by reading Section 13's own results: closure, inventory, seal, package, verification, and attempt-integrity-basis outcomes, the cancellation sealing outcome WP2-FAIL-026 requires among them, enter completion as recorded typed outcomes, never as an assumption from an absent failure. The accounting condition answers Section 12.11: an unresolved or disputed exposure survives into the study's completion record as itself, a registered completion policy states which such states block which surface, and no surface reports reconciliation that has not happened. WP2-AGG-021 answers Sections 10.13 and 16.10 in one rule: a deviation, incident, refused admission, or sanitized derivative has exactly the study-level effect its dimension's registered rules record for it — a detected-secret incident whose affected surfaces Section 13 marks blocks a clean aggregate through WP2-AGG-024, for example — and never an unrecorded one. Study validity is deliberately absent from these conditions: it is the sixth dimension's own registered decision, recorded through the Section 14 model, and no accumulation of clean operational results reaches it, as WP2-AGG-003 and WP2-AGG-038 keep separately checkable.

### 15.6 Aggregation source set and membership

A *primary aggregate* is a study aggregation produced to report the study's registered sample for its registered claim. An *audit and missingness surface* is the part of a study aggregation's record that accounts for every unit that did not contribute an authoritative accepted outcome. Both phrases name kinds or parts of the study aggregation record of Section 15.3 rather than further record types, and each carries no obligation beyond the requirements that cite it.

**WP2-AGG-022.** Every study aggregation **MUST** identify the study identity and version; the analysis identity and version; the exact aggregation identity; the exact sealed source-evidence set from which it derives, named by run-evidence-root, sealed-package, inventory, and seal identity as applicable; for every source of that set, the exact attempt-integrity-basis or seal-verification record and verdict relied upon; the study-membership-and-completion ledger identity and its version or correction lineage as read for the aggregation; every included authoritative planned-run outcome; every missing, invalid, excluded, cancelled, or non-authoritative execution attempt required for denominator and audit reporting; the aggregation rules and their version; and the producing analysis authority together with the execution and concurrency provenance of the aggregation operation itself — naming each of those that applies and stating the absence of any that does not.

**WP2-AGG-023.** Membership in a study aggregation's source set **MUST** be established by recorded identity alone, never from directory layout, storage location, timestamps, wall-clock proximity, file discovery, or successful-run count, consistent with WP2-ID-018, WP2-ID-023, and the join discipline WP2-ISO-014 states.

**WP2-AGG-024.** A duplicate source identity, a duplicate planned-run membership, two authoritative outcomes claimed for one planned run, an unregistered source, an omitted member the registered analysis requires, a source whose required seal does not verify, a source whose named attempt-integrity basis is not current under WP2-EVID-073, or not successful in the sense the attempt-integrity-basis entry of the register in Section 13.3 states, or whose relied-upon seal verification is not verified current and successful under the registered aggregation rules, or a source over which an undispositioned affected-or-compromised marking of the kind Section 13 requires for detected secret material stands **MUST** prevent a clean study aggregation over that source set, a study-level derived result remaining producible, where the registered rules permit one at all, only as the explicitly labeled partial record of Section 15.7.

**WP2-AGG-025.** A primary aggregate **MUST** include at most one authoritative accepted outcome per planned run, and none for a planned run that has none.

**WP2-AGG-026.** Non-authoritative execution attempts — failed, cancelled, invalid, excluded, superseded, late, losing, and replaced attempts among them — **MUST** remain referenced in the aggregation's audit and missingness surface rather than promoted into the primary aggregate, erased from the aggregation record, or counted as independent sample units.

**Rationale.** WP2-AGG-022 completes WP2-ID-021, which already requires every study aggregation to identify the study, the analysis version, and the exact set of source evidence from which it derives; what this section adds is the rest of the identification a concurrent laboratory needs before a derived result is auditable — the sealed identities of the sources, the exact roster the aggregation believed, the rules applied, and the provenance of the aggregating operation itself. The membership rule is the aggregation counterpart of WP2-ISO-014 and WP2-MODEL-018: temporal adjacency and storage proximity join nothing here either.
