The audit surface exists because charter Section 14.4 keeps failed, interrupted, invalid, excluded, and replacement attempts recorded evidence with typed dispositions and WP2-SCHED-029 preserves every losing or superseded result with a distinguishable non-authoritative disposition; an aggregate that dropped them would erase by summary what the record preserves item by item, while an aggregate that counted them as sample units would inflate the denominator WP2-AGG-012 fixes. The seal-verification and integrity-basis blockers read Section 13's results — a clean aggregation verifying, for every source, the named attempt-integrity basis or relied-upon seal verification and verdict under the registered aggregation rules, WP2-EVID-073 fixing which basis is current for an attempt and unseating a basis over whose named boundary, inventory, or seal a later non-successful verification stands, so that a clean aggregate can never rest on one favorable verification while another preserved verification disagrees — and the detected-secret blocker answers Section 16.10's assignment through Section 13's affected-surface marking, so that no clean aggregate stands over bytes an undispositioned incident touches.

### 15.7 Partial studies and partial aggregates

A *partial-study record* is a study-level completion or membership record produced while units remain unresolved, and a *partial aggregate* is the partial kind of the study aggregation record of Section 15.3. Each is a permitted, explicitly labeled, non-final record; neither phrase names a further record type, and each carries no obligation beyond the requirements that cite it.

**WP2-AGG-027.** A partial-study record or partial aggregate **MUST** be produced only where it is labeled explicitly as partial and identifies the exact membership snapshot and source-evidence set it read; every unresolved or undispositioned unit; every missing expected source; the producing analysis identity and version and the producing authority; and each respect in which the record is not final or not eligible for the study's registered claim.

**WP2-AGG-028.** A partial-study record or partial aggregate **MUST NOT** become final, or be recorded or reported as final, by renaming, overwriting, relocation, or the addition of files or records in place.

**WP2-AGG-029.** A later aggregate over the same study **MUST** be a new aggregation identity carrying explicit source lineage and explicit supersession lineage to each earlier aggregate it supersedes, with every earlier aggregate preserved.

**WP2-AGG-030.** A partial aggregate **MUST NOT** be reported or cited as the study's final aggregate, or as eligible for the study's registered claim.

**Rationale.** A partial result is legitimate exactly as far as it is honest about being partial. The identification WP2-AGG-027 requires is what keeps a snapshot from quietly hardening into a conclusion: the membership snapshot and source set fix what the record could see, the unresolved units and missing sources fix what it could not, and the eligibility statement fixes what it may never be cited for. Becoming final is a new act with a new identity rather than a promotion in place, in the same append-and-supersede discipline WP2-ID-025, WP2-ID-026, and charter Section 14.9 apply to every correction — Section 5.11 records, as evidence about the current system only, that the repository's one production analyzer already writes derived output create-once and refuses an existing destination, which shows the discipline is practicable without making the current mechanism a requirement.

### 15.8 Deterministic rebuild and derived-output integrity

**WP2-AGG-031.** Every study aggregation and partial aggregate **MUST** be written outside the immutable run-evidence-root and study-source-evidence boundaries it reads, in the Section 3.1 sense of aggregation.

**WP2-AGG-032.** An aggregation operation **MUST NOT** replace, mutate, reorganize, conceal, or write into the source evidence it reads.

**WP2-AGG-033.** Where the registered aggregation of a study is deterministic, the exact sealed sources, the analysis identity and version, the aggregation rules and their version, and the identified execution environment **MUST** reproduce the same derived output or produce a typed rebuild failure, never a silently different result.

**WP2-AGG-034.** Where a registered aggregation includes stochastic or model-assisted work, the aggregation record **MUST** state that fact explicitly and record the model, version, prompt, input, and uncertainty lineage charter Section 14.7 requires of a model-assisted label together with the random and stochastic-event lineage of any stochastic work and its non-engine-native evidentiary status, the output never being treated as deterministic, as engine-native, or as authoritative simulated-world truth under charter Section 14.8.

**WP2-AGG-035.** A correction, a changed analysis rule, a changed source set, or a changed aggregation rule **MUST** produce a new aggregation identity or a new analysis version, as applicable, with every existing aggregate preserved, consistent with WP2-ID-021, WP2-ID-024, and charter Section 14.9.

**Rationale.** These requirements answer what the WP2-ISO-004 rationale assigns here — what a study aggregation may write, and when it may declare a result — for the writing half: it writes outside every immutable boundary it reads, exactly as Section 3.1's aggregation term and charter Section 14.9's rule that derived analysis is written separately and records its source identities require, so that producing a study-level result can never be the operation that damages the evidence the result rests on. The deterministic-rebuild rule is what makes a derived output checkable rather than merely present: the same sealed sources under the same identified rules either reproduce it or fail as a typed rebuild failure, and a silent difference is exactly the outcome the rule excludes. The stochastic and model-assisted carve-out keeps that honesty two-sided — such work is permitted where registered, and its lineage and non-engine-native status are recorded so that charter Section 14.7's rule that a model-assisted label never silently becomes ground truth survives aggregation. No aggregation algorithm, statistical method, rebuild toolchain, or environment representation is selected, and Section 15.11 records each as unselected.

### 15.9 Zero-evidence studies

**WP2-AGG-036.** A study that produced no execution-attempt evidence **MUST** receive the applicable study-membership-and-completion ledger, completion-dimension results, missingness records, and, where the registered authority so decides, study-invalidity records of this section and Section 14, its ledger recording every registered planned run, every admitted execution attempt where any was admitted, and the exact reason no authoritative accepted outcome exists for each planned run.

**WP2-AGG-037.** A conforming laboratory **MUST NOT** create a study-aggregation identity, an empty aggregation, or any record described as a derived combination of execution attempts' evidence for a study that produced no execution-attempt evidence, merely to fill that absence.

**Rationale.** The Section 6.7 study-aggregation entry already states both halves of this rule in identity terms: a study aggregation derives from a non-empty set of evidence that execution attempts produced, no aggregation identity is reserved ahead of the evidence it names, and the study-level record of a study that produced no evidence is a distinct record assigned to this section rather than a study aggregation. This section supplies that distinct record: the membership-and-completion ledger, the completion-dimension results, and the missingness and invalidity records carry everything true about such a study — what was registered, what was admitted, what was cancelled or refused, and why nothing was accepted — without manufacturing a derived result whose defining derivation never occurred. A fictitious empty aggregate would not be a conservative placeholder; it would be a record asserting a combination of evidence that does not exist, and every claim-boundary rule of Section 15.10 would then attach to an object with nothing under it.

### 15.10 Completion and claim boundaries

**WP2-AGG-038.** The recorded completion of a study, in any completion dimension other than study validity for the registered claim or in all six of those dimensions together, **MUST NOT** be recorded, reported, or cited as study validity for the registered claim, which remains the separate registered decision the sixth dimension records.

**WP2-AGG-039.** A recorded study-validity result **MUST NOT** be reported or cited as evidence of behavioral quality, believability, semantic correctness, or observer value.

**WP2-AGG-040.** A sealed, rebuilt, or deterministically reproducible study aggregation **MUST NOT** be reported or cited as evidence of serial-versus-parallel behavioral parity, neutrality, or interchangeability.

**WP2-AGG-041.** Completion of any study, production of any aggregate, or conformance with this section **MUST NOT** be treated as beginning, satisfying, or substituting for the separately authorized Work Package 3 serial-versus-parallel parity study.

**WP2-AGG-042.** The operational closure, full disposition, or operational success of a study **MUST NOT** be recorded, reported, or treated as overriding, contradicting, or superseding a recorded study-invalidity decision, an operationally successful and scientifically invalid study remaining representable with both results current in their own dimensions under WP2-AGG-005.

**WP2-AGG-043.** Failures, cancellations, exclusions, or missing authoritative accepted outcomes within a study whose preregistered rules permit and report them **MUST NOT** be recorded, reported, or cited as by themselves establishing that study's invalidity, the study-validity decision remaining the explicit, versioned, evidence-linked determination the study's registered validity rules require, an adverse one being the study-invalidity decision whose record model Section 14 states.

**WP2-AGG-044.** A study aggregation or partial aggregate **MUST NOT** be cited beyond the registered study, treatment, evidence, analysis, and claim boundary its own record names.

**Rationale.** These boundaries close the section the way Sections 2.3 and 3.5 open the specification. Completion is an infrastructure fact and validity is a scientific decision, and charter Section 8.5 states directly that throughput does not establish scientific validity and that parallel execution is not presumed behaviorally neutral; WP2-SCOPE-011 and WP2-SCOPE-012 already deny a conformance result the parity and quality readings, WP2-SCHED-050 denies them to every concurrency and throughput record, and WP2-AGG-039 and WP2-AGG-040 deny the same readings to the study-level records this section adds, so that no layer of the evidence acquires by aggregation a meaning its parts are prohibited from carrying. Charter Section 10.4 requires parallel execution to earn behavioral use through a preregistered parity study and keeps concurrency a recorded experimental factor until then, which is why a reproducible aggregate — however clean — is not parity evidence and why WP2-AGG-041 keeps this section's completion machinery from being mistaken for Work Package 3, consistent with WP2-SCOPE-014's rule for the package as a whole. The last three requirements keep the validity dimension truthful in both directions: operational success neither overrides an invalidity decision nor is impugned by one, and preregistered, reported imperfection is not invalidity — charter Section 14.4 prohibits rerunning an attempt merely because its trajectory was unfavorable, and a study whose registered rules anticipated failures reports them rather than being condemned by them. What any aggregate may be cited for ends at the boundary its own record names, and nothing in this section extends it.

### 15.11 Options deliberately left unselected

**Unselected option.** Statistical method and inclusion rules. The statistical method of any registered analysis, every inclusion or exclusion threshold, and every missing-data method are the registered design's decisions, carried by the analysis identity and version and the registered rules WP2-AGG-020 and WP2-AGG-022 require to be named. This specification selects none of them, and WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting a threshold or any other numeric parameter.

**Unselected option.** Aggregation algorithm and rebuild realization. The aggregation algorithm, the rebuild toolchain, and the representation of the identified execution environment WP2-AGG-033 names may be realized by any arrangement that satisfies this section. No algorithm, toolchain, runtime, or environment-description scheme is selected.

**Unselected option.** Ledger realization. The study-membership-and-completion ledger may be carried by a single record, a set of linked records, an append-only log from which the current roster is derived, or another arrangement, in any store. WP2-AGG-008 and WP2-AGG-009 state the derivation and contents; no schema, encoding, store, or placement is selected.

**Unselected option.** Membership-snapshot and partial-record representation. The membership snapshot WP2-AGG-027 requires may be carried by a ledger version reference, a copied roster, a recorded query over identified records, or another representation, and a partial record's labeling may be realized in any explicit form. No representation is selected.

**Unselected option.** Completion-result representation and placement. The seven dimension results may be carried as fields of the ledger, as separate records it references, or in another arrangement, and the authority boundary WP2-AGG-004 requires may be realized by the same range of arrangements Sections 8.15 and 9.12 record for the boundaries of WP2-LIFE-034 and WP2-SCHED-052. No encoding, store, arbiter topology, or primitive is selected.

**Unselected option.** Denominator reporting form. How the registered sample denominator and the operational denominator are presented in a report — jointly, separately, or with further registered breakdowns — is a reporting decision of the registered design, provided the two are never merged, as WP2-AGG-011 requires. No reporting format is selected.

### 15.12 Boundaries assigned elsewhere

This section states how a study's membership, execution, evidence, accounting, analysis readiness, validity, and aggregation reach recorded conclusions, what a study aggregation identifies, and what no completion result or aggregate may be cited as establishing. It deliberately defines none of the following: the evidence-closure, replay, finalization, inventory, sealing, package, and verification records whose results the evidence dimension reads, which Section 13 states; the failure-class taxonomy, the typed disposition vocabulary, the study-invalidity record model, and replacement eligibility, which Section 14 states; the conformance checks, fault injections, and pass conditions for every requirement stated here, which belong to Section 17 and to the conformance document; the implementation handoff and the ranking of the options this section leaves unselected, which belong to Section 18; and the dependency boundary toward, and the design of, any serial-versus-parallel parity study, which belong to Section 19 and to the separately authorized Work Package 3.

## 16. Security and secret boundaries

### 16.1 What this section establishes

This section states the security and secret boundaries that a conforming laboratory has to satisfy while many independent runs and many independent studies execute at the same time over shared infrastructure, against provider credentials, and through provider sessions. It completes what the Section 7.21 boundary statement and the Section 7.16 rationale assign here — secret custody, injection, rotation, redaction, scanning of evidence, and the security boundary in general, beyond the isolation boundary WP2-ISO-025 states. It completes the issuance side of the nonsecret identity WP2-SCHED-047 requires, which the Section 10.13 boundary statement assigns here as the secret boundary within which a nonsecret alias is issued and held. And it states the secret-material half of the request-content rule of Section 12, whose record register requires the immutable request specification to contain no secret material and coordinates the obligation behind that clause with this section, where WP2-SEC-004 carries it. It designs no secret manager and selects no encryption mechanism, credential store, injection mechanism, detection algorithm or threshold, or rotation interval; the selections WP2-SCOPE-015 enumerates remain prohibited throughout this document. The incident taxonomy and the typed dispositions of the events this section requires to be recorded are assigned to Section 14, and the evidence format, retention, and sealing treatment of its records are assigned to Section 13.

Section 16.2 defines the five controlling terms and carries the requirement that makes them binding. Sections 16.3 through 16.8 state the requirements. Section 16.9 records options deliberately left unselected, and Section 16.10 states the boundaries assigned elsewhere.

Every substantive statement here is a Required future invariant carrying exactly one `WP2-SEC-###` identifier, a Design rationale introduced by a **Rationale.** lead-in, or an Unselected option introduced by an **Unselected option.** lead-in, in the Section 4.3 sense, or else a definition of the definitional block in Section 16.2, whose normative force flows through WP2-SEC-001 rather than through an identifier of its own, the credential-authority event model in Section 16.6, its introducing statements and each of its entries, whose normative force flows through WP2-SEC-017 rather than through an identifier of their own, or a boundary statement in Section 16.10. Nothing in this section is a Current fact or a Current limitation; those belong to Section 5, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps a current mechanism from being read as a requirement. Every defined term used here carries the meaning Section 3.1 assigns it, as WP2-SCOPE-005 requires, and every lifecycle term carries the meaning Section 8.3 assigns it, as WP2-LIFE-002 requires, the admission of candidate output as evidence and the replacement of a credential being ordinary description in the carve-out sense Section 8.3 itself states for the admission of a study, a registration, a replacement, or a laboratory worker into service.

### 16.2 Definitions

The terms below carry exactly the meanings given here throughout this specification and the reporting governed by it, wherever they name a secret-handling fact, boundary, distinction, or record. They refine, and never displace, the Section 3.1 definitions, and they leave the isolation boundary WP2-ISO-025 states exactly where Section 7 put it. An occurrence of one of these words in a sense outside that secret-handling use — the secret scan or credential file a Section 5 current fact describes, a credential boundary named as one kind of shared infrastructure in WP2-ISO-001 and WP2-ISO-033, or a secret kept by a simulated character inside its simulated world — is ordinary description rather than a use of a term defined here.

- **Secret material** is credential or key material whose disclosure would permit acting against a provider or a resource: an API key, a token, a session or cookie secret, signing material, a password, and any value from which one of those is directly recoverable. Secret material is *live* while it remains usable against the provider or resource it concerns; a value that has been revoked and rendered unusable is no longer live, and it remains secret material for every rule of this section that does not say *live*.
- **Nonsecret credential reference** is the stable nonsecret identity — together with its version, where the identity is versioned — by which a credential, a provider project, or an account scope is named in records without revealing, containing, or permitting reconstruction of the secret material it concerns: the nonsecret alias, digest, or registered identity WP2-SCHED-047 requires, in the form and for the identifiers that requirement states, and the identity to which the records of Sections 10 and 12 attribute provider-project and account-scope facts.
- **Provider-session state** is the mutable upstream session, connection, or authentication state that a provider interaction establishes, and its *raw* form is that state as held for use against the provider — a form that can carry or expose secret material — as distinct from a nonsecret record about the session.
- **Provider-dispatch secret boundary** is the minimum component boundary within which secret material is available for making provider dispatches, identified and versioned as the applicable registered secret-handling rules require; charter Section 14.9 requires secret-handling rules to be versioned. Naming the boundary names a property of the laboratory's own division of custody rather than a component: no component, mechanism, or topology is selected by it, and Sections 7.20 and 16.9 record the custody mechanisms as unselected.
- **Access versus attribution** is the distinction between a component's access to a credential and a record's attribution to that credential's nonsecret scope identity. A record attributes usage, exposure, contention, or behavior to a nonsecret credential reference; attribution neither requires nor confers access to the secret material, and a record that attributes never accesses or carries what it attributes to.

**WP2-SEC-001.** The normative prose of this specification and the reporting governed by it **MUST** use the five terms defined in Section 16.2 with the meanings assigned there, wherever they name a secret-handling fact, boundary, distinction, or record of the kind this section governs, each naming a distinct meaning rather than a synonym for another, an occurrence outside that secret-handling use being ordinary description as Section 16.2 states.

**Rationale.** The definitional block supplies the controlling meanings, and WP2-SEC-001 is the single requirement that makes them binding, in the way WP2-LIFE-002 binds the lifecycle vocabulary, so that no individual definition carries a requirement identifier of its own. The access-versus-attribution distinction is the one the rest of this section rests on: WP2-SCHED-047 and Section 12 need stable identities that records are joined by, WP2-ISO-025 needs availability confined, and a laboratory that conflated the two would either widen access every time it attributed a record or strip its records of attribution every time it narrowed access. Keeping the *raw* form of provider-session state a defined form serves the same separation: a session record that describes is attribution, and session state that authenticates is access.

### 16.3 No live secret on any surface

**WP2-SEC-002.** A conforming laboratory **MUST NOT** write, expose, or permit live secret material to appear on any of the following surfaces, each prohibited separately: a browser or client surface; a simulation or laboratory-worker input; a model prompt or context; request or response evidence; a run or study artifact; an aggregate; a log or diagnostic record; a filename; a URL; a command line; error text; a pull request; documentation; or an example.

**WP2-SEC-003.** A conforming laboratory **MUST** make secret material available only within the provider-dispatch secret boundary authorized to use it, and to no component, surface, or record outside that boundary other than the configured provider endpoint that the provider-authentication egress WP2-SEC-019 states reaches for one identified provider attempt, that egress being the one authorized crossing of that boundary and no general exception.

**WP2-SEC-019.** Secret material, and provider-required authentication material, **MUST** leave the provider-dispatch secret boundary that holds it only through the minimum authorized provider-authentication egress for one identified provider attempt, to the configured provider endpoint and route recorded for that one identified provider attempt, the nonsecret identity of that endpoint and of that route being recorded with the attempt, an egress that makes the material available to no other laboratory component, isolation unit, record, log, diagnostic, error text, redirect target, or destination, and every record of which carries only nonsecret endpoint, provider, route, boundary, credential-reference, and policy identities.

**WP2-SEC-004.** Secret material **MUST NOT** become part of the content of a logical model request, of the immutable request specification of Section 12, or of any evidence identity — a digest, an inventory entry, a seal, or any other identity-bearing value by which evidence is identified, joined, or authenticated.

**Rationale.** The surfaces are enumerated in one prohibition, in the form WP2-ISO-030 establishes, because each is a route by which a secret would leave the provider-dispatch secret boundary and enter evidence, an aggregate, an operator surface, or this specification package itself; the pull-request and documentation surfaces are the ones on which a conforming laboratory's own reporting would carry a secret outward, and Section 4.2 records that a requirement governing this specification package rather than an implementation can receive a document-audit check in the conformance document. This specification writes no example value shaped like a key, token, password, or credential anywhere, not even as an obviously fabricated placeholder, for the same reason. WP2-SEC-004 completes the request-content rule of Section 12: the immutable request specification is the request-specific binding record bound to one logical request, and the immutable semantic content identity or digest it fixes is never reused for different content, so secret material inside that specification or its content identity would make the secret part of an identity that request, response, idempotency, and evidence records join by — after which a rotation under Section 16.6 could neither remove the secret without rewriting recorded content identity nor preserve that identity without preserving the secret. WP2-SEC-003 states the availability rule and selects nothing about how custody is realized; Sections 7.20 and 16.9 record those mechanisms as unselected, and what happens where a secret nonetheless reaches candidate output is governed by Section 16.8. The provider-authentication egress WP2-SEC-019 authorizes clarifies that availability rule without broadening it: transmitting credential or authentication material to the configured provider endpoint is what an authorized provider dispatch consists of, so the egress is the authorized transmission to the provider for one identified provider attempt, and WP2-SEC-002 continues to prohibit realizing it as an appearance on any of the fourteen surfaces it enumerates — a URL and a command line among them — so the egress is confined rather than exempted; it relaxes neither WP2-SEC-002 nor WP2-SEC-004, and every record of it carries only the nonsecret identities WP2-SEC-019 states.

### 16.4 Isolation of credentials and provider sessions

**WP2-SEC-005.** A conforming laboratory **MUST NOT** permit the credential material or the raw provider-session state held for one study, one planned run, one execution attempt, one laboratory worker, or one provider session to become available to another of them, through a shared process, a shared credential store, a shared connection, or any other shared infrastructure.

**WP2-SEC-006.** A conforming laboratory **MUST** confine any use of one credential on behalf of more than one study, planned run, execution attempt, laboratory worker, or provider session to a provider-dispatch secret boundary, versioned and identified as Section 16.2 states, that makes the credential and the raw provider-session state it establishes available to no unit it serves and that preserves complete request, usage, project-scope, and treatment attribution for every isolation unit it serves.

**Rationale.** WP2-SEC-005 extends WP2-ISO-025, which prohibits secret material made available to one isolation unit from becoming available to another study, another independent run, another execution attempt, or another provider session as a consequence of shared infrastructure: what this section adds is the laboratory worker and the provider session as subjects whose access is bounded, and raw provider-session state as a protected object alongside credential material, because an upstream session established for one unit's dispatches holds authentication state from which secret material can be recoverable, and a laboratory that isolated credential files while sharing raw sessions would have moved the exposure rather than removed it. WP2-SEC-006 answers the question WP2-SEC-005 leaves open: sharing one credential across units is not prohibited — it is confined behind one versioned provider-dispatch secret boundary whose records keep every served unit's request, usage, project-scope, and treatment attribution complete, so that WP2-ISO-001's rule that sharing never merges identities, treatments, mutable state, evidence, or budget attribution, WP2-ISO-016's attribution at all three isolation levels, and WP2-ISO-033's rule that sharing never relaxes an isolation requirement all survive the sharing intact. How sessions, connections, or credentials are partitioned is a mechanism, and Section 16.9 records it as unselected.

### 16.5 Nonsecret project-scope identity

**WP2-SEC-007.** Every nonsecret credential reference **MUST** remain stable across every record that the contracts of Sections 10 and 12 require to be joined through it.

**WP2-SEC-008.** A nonsecret credential reference **MUST NOT** reveal, contain, or permit reconstruction of the secret material of the credential, provider project, or account scope it names.

**WP2-SEC-009.** The issuance of a nonsecret credential reference that is derived from the secret material it names, and every record or operation that maps or resolves such a reference to that secret material, **MUST** remain within the provider-dispatch secret boundary that holds that secret material, the reference itself remaining usable in records outside that boundary.

**Rationale.** These three complete the issuance side of WP2-SCHED-047, which requires a stable nonsecret alias, digest, or registered identity wherever a provider-project or account identifier would expose secret material, and they answer what the Section 10.13 boundary statement assigns here: the secret boundary within which such an alias is issued and held is the provider-dispatch secret boundary of the secret it stands for. Stability is what the joins need — the WP2-SCHED-047 rationale records that the category model of Section 10.5 needs the provider-project or account scope in order to attribute contention, rate limiting, and correlated failure correctly, and Section 12 attributes requests, usage, and budget events to the same nonsecret scope identity — so a reference that changed between records would sever exactly the joins it exists to preserve. The reconstruction prohibition reaches the derivation: an alias or digest derived from the secret in a way that permits recovery of it does not satisfy WP2-SEC-008, however convenient the derivation. WP2-SEC-009 is what lets the reference be everywhere while the secret is nowhere: resolution of the reference back to secret material is possible only inside the boundary, and no record outside it carries the mapping. A reference the laboratory does not derive from the secret — a provider-issued project identifier, or an identity fixed in the registration under the ninth category of Section 10.5 — is issued wherever the registered design issues it; only its mapping and resolution to secret material are confined. No alias, digest, or mapping scheme is selected, as Sections 10.12 and 16.9 record.

### 16.6 Rotation, revocation, and replacement

The credential-authority event model below states, in the register style of Section 6, what every credential-authority event — the individually recorded authority event by which a credential's lifecycle is operated over its nonsecret credential reference and version — establishes, in the way the Section 9.6 record model states authority over an assignment-authority grant. Its entries carry no requirement identifier of their own; their normative force flows through WP2-SEC-017.

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Event identity | Every credential-authority event the laboratory records, across all credentials, provider projects, account scopes, and concurrent operation, each such event separately identified within that scope by an identity that is never reused for another event. |
| Operation identity and membership | The explicit identity of the one credential-lifecycle operation that produced the event, never reused for another operation; every event of one operation names it, and where one operation produces more than one event, the operation's complete event membership is explicit, through those events or through one explicit operation record, so that the all-or-none acceptance WP2-SEC-017 requires is checkable without inference from wall-clock time, event order, storage location, or proximity. |
| Credential concerned | The nonsecret credential reference and version the event concerns; no credential-authority event carries secret material. |
| Lifecycle operation recorded | Exactly one of issuance, rotation, revocation, replacement, correction, or a further operation the registered secret-handling policy defines. |
| Predecessor | Every event other than an issuance names the current predecessor authority event it succeeds; an issuance names none and is recorded as the issuance. A correction carries two distinct references — the current predecessor authority event from which it advances currentness, and the corrected record, current or historical, whose content it corrects — and the corrected record never stands in for that predecessor, the two references naming one event only where the corrected record is itself the current predecessor, a correction advancing currentness only from the current event rather than branching from or returning currentness to a historical one, in the dual-reference construction the Section 9.6 record model states. At most one issuance event is accepted for one exact nonsecret credential reference and version, competing initial issuances for that reference and version being decided at the boundary WP2-SEC-017 requires with losers preserved as the rejections that requirement states, so that one credential version never acquires two independent currentness chains. |
| Governing policy | The identity and version of the registered secret-handling policy under which the event was recorded — the registered secret-handling rules charter Section 14.9 requires to be versioned. |
| Recording authority and reason | The authority that recorded the event and the reason for which it was recorded. |
| Authority point at which the operation takes effect | The authority point at which the recorded lifecycle operation takes effect, decided at the boundary WP2-SEC-017 requires and never inferred from wall-clock arrival order. |
| Validity or usability fact established | The validity or usability fact the event establishes, stating which credential versions it leaves usable: more than one version of one credential remains usable at one authority point only where the registered secret-handling policy permits that overlap, that permission neither requiring an overlap nor prohibiting one, and a version that a prevailing revocation or other credential-authority event has made unusable stays unusable unless a further recorded event establishes otherwise. |
| Correction rule | Never rewritten in place: a material error in a credential-authority event is corrected under WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026 by a further recorded event that names, as the Predecessor clause states, both the current predecessor from which it advances currentness and the corrected record. |

**WP2-SEC-010.** Every rotation, revocation, or replacement of a credential **MUST** be recorded through the nonsecret credential reference and version of the credential, provider project, or account scope it affects, as one or more individually recorded credential-authority events, each conforming to the credential-authority event model of this section as WP2-SEC-017 requires and naming the recording authority and the registered secret-handling policy identity and version under which it was recorded, with no secret material in any such record.

**WP2-SEC-011.** A rotation, revocation, or replacement of a credential **MUST NOT** erase, rewrite, or reattribute the historical attribution of any request, usage, or provider-behavior record, each such record remaining attributed to the nonsecret credential reference and version that was current for it under the credential-authority events of this section at the authority point at which the fact it records occurred, the version and the credential-authority event or recorded validity or usability fact WP2-SEC-018 requires that record's operation to name being the attribution it keeps.

**WP2-SEC-017.** Every credential-lifecycle operation — an issuance, rotation, revocation, replacement, or correction, and any further operation the registered secret-handling policy defines — **MUST** be decided at an authority boundary that is atomic, or safe against competing credential-lifecycle operations to an equivalent degree, the operation that prevails being recorded as a credential-authority event conforming to every clause of the credential-authority event model in Section 16.6 — its event identity, operation identity and membership, credential concerned, lifecycle operation recorded, predecessor and corrected-record references, governing policy, recording authority and reason, authority point at which the operation takes effect, validity or usability fact established, and correction rule — two competing operations proceeding from one current predecessor authority event, and two competing initial issuances for one exact nonsecret credential reference and version, never both prevailing, at most one issuance event being accepted for one exact nonsecret credential reference and version, and one credential version never acquiring two independent currentness chains, a losing or stale operation preserved as a distinguishable, recorded rejection naming the exact nonsecret credential reference and version it concerned, the predecessor it claimed where it claimed one — an initial issuance claiming none — the operation it attempted, the authority that attempted it, and the reason it did not prevail, a rejection that is not a credential-authority event of that model, every credential-lifecycle operation carrying an explicit operation identity never reused for another operation, every credential-authority event naming the operation identity that produced it, the complete event membership of an operation recorded as more than one credential-authority event being explicit through those events or through one explicit operation record, all the events of that membership becoming current together or not at all at that boundary, in the all-or-none form WP2-SCHED-013 states for an admission, neither that membership nor that acceptance being reconstructed from wall-clock time, event order, storage location, or proximity, and the currentness and usability of every credential version read from the recorded credential-authority events and the validity or usability facts they establish, never from wall-clock arrival order.

**WP2-SEC-018.** Every provider attempt, and every recorded access to secret material, **MUST** name the exact nonsecret credential reference and version used and the credential-authority event or recorded validity or usability fact that established that version's usability for that operation, no provider dispatch proceeding with a credential version that a prevailing revocation or other credential-authority event has made unusable under the registered secret-handling policy.

**Rationale.** A credential's lifecycle is recorded the way Section 9.6 records authority over a grant — as individually recorded, attributable authority events over a stable identity, never as an in-place rewrite — and the credential-authority event model above states that record form, WP2-SEC-017 being the single requirement that incorporates the model and makes each clause binding, in the way WP2-SCHED-051 binds the assignment-authority record model, so that no individual table cell carries a requirement identifier of its own. A material error in such an event is corrected the way WP2-ID-025 and WP2-ID-026 require, by a further recorded event naming, as two distinct references, the current predecessor from which it advances currentness and the record it corrects — the dual-reference construction whose purpose the Section 9.6 rationale states. The authority boundary WP2-SEC-017 requires closes the race that identity and linkage alone cannot: without it, two competing lifecycle operations could each name one current predecessor authority event and both claim to have succeeded it, leaving no recorded rule to say which rotation, revocation, or replacement prevailed under concurrent operation. An issuance is the one event kind that names no predecessor, so the race between competing initial issuances for one exact nonsecret credential reference and version is named separately at that boundary rather than reached through a predecessor clause that cannot apply to it, at most one issuance being accepted and one credential version never acquiring two independent currentness chains. The operation identity and membership clause is what makes the all-or-none acceptance of a compound operation checkable: every event of one credential-lifecycle operation names the operation that produced it, and the operation's complete event membership is read from those events or from one explicit operation record rather than inferred from wall-clock time, event order, storage location, or proximity. Currentness and usability are read from the recorded events rather than from arrival order, and the overlap question is answered by representation rather than by prohibition: the registered secret-handling policy may leave more than one credential version usable during a rotation, the validity-or-usability clause of the model is where such an authorized overlap is recorded explicitly, and WP2-SEC-018 keeps a version that a prevailing revocation or other credential-authority event has made unusable from authorizing a later provider dispatch, tying every provider attempt and every recorded access to secret material to the credential version it used and the validity or usability fact that permitted the use. WP2-SEC-011 protects the historical half: usage, rate-limit, and correlated-failure evidence is interpretable only against the project scope that was current when each request was made, and a laboratory that reattributed history to the successor scope after a rotation would silently mutate evidence, which charter Section 14.9 requires to be treated as an explicit failure rather than concealed. Versioning the reference is what lets the successor be told apart from the predecessor without either record naming the secret. When rotations occur, and on what schedule, is a matter for the applicable registered secret-handling rules; Section 16.9 records the schedule, and the realization of the authority boundary, as unselected, and WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting a rotation interval.

### 16.7 Auditability without the secret

**WP2-SEC-012.** A conforming laboratory **MUST** keep every access to secret material, every denial of such access, every rotation, revocation, or replacement of a credential, and every violation of a boundary this section requires auditable from records that contain no secret material, each such record carrying the applicable nonsecret credential reference and the provider-dispatch secret boundary identity and version concerned, for each audited access the nonsecret credential reference and version concerned together with the credential-authority event identities relied upon for it under WP2-SEC-018, for each audited denial the nonsecret credential reference and version concerned together with the recorded validity or usability fact, or the absence of one, on which the denial rested, and the attribution WP2-ISO-017 requires of a retained log or diagnostic record.

**Rationale.** An audit trail that contained the secret would widen the boundary it audits: every store, transport, and reader of the trail would join the set of places the secret lives, and the trail could never satisfy WP2-SEC-002. Keeping audit records nonsecret keeps them ordinary records — attributable under WP2-ISO-017 and WP2-ISO-018, joinable through nonsecret credential references under WP2-SEC-007, and preservable under whatever evidentiary treatment Section 13 later assigns them — so that access, denial, rotation, revocation, and violation can be reviewed, and a conformance check exercised, entirely from outside the provider-dispatch secret boundary. Charter Section 14.9 requires the applicable secret-handling rules to be versioned; the credential-lifecycle events of WP2-SEC-010, which name the registered secret-handling policy identity and version, and the detected-secret incidents of WP2-SEC-014, which name the registered secret-handling rule identity and version, are each recorded under an identified, versioned registered secret-handling design object, and WP2-SEC-012 makes every access, denial, and boundary violation auditable against the boundary identity and version concerned.

### 16.8 Detected secret material in candidate output

**WP2-SEC-013.** A conforming laboratory **MUST** refuse ordinary persistence, and admission as evidence, to candidate output — output any component produces that is a candidate for persistence or for admission as evidence, a provider response, a derived artifact, and a log line among them — in which live secret material is detected.

**WP2-SEC-014.** Every refusal WP2-SEC-013 requires **MUST** be recorded as a typed incident record that contains no secret material and that identifies the refused candidate output's source event, the surface on which the material was detected, the recording authority, the registered secret-handling rule identity and version under which the detection was made, and every study, planned run, execution attempt, laboratory worker, model request, provider attempt, and provider session affected, naming each of those that applies and stating the absence of any that does not.

**WP2-SEC-015.** A refusal WP2-SEC-013 requires **MUST NOT** silently delete, silently redact, truncate, or render unverifiable any authoritative evidence, the refused candidate output being withheld from persistence and admission rather than admitted and then silently altered, and everything preservable without persisting the secret material — the incident record, its nonsecret lineage, and every unaffected record — remaining preserved.

**WP2-SEC-016.** Every sanitized derivative produced from candidate output that WP2-SEC-013 required to be refused **MUST** be explicitly identified as sanitized and retain explicit nonsecret lineage to the typed incident record of that refusal and, through it, to the refused source event, without retaining the secret material.

**Rationale.** These four resolve the apparent conflict between two obligations this specification never trades against each other: WP2-SEC-002 prohibits the secret from being persisted, and charter Section 14.9 prohibits evidence loss, mutation, or concealment from passing silently. The resolution is the one WP2-ISO-030 through WP2-ISO-032 establish for a refused operation: refuse, type the refusal, and preserve. Candidate output refused admission never became authoritative evidence, so withholding it deletes nothing authoritative; what the laboratory keeps instead is the typed nonsecret incident record, which preserves the fact, the surface, the attribution, and the rule version of the detection — everything preservable without persisting the secret. Silent redaction is prohibited for the same reason silent deletion is: a record altered without a record of the alteration is a concealed mutation, whereas a sanitized derivative under WP2-SEC-016 is explicit about being sanitized and about the incident it derives from, which keeps later analysis possible without the secret and keeps the derivative from being mistaken for the refused original. What incident types exist, and what dispositions they receive, are assigned to Section 14, consistent with charter Section 14.4's typed dispositions; how incident records are formatted, retained, and sealed is assigned to Section 13; and the detection mechanism itself is recorded as unselected in Section 16.9, because selecting a scanner or a threshold is exactly what WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits.

### 16.9 Options deliberately left unselected

**Unselected option.** Secret custody realization. The provider-dispatch secret boundary may be realized by per-unit credential issuance, an external secret manager, process-scoped injection, or another mechanism, exactly as Section 7.20 records for the boundary WP2-ISO-025 requires. No manager, provider, store, or deployment topology is selected.

**Unselected option.** Provider-authentication egress realization. How the provider-authentication egress WP2-SEC-019 authorizes carries credential or authentication material to the configured provider endpoint for one identified provider attempt may be realized by any arrangement that satisfies this section. No authentication protocol, transport, secret manager, or network topology is selected.

**Unselected option.** Credential storage and encryption. How secret material is stored, encrypted, and made usable within its boundary may be realized by any arrangement that satisfies the requirements of this section. No credential store, encryption mechanism, algorithm, or key-management scheme is selected.

**Unselected option.** Rotation and revocation schedule. Which rotations occur, on what schedule, and under which triggering conditions are matters for the applicable registered secret-handling rules. WP2-SEC-010 and WP2-SEC-011 state what a rotation, revocation, or replacement records and preserves, and WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting a rotation interval or any other numeric operating parameter. The atomic or equivalently safe authority boundary WP2-SEC-017 requires for competing credential-lifecycle operations is of the same family as the scalar-transition boundary WP2-LIFE-034 requires and the grant-operation boundary WP2-SCHED-052 requires, and it may be realized by the same range of arrangements Sections 8.15 and 9.12 record for those boundaries; no primitive, store, arrangement, operation-record schema, or grouping mechanism is selected here either.

**Unselected option.** Detection mechanism. The detection WP2-SEC-013 presupposes may be realized by pattern matching, entropy analysis, provider-issued marker recognition, a registered detector of another kind, or a combination of them, applied under a registered secret-handling rule identity and version. No algorithm, threshold, or scanning tool is selected, and the treatment of a detection failure belongs to the failure taxonomy of Section 14.

**Unselected option.** Session-isolation realization. The isolation WP2-SEC-005 requires may be realized by per-unit provider sessions, by per-unit connections within one boundary, by session-scoped partitioning inside the provider-dispatch secret boundary, or by another arrangement. No connection, pooling, or session mechanism is selected.

**Unselected option.** Nonsecret-reference scheme. The nonsecret credential reference may be realized by a registered mapping, a keyed digest, an authority-issued alias, or another scheme, as Section 10.12 already records for the identity WP2-SCHED-047 requires. No scheme is selected here either, and WP2-SEC-008 states the property any selection has to satisfy.

### 16.10 Boundaries assigned elsewhere

This section states which secret-handling boundaries exist, what may never carry secret material, how credentials and provider sessions remain isolated, how a nonsecret credential reference is issued and held, and how secret-handling events remain auditable and preserved without the secret. It deliberately defines none of the following, each of which the staged instructions assign elsewhere: the incident taxonomy and the typed dispositions of a detected-secret incident, a boundary violation, or a refusal this section requires, which belong to Section 14; the treatment of live secret material detected in a record that has already been persisted or admitted as authoritative evidence, whose incident typing, remediation, and disposition belong to Section 14 and whose evidentiary treatment belongs to Section 13; the evidence format, retention, and sealing treatment of incident records, audit records, and sanitized derivatives, which belong to Section 13; the consequences of a detected-secret incident, a refused admission, or a sanitized derivative for study completeness and for any aggregation surface, which belong to Section 15; and the conformance checks, fault injections, and pass conditions for every requirement stated here, which belong to Section 17 and to the conformance document.

## 17. Conformance model

### 17.1 What this section establishes

This section defines the implementation-neutral conformance model of this specification: what one conformance assessment is assessed against, how the accountability of requirements relates to the conceptual checks that test them, the outcome vocabulary in which every check reports, the seven check classes that stay distinct, the keyless boundary within which implementation acceptance is decided, what conformance evidence identifies, and what a whole-implementation conformance verdict requires and what it never establishes. It answers what every one of Sections 6 through 16 assigns here in its closing boundary statement — the conformance checks, and where those statements name them the fault injections and pass conditions, for every requirement those sections state, which Sections 6.18, 7.21, 8.16, 9.13, 10.13, 11.13, 12.11, 13.14, 14.11, 15.12, and 16.10 assign to this section and to the accompanying conformance document at [`WORK_PACKAGE_002_CONFORMANCE_REQUIREMENTS.md`](./WORK_PACKAGE_002_CONFORMANCE_REQUIREMENTS.md), and which the rationales of Sections 7.18 and 8.14 assign here for how a refusal is exercised in a conformance check. The division between the two documents is fixed here: this section states the model — the properties every conformance assessment has to satisfy — and the conformance document carries the operating principles that follow from this model, the requirement-to-check matrix, the conceptual check catalogue, the fault-injection catalogue, the keyless implementation-acceptance suite, the conformance-run evidence contract, the conformance-side statement of the parity boundary Section 19 states, the unresolved conformance questions, and the conformance acceptance criteria, as a test document that carries no requirement of its own and whose binding force flows from the requirements of this section and from the requirements its entries map, never in the reverse direction. It selects no mechanism: no test framework, runner, harness, concurrency primitive, interleaving-control technology, fake-provider design, pipeline product, venue realization, or numeric operating parameter is selected anywhere in it, and the selections WP2-SCOPE-015 enumerates remain prohibited throughout this document.

Section 17.2 states the conformance-assessment model — exact-version binding, the binding of the conformance document, requirement accountability, requirement-to-check relations, false-pass prevention, inapplicability, and the outcome vocabulary. Section 17.3 states the seven check classes. Section 17.4 states the keyless implementation-acceptance boundary. Section 17.5 states what every recorded check outcome identifies. Section 17.6 states what a whole-implementation conformance verdict requires and what it never establishes. Section 17.7 records options deliberately left unselected, and Section 17.8 records what is assigned elsewhere.

Every substantive statement here is a Required future invariant carrying exactly one `WP2-HAND-###` identifier, a Design rationale introduced by a **Rationale.** lead-in, or an Unselected option introduced by an **Unselected option.** lead-in, in the Section 4.3 sense, or else the definitional statement opening Section 17.2 or the one opening Section 17.6, each of which states how a term is used in this section, in Sections 18 through 21, and in the conformance document, and carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements that cite it, an entry of the check-class table in Section 17.3, its introducing statements included, each of which states how a class is named and read and carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements that cite it, or a boundary statement in Section 17.8. Nothing in this section is a Current fact or a Current limitation; those belong to Section 5, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps a current mechanism from being read as a requirement. Every defined term used here carries the meaning Section 3.1 assigns it, as WP2-SCOPE-005 requires; every lifecycle term carries the meaning Section 8.3 assigns it, as WP2-LIFE-002 requires; and every secret-handling term carries the meaning Section 16.2 assigns it, as WP2-SEC-001 requires.

### 17.2 The conformance-assessment model

Throughout this section, Sections 18 through 21, and the conformance document, a *conformance assessment* is the recorded evaluation of one identified implementation against the requirements of this specification — over one check, one requirement, or the implementation as a whole — and an *implementation-acceptance requirement* is a requirement whose row in the requirement-to-check matrix of the conformance document assigns the implementation-acceptance boundary in either of the two renderings that matrix's boundary discipline defines — the parity-claim-boundary annotation marking what a requirement is about and never removing it from implementation acceptance — a requirement being outside implementation acceptance only where its row assigns a Work Package 3 parity boundary instead. Each of the two names a role in this model, carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements that cite it, and adds no Section 3.1 term.

**WP2-HAND-001.** Every conformance assessment under this specification **MUST** be conducted against, and every outcome it records be bound to, one exact main-specification identity and version, one exact conformance-document identity and version, and one exact implementation build and source identity, each named explicitly in the assessment's records, an assessment against an unidentified, mixed, or superseded version of any of the three establishing conformance for none of them.

**WP2-HAND-002.** Every conformance assessment **MUST** be conducted under a conformance document whose requirement-to-check matrix carries a requirement-ID set exactly equal to the requirement-ID set of the exact main-specification version the assessment is bound to under WP2-HAND-001 — no requirement identifier missing, none duplicated, none extra, no range standing in for enumerated identifiers, and no implicit coverage standing in for an explicit row — and whose every row relates its requirement to at least one conceptual check defined in that document.

**WP2-HAND-003.** The conceptual-check, fault-entry, fixture, suite-case, and unresolved-question identifiers of the conformance document **MUST NOT** be cited, recorded, or relied upon as normative requirement identifiers of this specification, each being a test-document identifier that names a conformance-document entry, visibly distinct from every `WP2-*` requirement namespace Section 4.2 reserves, and conferring no normative force of its own — the normative force of every conformance obligation flowing from the requirements of this specification alone.

**Rationale.** Version `1.0.0` supplies the exact main-specification identity needed for a later conformance assessment, but version assignment does not authorize such an assessment and none is performed or claimed here. The conformance document is a distinct versioned test document: its matrix, catalogue, fault, suite, evidence, question, and acceptance-criteria entries elaborate the requirements of this section and the requirements they map, and its force flows from this section — WP2-HAND-002 binds its matrix, WP2-HAND-009 its outcome vocabulary, WP2-HAND-012 its keyless boundary — never from the conformance document toward this specification. Section 4.2 records that every requirement of the two mandatory Section 4.1 classes receives a corresponding entry there, and that a requirement governing the specification package rather than an implementation may receive a document-audit check; WP2-HAND-002 makes that correspondence exact and testable as set equality, and WP2-HAND-003 keeps the two identifier systems from ever being mistaken for one another.

**WP2-HAND-004.** Every normative requirement of this specification **MUST** be independently accountable in every conformance assessment whose scope reaches it — assessed through the explicit requirement-to-check relations of its own matrix row, with its own recorded outcome and its own recorded evidence references — even where one conceptual check covers several requirements or several requirements share fixtures, faults, evidence, or a venue, the recorded outcome of one requirement never standing in for, or being derived by assumption from, the recorded outcome of another.

**WP2-HAND-005.** A requirement whose clauses can fail independently **MUST** be related to conceptual checks that together reach every independently falsifiable clause of it, one requirement being related to several checks wherever one check cannot falsify every clause, and a passing outcome for such a requirement being recordable only where every one of its related checks other than a check of the later registered-study evidence class supports it.

**WP2-HAND-006.** One conceptual check **MUST NOT** be treated as covering more than one requirement except where every requirement-to-check relation it participates in is stated explicitly in the matrix row of every requirement it covers, coverage arising only through those stated relations and never through inference, adjacency, shared fixtures, or an implicit reading of a neighboring row.

**WP2-HAND-007.** The absence of a recorded failure, absent evidence, an unrun check, a blocked check, and a check whose triggering condition, injected fault, or contended race did not in fact occur **MUST NOT** be recorded, reported, or relied upon as a pass — of the check, of any requirement it relates to, or of any verdict resting on it — in any conformance assessment.

**WP2-HAND-008.** An inapplicability outcome **MUST NOT** be recorded for any requirement or check except where the requirement governing the assessed conduct itself permits inapplicability, and then only with an explicit recorded basis identifying the permitting language and the condition that satisfies it, the recorded basis identifying the observation from which that condition was confirmed for the assessed conduct, an outcome lacking that recorded basis being blocked rather than inapplicable.

**WP2-HAND-009.** Every recorded check outcome of a conformance assessment **MUST** distinguish, at minimum, pass, fail, blocked or not run, and explicitly justified inapplicability, each recorded as the distinct outcome it is and never collapsed into one scalar result, score, count, ratio, or overall impression.

**Rationale.** The four outcomes answer different questions: pass and fail report what a check established, blocked or not run reports that it established nothing, and explicitly justified inapplicability reports that the governing requirement itself withdraws the question for the assessed conduct. Collapsing them into one scalar is the mechanism by which absence becomes a pass, which WP2-HAND-007 prohibits; the vocabulary keeps them apart in the way WP2-EVID-019 keeps the states of an evidence class distinct rather than collapsed, and WP2-SCOPE-007's conditional form is the model for WP2-HAND-008: an inapplicability is a property the governing requirement itself grants, never a judgment the assessment invents.

### 17.3 The seven check classes

The table below names the seven check classes of this model and states how each is read. The classes classify conceptual checks; they select no venue realization, runner, or technology, and Section 17.7 records those realizations as unselected.

| Check class | What a check of this class establishes |
| --- | --- |
| Static document audit | Examination of documents, registers, plans, registrations, matrices, and records, without executing the implementation — the class under which a requirement that governs this specification package rather than an implementation is checked, as Section 4.2 anticipates. |
| Deterministic keyless execution | Execution of the implementation over a deterministic path with fixed, identified inputs and no live provider credential, whose expected observations are reproducible from the fixtures alone. |
| Concurrency and race testing | Execution in which the material interleavings of competing operations are forced or systematically enumerated under the discipline WP2-HAND-011 states, so that the race outcome observed is the one the check made happen rather than one that happened to occur. |
| Restart and recovery testing | Execution in which work is interrupted and re-entered — a process restart, an orchestrator restart, a laboratory-worker crash, a resume, or a recovery among the interruptions — observing what is preserved, what is refused, and what is recorded across the interruption. |
| Fake-provider testing | Execution against a substitute provider that answers from local logic, holds no live secret material, and dispatches to no live provider, exercising request, accounting, failure, and provider-behavior paths without any live credential. |
| Evidence-integrity testing | Controlled omission, duplication, substitution, mutation, or damage applied to evidence, inventories, seals, packages, or integrity bases, establishing that the damage is detected and surfaced as the typed outcomes the applicable requirements state rather than concealed. |
| Later registered-study evidence | Evidence produced by a separately authorized, registered study executed after implementation acceptance — the one class that is not available for implementation acceptance itself, confined by WP2-HAND-013 to supplementing a keyless check and kept by WP2-HAND-014 from ever being an implementation-acceptance pass condition where it is Work Package 3 parity activity. |

**WP2-HAND-010.** Every conceptual check of the conformance document **MUST** be assigned exactly one of the seven check classes of Section 17.3 — static document audit, deterministic keyless execution, concurrency and race testing, restart and recovery testing, fake-provider testing, evidence-integrity testing, and later registered-study evidence — the seven classes remaining distinct wherever they are recorded, none merged into, renamed as, or silently subsumed by another.

**WP2-HAND-011.** A check of the concurrency and race testing class **MUST** force, or systematically enumerate at the conceptual level, the material interleavings of the contention it tests, an outcome reached under ordinary scheduling luck, an unforced interleaving, or a contention that never in fact arose supporting no pass of that check.

### 17.4 The keyless implementation-acceptance boundary

**WP2-HAND-012.** Implementation acceptance under this specification, and every conceptual check and pass condition on which an implementation-acceptance outcome rests, **MUST NOT** require a live provider credential, a repository game-model call, a browser experiment, or any Work Package 3 study, every fixture such a check assumes being synthetic and non-live in the Section 16.2 sense, so that no check requires what WP2-SEC-002 prohibits any surface from carrying.

**WP2-HAND-013.** Later registered-study evidence **MUST NOT** substitute, silently or expressly, for the keyless static or dynamic check of an implementation-acceptance requirement — such evidence supplements a keyless check where a separately authorized registered study later produces evidence bearing on the same requirement, an implementation-acceptance outcome whose only support is later registered-study evidence is no pass, and the absence of such evidence never withholds a current passing basis from a requirement whose keyless checks support it.

**WP2-HAND-014.** A Work Package 3 parity check, parity result, or parity-eligibility determination **MUST NOT** be, or be a constituent of, a Work Package 2 implementation-acceptance pass condition, the parity boundary remaining where WP2-SCOPE-011 and Section 19 place it and the claim boundary where WP2-SCOPE-012 places it.

**Rationale.** The keyless boundary is not conjectural: Section 5.12 records as current fact that the repository's rehearsals are keyless by construction, exercising strict finalization, replay, sealing, failure handling, and recovery without any credential, which is evidence that a keyless path can reach the conduct this contract governs — and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps that observation from being read as the required mechanism, no runner, harness, or venue realization being selected here, as Section 17.7 records. The boundary also keeps the three gates distinct wherever this section is applied: a keyless implementation-acceptance pass is an instrument of future implementation acceptance alone, among the gates Section 20 states; Work Package 2 documentation-package acceptance is not reached by any conformance outcome; and Work Package 3 parity eligibility and evidence remain separately authorized and preregistered, as Section 19 states and WP2-SCOPE-011, WP2-SCOPE-012, and WP2-SCOPE-014 already bind.

### 17.5 Conformance-evidence identification

**WP2-HAND-015.** Every recorded check outcome of every conformance assessment **MUST** identify the exact requirement or requirements assessed, the conceptual check applied, the fixtures used, the injected fault where any and an explicit clean-path statement where none, the exact implementation build and source identity, the exact main-specification and conformance-document identities and versions of WP2-HAND-001, the environment in which the check was evaluated, the outcome in the vocabulary WP2-HAND-009 states, and the source evidence supporting that outcome, each referenced sufficiently to locate and re-evaluate it.

**Rationale.** The identification repeats WP2-HAND-001's exact-version binding at the level of every recorded outcome because a verdict is only as re-derivable as its parts: an outcome that cannot say what it tested, against which versions, with which fixtures, under which fault, in which environment, and on what evidence supports nothing, and a later reader has to be able to reconstruct the assessment from its records rather than from trust. What the complete evidence package of a future conformance run contains, and how it is sealed and preserved, is the conformance-run evidence contract the conformance document states under this requirement; nothing here makes conformance evidence study evidence, and Section 19 states that boundary for research claims.

### 17.6 The whole-implementation verdict

Throughout this section, Sections 18 through 21, and the conformance document, a *whole-implementation conformance verdict* is the recorded conclusion that one identified implementation, at one exact build and source identity, conforms to one exact main-specification version under one exact conformance-document version, and a *current passing basis* for a requirement is a recorded passing check outcome identified as WP2-HAND-015 requires, bound to those same three exact identities, supported by every one of that requirement's related checks other than a check of the later registered-study evidence class, whose evidence WP2-HAND-013 makes supplementary, as WP2-HAND-005 requires, and not superseded by any later recorded outcome for the same requirement and the same implementation build. Each of the two statements names how a term is used here, carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements that cite it, and adds no Section 3.1 term.

**WP2-HAND-016.** A whole-implementation conformance verdict **MUST NOT** be recorded, reported, or relied upon unless every implementation-acceptance requirement of the exact main-specification version concerned holds, at the time the verdict is recorded, a current passing basis in the Section 17.6 sense — or the explicitly justified inapplicability outcome WP2-HAND-008 permits, where that requirement's own language permits inapplicability — a fail, blocked, not-run, or not-yet-assessed outcome for any one of them leaving the verdict unavailable rather than partial, qualified, or presumed.

**WP2-HAND-017.** A whole-implementation conformance verdict, and every constituent conformance outcome, **MUST NOT** be cited, recorded, or reported as establishing behavioral quality, believability, semantic correctness, observer value, the scientific validity of any study, serial-versus-parallel behavioral parity, neutrality, or interchangeability, the success of any candidate cognitive architecture, Work Package 2 documentation-package acceptance, or authorization to implement, deploy, operate, register, execute, or begin Work Package 3 activity — a verdict establishing infrastructure conformance against the exact identities WP2-HAND-001 binds and nothing beyond it.

**Rationale.** Section 2.3 states what infrastructure conformance does not establish, charter Section 8.5 states directly that throughput does not establish scientific validity and that parallel execution is not presumed behaviorally neutral, and WP2-SCOPE-011, WP2-SCOPE-012, and WP2-SCOPE-014 already bind the reporting of conformance results; WP2-HAND-017 makes the same discipline testable at the verdict itself, where the temptation to overclaim concentrates. The verdict is deliberately all-or-nothing over the implementation-acceptance requirements: WP2-HAND-016 leaves room for partial progress reports as ordinary assessment records under WP2-HAND-009's vocabulary, but a whole-implementation verdict that tolerated an unresolved requirement would be the scalar collapse WP2-HAND-009 prohibits, performed at the largest scale.

### 17.7 Options deliberately left unselected

**Unselected option.** Conformance runner, harness, and venue realization. How a conceptual check is realized as an executable or auditable procedure, which runner or harness evaluates it, and how the static, local, and continuous-integration venues the conformance document names are provided — locally, in a hosted pipeline, or in another arrangement — are unselected. WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting a workflow framework or topology, and the properties any realization has to satisfy are stated in WP2-HAND-001 through WP2-HAND-017. No test framework, runner, harness, pipeline product, or venue realization is selected.

**Unselected option.** Fake-provider realization. How the fake-provider testing class of Section 17.3 is realized — the substitute provider's form, interface, and behavior model — is unselected, provided the realization dispatches to no live provider and holds no live secret material, as WP2-HAND-012 and Section 16 require. No fake-provider design, product, or protocol is selected.

**Unselected option.** Interleaving control. How the concurrency and race testing class of Section 17.3 forces or enumerates material interleavings — deterministic scheduling, controlled interception points, conceptual enumeration over the model, or another technique — is unselected, provided the discipline WP2-HAND-011 states is satisfied. No concurrency primitive, scheduler, or interception technology is selected.

### 17.8 Boundaries assigned elsewhere

This section states the conformance model: what an assessment binds to, how requirements and checks relate, which outcomes exist, which check classes stay distinct, where the keyless boundary lies, what conformance evidence identifies, and what a whole-implementation verdict requires and never establishes. It deliberately defines none of the following: the requirement-to-check matrix, the conceptual check catalogue, the fault-injection catalogue, the keyless implementation-acceptance suite, the conformance-run evidence contract, the unresolved conformance questions, and the conformance acceptance criteria, which the accompanying conformance document carries under the requirements of this section; the implementation handoff boundary and the consolidated register of options deliberately left unselected, which Section 18 states; the Work Package 3 dependency boundary and the evidence-versus-study separation for research claims, which Section 19 states; the acceptance gates that keep documentation-package acceptance, implementation acceptance, and Work Package 3 parity distinct, which Section 20 states; and the open-questions register and its discipline, which Section 21 states.

## 18. Implementation handoff and unselected options

### 18.1 What this section establishes

This section defines the implementation handoff boundary of this specification: the one canonical implementation-decision register in which every technical choice Sections 1 through 17 deliberately leave open is recorded and kept completely accounted for, how that register is read, and the conditions under which a future, separately authorized implementation effort takes this contract up. The register consolidates, as canonical decisions, every block Sections 1 through 17 introduce with the **Unselected option.** lead-in of Section 4.3 and the selections WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits this specification from making, and the handoff boundary states what completion of this specification does and does not change — what a future implementation plan has to identify, what no implementation may silently reinterpret, what any selected mechanism has to satisfy independent of its technology, which changes fall under the charter's versioning and registration rules, and what implementation acceptance rests on. It is implementation-neutral: it records decision subjects, sources, required properties, option families, tradeoffs, evidence, authorities, and version consequences, and it selects, ranks, prefers, and recommends nothing — no cloud provider, container platform, job queue, broker, scheduler, database, object store, workflow framework, browser or headless split, serialization or archive technology, deployment topology, or numeric operating parameter, the selections WP2-SCOPE-015 enumerates remaining prohibited throughout this document.

It answers the questions the earlier sections expressly assign here.
