Sections 12.11, 13.14, 14.11, and 15.12 assign the implementation handoff and the ranking of the options each of those sections leaves unselected, which Sections 18.3 and 18.4 state — the ranking arriving here as a canonical decision structure rather than as a ranking, because no technical option of this document is ranked, preferred, or selected: what the register adds to an unselected option is the record a future selection needs — its sources, affected requirements, required property, option families, tradeoffs, evidence, effect classification, version consequence, and decision authority — and the selection itself remains with the decision authority each entry states, exercisable only under the boundary Section 18.4 states and never within this specification package. Every block Sections 1 through 17 introduce with the **Unselected option.** lead-in is a source of the register whether or not its section states that assignment expressly — the blocks of the options subsections 6.17, 7.20, 8.15, 9.12, 10.12, 11.12, 12.10, 13.13, 14.10, 15.11, 16.9, and 17.7 among them, together with the block Section 12.9 states inline — and WP2-SCOPE-015's consolidated prohibition remains in force over every entry: recording a decision here neither performs nor licenses the selection it prohibits this specification from making.

Every substantive statement here is a Required future invariant carrying exactly one `WP2-HAND-###` identifier or a Design rationale introduced by a **Rationale.** lead-in, in the Section 4.3 sense, or else a clause of the implementation-decision register in Section 18.3 — its introducing statements and its source-coverage table included — whose normative force flows through WP2-HAND-018 rather than through an identifier of its own, an entry of the field table in Section 18.2, which states how the register is read and carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements that cite it, a reading-contract or register-key statement of Section 18.2, which is definitional and carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements that cite it, the orienting statement that opens Section 18.4, which carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements of that subsection, or a boundary statement in Section 18.5. The register in Section 18.3 is the one structured block of this section that carries normative clauses; WP2-HAND-018 is its single incorporation requirement, WP2-HAND-019 states the coverage discipline its source-coverage table has to satisfy, and no other table of this section carries a hidden or unbound obligation. This section introduces no new technical option: the register records, by citation and consolidation, the options Sections 1 through 17 already leave open, and nothing here adds one. Nothing in this section is a Current fact or a Current limitation; those belong to Section 5, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps a current mechanism from being read as a requirement. Every defined term used here carries the meaning Section 3.1 assigns it, as WP2-SCOPE-005 requires.

### 18.2 How the implementation-decision register is read

Each canonical entry of the register in Section 18.3 states the same ten fields in the same order. The register supplies the canonical record of every technical choice this specification deliberately leaves open, which WP2-HAND-018 incorporates and makes binding clause by clause — the selection itself excepted, because the selection is exactly what every entry leaves unmade.

Each canonical entry carries a register key of the form `HD-##`, and the register's source-coverage table cites each source occurrence by a key of the form `SRC-##` together with the subsection that states it. Both are expressly register keys and not requirement identifiers: they belong to no requirement-ID namespace of Section 4.2, they are never cited as requirements, no conformance obligation attaches to a key rather than to a requirement, and the register's normative force flows through the requirements of this section rather than through any key. A *source occurrence*, throughout this section, is one block that a subsection of Sections 1 through 17 introduces with the **Unselected option.** lead-in of Section 4.3, wherever that block stands — in a section's options subsection or inline in another subsection. A canonical decision of the register is *blocking* for a given activity where the latest decision point its entry states falls at or before that activity, and where an entry states more than one latest decision point each governs the activity it names; a canonical decision is accordingly blocking for implementation acceptance exactly where a latest decision point its entry states falls at or before the conformance assessment that exercises its affected requirements, and an entry whose only stated latest decision point is the registration of a study or a later evidentiary run is blocking for that registration or run instead.

| Field | What the entry states |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The one technical choice the entry records as deliberately unselected. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | Every subsection of Sections 1 through 17 whose source occurrence or selection prohibition the entry consolidates, and every requirement ID whose obligations constrain, or are realized by, any future selection of the decision. |
| Required property or invariant | What any selection has to satisfy for the affected requirements to remain satisfiable, stated by citation to the requirements that carry it rather than as a new obligation. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | The families of mechanisms that could realize the decision, described neutrally; naming a family selects, ranks, and recommends nothing. |
| Principal tradeoffs | The principal consequences that distinguish the option families from one another, stated without preferring any of them. |
| Evidence needed to choose | What a future implementation effort would have to measure, prototype, audit, or otherwise establish for the selection to be made on recorded grounds rather than by impression. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Whether a selection can influence a treatment, a condition, or observable simulated behavior, only operational properties such as cost, latency, capacity, or reliability, or both. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | The version consequence of selecting or later changing the decision — which of an implementation version, a registered treatment version, a registered condition version, or an experiment version the change requires, charter Sections 14.2 and 14.3 governing wherever a treatment can be affected. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The role answerable for making the selection, and the latest boundary in a future implementation effort at which the selection can still be made without blocking that effort. |
| Current status | The entry's present state. Every canonical decision of this register is unselected; no selection exists within this specification package, and the boundary in Section 18.4 governs how a future selection is made and recorded. |

**WP2-HAND-018.** Every canonical implementation decision of the register in Section 18.3 that a future implementation plan, a later explicit ruling, or any activity governed by this specification selects, resolves, defers, cites, or relies upon **MUST** be treated in conformity with every clause of the implementation-decision register in Section 18.3 — its introducing statements, its source-coverage table, and, for the applicable entry, its decision subject, its source sections and affected requirement IDs, its required property or invariant, its plausible implementation-neutral option families, its principal tradeoffs, its evidence needed to choose, its statement of whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both, its statement of whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version, its decision authority and latest decision point, and its current status — except only the selection that the entry expressly leaves unmade to the decision authority it states, an exemption that reaches no other clause of that attribute or entry.

**WP2-HAND-019.** The implementation-decision register in Section 18.3 **MUST** cover its sources completely and exclusively: every source occurrence in Sections 1 through 17 maps, in the source-coverage table, to exactly one canonical entry; no source occurrence is unmapped; a canonical entry consolidating more than one source occurrence lists every consolidated source subsection and every affected requirement ID; and no canonical entry stands without either at least one mapped source occurrence or a directly necessary handoff rationale stated in the entry itself.

**Rationale.** WP2-HAND-018 is the single requirement that incorporates the register and makes every non-deferred clause binding, in the way WP2-ID-027 binds the entity register, WP2-LIFE-001 binds the lifecycle models, WP2-SCHED-051 binds the assignment-authority record model, WP2-MODEL-001 binds the model-request record register, WP2-SEC-017 binds the credential-authority event model, and WP2-EVID-001 binds the evidence-record register, so that no individual table cell carries a requirement identifier of its own and no cell commands. What the incorporation exempts is deliberate and narrow: the one clause a register entry does not state is the selection, because stating it is what WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits and what the decision authority of each entry later does under the boundary Section 18.4 states. WP2-HAND-019 exists because handoff is where an unselected option could otherwise quietly disappear or quietly become chosen: complete mapping makes an omitted option visible as an unmapped source occurrence, and the sourceless-entry prohibition makes an invented decision visible as an entry with neither a source nor a stated handoff ground. An authority named in the register is a role — the party answerable for a future selection — and never a component, service, store, or product, exactly as Section 6.2 keeps every authority in the entity register a role; a required property or invariant cited in an entry adds no obligation beyond the requirements it cites, consistent with Section 4.3's rule that a required invariant is created only where the section assigned that invariant states it; and the field table above states how the register is read and carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements that cite it.

### 18.3 The implementation-decision register

One register entry follows for each of the thirty-seven canonical implementation decisions this specification deliberately leaves unselected, HD-01 through HD-37 — HD-01 through HD-36 ordered by the earliest appearance of each decision's source occurrences in Sections 6 through 17, and the sourceless entry HD-37 standing last. An HD key is a register key of this section and not a requirement identifier: it names one entry of this register, and the normative force of the register flows through the incorporation requirement this section states rather than through any HD key or table cell, no cell of this register carrying an obligation of its own.

Each entry states the same ten fields in the same order: the decision subject; the source section or sections and the affected requirement IDs its source blocks name, or, for the one sourceless entry, the directly necessary handoff ground on which it stands together with its affected requirement IDs; the required property or invariant any selection has to satisfy; the plausible implementation-neutral option families; the principal tradeoffs; the evidence needed to choose; whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both; whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version; the decision authority and latest decision point; and the current status, which is unselected for every entry of this register.

A canonical entry consolidates every source occurrence that leaves the same decision open, and lists every one of them. A source occurrence whose one block records two coupled questions maps to exactly one entry — the entry covering its titled subject — and the entry receiving it covers the whole block, the entries it touches by citation cross-referencing one another without claiming one another's sources; the realization family of the atomic or equivalently safe authority boundaries, which four further source blocks incorporate by citation — SRC-19 and SRC-39 citing the arrangements Section 9.12 records for the authoritative-commit boundary, and SRC-63 and SRC-68 citing the arrangements Sections 8.15 and 9.12 record — is consolidated this way under HD-10. No entry ranks or prefers an option family; a mechanism class a source block names — a message broker, a database transaction, an event-keyed hash among them — is a neutral example member of an option family in the Section 4.3 sense, never a selection, and where a source block itself marks a technique as an example, the entry preserves that example status expressly. One entry, HD-37, stands on the directly necessary handoff rationale its own entry states rather than on a mapped source occurrence; every other entry is sourced. The coverage table that closes this register assigns a source key, SRC-01 through SRC-74, to each of the seventy-four unselected-option source occurrences of Sections 6 through 17 in document order, each identified by its section and its bold lead-in subject, and maps each occurrence to exactly one canonical entry, so that the mapping is total and no occurrence maps twice.

**HD-01 — Identity value form and uniqueness strategy.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The concrete value form of every identity the Section 6 identity contract requires, and the strategy by which uniqueness within each stated identity scope is achieved. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-01 (Section 6.17, Identifier form); SRC-04 (Section 6.17, Namespace disambiguation strategy). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-ID-001, WP2-ID-003, WP2-ID-022. |
| Required property or invariant | Any selection has to satisfy the properties WP2-ID-001, WP2-ID-003, and WP2-ID-022 state, whatever value form carries the identity: the stated uniqueness within the stated identity scope, with identities that remain stable and reference-safe across all studies and all concurrent execution. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | A randomly generated value; a content-derived digest; a hierarchical composite of parent identities; an authority-issued sequence; a globally unique value; composition from parent identities; an authority-enforced reservation; another form or strategy entirely. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Random values need no issuance coordination but carry no readable structure and rest uniqueness on entropy; content-derived digests give content addressability but tie the identity to a canonicalization scheme and to content availability at issuance time; hierarchical composites make lineage readable in the identity itself but couple every child identity to its parents' form; authority-issued sequences are compact and orderable but make the issuing authority a contention and availability surface under concurrency. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The expected identity volume and concurrent-issuance profile of the implementation; a collision and reuse analysis for each candidate form at that profile; the audit-readability needs of the evidence contract; and the interaction with the issuance and reservation mechanism HD-02 registers. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. Identity values attribute, join, and reference records; they enter no treatment content. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version. No new treatment, condition, or experiment version, provided the required properties continue to hold, an identity form being attribution rather than treatment content. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan the handoff boundary of this section requires, under the charter's versioning and registration rules; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements against the implementation build embodying the selection. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-02 — Identity issuance and reservation mechanism.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The mechanism that discharges the identity-issuing role, and the authenticated creation or reservation transaction that binds a location to its expected evidence-root identity. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-02 (Section 6.17, Issuance and reservation mechanism); SRC-07 (Section 7.20, Creation or reservation transaction). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-ISO-028, WP2-SCOPE-015. |
| Required property or invariant | The Section 6 register names each issuing authority as a role, never a mechanism, and WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting the mechanism; the transaction WP2-ISO-028 refers to has to bind the location to the expected evidence-root identity in a way the laboratory can later authenticate. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | A declaration in source; a registration file; a service; a database; a filesystem or object-store transaction; an exclusive-create filesystem operation; a conditional object-store write; a database insertion; an authority-issued reservation; another mechanism. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Source or registration-file issuance is fully auditable in version control but makes each admission a source change and serializes issuance; a service or database issues concurrently but adds an availability and integrity surface the evidence contract has to account for; exclusive-create and conditional-write transactions give a single authoritative collision answer without a separate issuer, but their atomicity semantics differ by storage class and have to be authenticated per class. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The expected concurrent-issuance and admission profile; the documented atomicity and authentication guarantees of each candidate transaction; and the conformance results of the checks that exercise creation, reservation, and collision-refusal paths conceptually. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version only, provided the binding and authentication property continues to hold. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan, under the charter's versioning and registration rules; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-03 — Lineage and linkage reference representation.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | How a required lineage reference, and the explicit linkage among the records of one incident event, are represented and carried. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-03 (Section 6.17, Lineage record placement); SRC-55 (Section 14.10, Incident-linkage representation). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-ID-023, WP2-FAIL-004. |
| Required property or invariant | The reference and the linkage have to be explicit and readable from the record, as WP2-ID-023 and WP2-FAIL-004 state; where model-request lineage is written remains Section 12's question, exactly as the source block records. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Inline on the child record or on each record of the event; a separate lineage or linking record; a shared incident identity carried on each record; an index; more than one of those. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Inline references keep each record self-describing at the cost of repetition; separate records and indexes give one join surface but make readability depend on that surface's availability and integrity; carrying more than one representation adds redundancy that the correction discipline has to keep consistent. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The join and audit access patterns of the implementation's record stores; the cost of keeping duplicate references consistent under the applicable correction rules; and the conformance results of the checks that exercise lineage readability. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version only. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-04 — Isolation namespace and partitioning realization.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | How the isolation namespace is realized, and how authoritative partitioning of unit-scoped records is realized inside a shared mutable service. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-05 (Section 7.20, Namespace realization); SRC-06 (Section 7.20, Partitioning mechanism in a shared mutable service). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-ISO-002, WP2-ISO-007, WP2-ID-018. |
| Required property or invariant | Any selection has to satisfy the namespace property WP2-ISO-007 states under the identity-rather-than-location discipline WP2-ID-018 states, and the authoritative partitioning WP2-ISO-002 requires for unit-scoped records; no storage class, layout, key format, or naming convention is selected. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | A directory tree; an object-store prefix; a database schema; a tenant; separate stores; per-unit keys; row-level ownership; tenancy; another arrangement or mechanism. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Physically separate stores isolate most strongly at the highest provisioning and operational cost; logical partitioning by key or row is light but rests isolation on uniform enforcement at every access path; tenancy delegates enforcement to the service at the cost of coupling to that service's capability model. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The store classes the implementation adopts under HD-09; the enforcement guarantees each candidate arrangement documents; and the conformance results of the cross-unit and cross-study isolation checks, including the contamination fault classes the conformance document catalogues conceptually. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version only, provided the isolation properties continue to hold. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-05 — Unit-claim and assignment-authority grant mechanism.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The mechanism by which units of work are claimed, where the laboratory claims units at all, and by which the assignment authority's grant is realized as an explicit, revocable record. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-08 (Section 7.20, Claim mechanism); SRC-19 (Section 9.12, Assignment-authority mechanism). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-ISO-020, WP2-ISO-021, WP2-SCHED-013, WP2-SCHED-030, WP2-SCHED-052, WP2-SCOPE-015 — WP2-SCHED-030 entering through the authoritative-commit option SRC-19 cites for the admission-bundle and grant-operation boundaries. |
| Required property or invariant | Any selection has to satisfy the claim properties WP2-ISO-020 and WP2-ISO-021 state and the grant-record property WP2-SCHED-013 states, grant operations deciding at the atomic or equivalently safe boundary WP2-SCHED-052 requires; no lease duration, renewal interval, expiry rule, clock, store, or representation of the authority fact is selected, WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibiting every numeric operating parameter. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | A lock; a time-bounded lease; a fencing token; an ownership record; a conditional write; another explicit, revocable record. The admission-bundle and grant-operation boundary realizations the source block incorporates by citation belong to the family HD-10 registers. |
| Principal tradeoffs | A time-bounded lease recovers abandoned units without intervention but stakes correctness on expiry and clock discipline, the stale-lease family of faults; a fencing token excludes stale writers wherever the token is verified but requires that verification at every authority boundary; a plain ownership record or conditional write is simple and durable but recovers liveness only through a separate mechanism such as the one HD-12 registers. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Contention and laboratory-worker-failure profiles; the guarantees candidate stores document; the liveness mechanism selected under HD-12; and the conformance results of the duplicate-claim, stale-lease, and grant-race checks the conformance document specifies conceptually, with their material interleavings enumerated rather than left to timing luck. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations, the realized scheduling remaining part of the recorded concurrency provenance Section 10 requires; a selection that alters the registered concurrency profile is compared under the rule WP2-SCHED-048 names. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version; no new treatment, condition, or experiment version of itself, the profile-comparison discipline of Section 10 governing any profile-affecting consequence. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-06 — Stochastic-input identity scheme.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The scheme by which stochastic inputs acquire the event-level lineage the isolation contract requires. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-09 (Section 7.20, Stochastic-input identity scheme). Affected requirement ID named by that block: WP2-ISO-012. |
| Required property or invariant | Any selection has to satisfy the event-level lineage WP2-ISO-012 requires — lineage sufficient to establish which stochastic events correspond across the paired units, or that a counterpart is absent — together with the before-execution fixing of the scheme, derivation rule, master inputs, and branch-conditional rule that the same requirement assigns to the registered design. Charter Section 11.3 names event-keyed hashing as one possible technique rather than a selected one, and this entry preserves that example status. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Event-keyed hashing, in the charter's example status; registered per-event noise tables; another scheme satisfying the lineage property. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Event-keyed derivation stores no tables and reproduces draws from identities alone, but fixes every draw to the keying scheme's exact inputs; registered per-event noise tables make draws inspectable and registrable in advance, but add registered artifacts whose custody and size the evidence contract has to carry; other schemes trade the same axes of storage, inspectability, and coupling. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The registered study designs' reproducibility and inspection needs; the evidence-size consequence of any stored-table family under the applicable retention policies; and the conformance results of the checks that exercise stochastic-input lineage and replay. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Both. The scheme fixes which stochastic inputs an event in fact receives, so two conforming schemes still realize different draws; the selection is registered, treatment-relevant design content rather than an interchangeable operational detail. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new treatment, condition, or experiment version under the charter's versioning and registration rules, in addition to a new implementation version. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The registered study design whose registration charter Section 14.3 governs, the implementation supplying its realization; latest decision point: registration of the first study whose events rely on the scheme. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-07 — Log and diagnostic destination and transport.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The destination, transport, and arrangement in which attributed log and diagnostic output is carried. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-10 (Section 7.20, Log destination and transport). Affected requirement IDs named by that block: WP2-ISO-017, WP2-ISO-018. |
| Required property or invariant | Any selection has to satisfy the attribution properties WP2-ISO-017 and WP2-ISO-018 state; no destination, transport, format, or retention rule is selected. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | A per-unit stream; a shared stream with per-record attribution; a structured record store; another arrangement. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Per-unit streams give physical separation and self-evident attribution at the cost of many destinations to manage and inventory; a shared stream centralizes collection but rests attribution on per-record discipline at every writer; a structured record store makes querying and joining cheap but adds a schema and availability surface of its own. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The diagnostic volume and fan-in expected under concurrent execution; the evidentiary weight the registered evidence contract assigns diagnostic records under Section 13; and the conformance results of the attribution checks. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version only. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-08 — Provider-dispatch secret boundary realization: custody, storage, egress, and session isolation.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | How the provider-dispatch secret boundary is realized end to end: the custody of secret material, its storage and encryption within the boundary, the authorized provider-authentication egress, and session isolation between units. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-11 (Section 7.20, Secret custody); SRC-65 (Section 16.9, Secret custody realization); SRC-66 (Section 16.9, Provider-authentication egress realization); SRC-67 (Section 16.9, Credential storage and encryption); SRC-70 (Section 16.9, Session-isolation realization). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-ISO-025, WP2-SEC-005, WP2-SEC-019 — SRC-67 naming no individual requirement and deferring to the requirements of Section 16 as a body. |
| Required property or invariant | Any selection has to satisfy the boundary property WP2-ISO-025 requires, the session isolation WP2-SEC-005 requires, the egress authorization WP2-SEC-019 states for one identified provider attempt, and every other requirement of Section 16 as a body; no provider, manager, store, deployment topology, authentication protocol, transport, encryption mechanism, algorithm, or key-management scheme is selected. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Per-unit credential issuance; an external secret manager, as a family with no manager or provider named; process-scoped injection; per-unit provider sessions; per-unit connections within one boundary; session-scoped partitioning inside the provider-dispatch secret boundary; any storage, encryption, and key-management arrangement satisfying Section 16; any egress arrangement satisfying Section 16; another mechanism. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Per-unit issuance minimizes the blast radius of any one exposure but multiplies issuance and rotation traffic; a central manager family concentrates rotation, custody, and audit but becomes an availability and trust dependency; process-scoped injection is operationally simple but widens in-process exposure; finer session isolation lowers cross-unit contamination risk at higher connection and session cost. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The exposure analysis across the prohibited persistence and presentation surfaces Section 16 states; expected rotation and issuance traffic; and the conformance results of the secret-boundary and sanitized-derivative checks, exercised with synthetic, non-live fixtures only. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version only, provided every Section 16 property continues to hold. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan, with the registered secret-handling rules carrying the registered portions; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-09 — Record placement, encoding, schema, and store realization.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | Where the contract's registers, status dimensions, ledgers, and accounting records are placed, and under what encoding, schema, serialization, and store — the recurring per-section store, schema, and placement question of Sections 8 through 16. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-12 (Section 8.15, Status representation); SRC-25 (Section 10.12, Profile record placement and encoding); SRC-33 (Section 11.12, Timing-record placement); SRC-36 (Section 12.10, Request-record placement and encoding); SRC-49 (Section 13.13, Post-closure record placement); SRC-52 (Section 13.13, Evidence-contract representation); SRC-54 (Section 14.10, Disposition-record placement); SRC-61 (Section 15.11, Ledger realization); SRC-62 (Section 15.11, Membership-snapshot and partial-record representation); SRC-63 (Section 15.11, Completion-result representation and placement). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-LIFE-003, WP2-LIFE-004, WP2-LIFE-032, WP2-LIFE-034, WP2-SCHED-035, WP2-SCHED-039, WP2-SCHED-052, WP2-TIME-020, WP2-MODEL-019, WP2-MODEL-023, WP2-MODEL-053, WP2-EVID-016, WP2-EVID-054, WP2-FAIL-001, WP2-FAIL-004, WP2-AGG-004, WP2-AGG-008, WP2-AGG-009, WP2-AGG-027 — WP2-LIFE-034 and WP2-SCHED-052 entering through the boundary citation of SRC-63, whose realization family HD-10 registers. |
| Required property or invariant | For each register and record family, any selection has to satisfy the attribution, join, derivation, readability, and current-value properties the affected requirements state, corrections arriving as further identified records; the semantic content identity and the provider-facing rendering or payload identity or digest WP2-MODEL-053 keeps distinct remain distinct under every encoding, no payload format, hashing scheme, prompt representation, or wire encoding being selected for either; Section 13 decides how evidence is written wherever a source block assigns that question there; and the completion-recording authority boundary WP2-AGG-004 requires belongs to the family HD-10 registers. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Fields on one record; separate records; an append-only transition or event log from which current values are derived; an event stream; an index; records carried on the entities they describe; separate accounting or disposition records; a ledger as a single record, a set of linked records, or an append-only log; carriage in the study registration and evidence records or in separate documents; a membership snapshot as a ledger version reference, a copied roster, or a recorded query over identified records; more than one of those; any encoding or serialization. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Single-record fields read simply but concentrate concurrent writes on one record; separate records, event streams, and append-only logs suit concurrent appends and preserve history natively but make every current value a derivation discipline; indexes accelerate joins at the price of a second copy the correction rules have to keep honest; registration-carried placement binds records to registered custody early, while separate documents keep registration artifacts small at the cost of a join. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Read and write patterns of each register under the expected concurrency profile; the derivation and audit cost of each candidate arrangement; the store guarantees the boundary realization under HD-10 relies on; and the conformance results of the checks that exercise each register's attribution, join, and correction properties. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. The placement, schema, and store of a record never alter what a provider or a study subject receives; what a provider receives is governed by the Section 12 identity chain, and a change to dispatched content lies outside this decision. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version only. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan, with each study's registered evidence contract fixing, per study, what is recorded wherever an affected requirement assigns that question to it; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected registers. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-10 — Atomic or equivalently safe authority-boundary realization.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The realization of the contract's atomic or equivalently safe authority boundaries — scalar-transition acceptance, compound all-or-none coherence, and authoritative commit — together with the placement of transition provenance records, which one source block records beside the boundary question in a single coupled option. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-13 (Section 8.15, Transition-record placement and acceptance-boundary realization); SRC-23 (Section 9.12, Authoritative-commit primitive). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-LIFE-013, WP2-LIFE-015, WP2-LIFE-033, WP2-LIFE-034, WP2-SCHED-030. Four further source blocks incorporate this same realization family by citation — SRC-19 under HD-05 and SRC-39 under HD-23, each citing the arrangements Section 9.12 records for the authoritative-commit boundary, and SRC-63 under HD-09 and SRC-68 under HD-35, each citing the arrangements Sections 8.15 and 9.12 record — each mapped to the entry covering its titled subject and cross-referencing this one. |
| Required property or invariant | At each boundary, competing operations resolve to at most one accepted outcome, with every losing or superseded operation preserved as the affected requirements state; the compound outcomes WP2-LIFE-033 names decide as one all-or-none coordination; and the transition history, with its predecessor linkage, remains reconstructible from the record under WP2-LIFE-015 whatever the placement. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | A transactional record store; a single arbiter role that serializes acceptance for one entity's status dimension; a compare-against-current-revision append discipline; a consensus mechanism; a database transaction; a conditional or compare-and-set write; a single-writer arrangement; an authority-issued token; another mechanism. For transition provenance: inline on the entity's record; a separate transition record; an event stream; more than one of those. |
| Principal tradeoffs | A store transaction gives local atomicity while coupling the boundary to one store technology; a single arbiter is simple to reason about but bounds throughput at the arbiter and adds its own availability question; compare-and-set and revision-checked appends are store-portable and optimistic but retry under contention; consensus tolerates distribution and failure best at the highest operational complexity. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The contention profile at each boundary class; the documented guarantees of candidate stores and protocols; and the conformance results of the conceptual race checks, whose material interleavings the conformance document enumerates rather than leaving to timing luck. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations in content terms — every race outcome is operationally recorded and preserved, and no resolution rewrites treatment content — while the boundary's realization is part of the logical commit policy and execution architecture the registered concurrency profile of Section 10 records, so a selection that alters that profile is compared under the rule WP2-SCHED-048 names and remains a recorded experimental factor rather than a behaviorally neutral detail. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version, provided the at-most-one-winner, loser-preservation, and all-or-none properties continue to hold, with any profile-affecting change compared under the rule WP2-SCHED-048 names and the profile-comparison discipline of Section 10 governing its consequence. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-11 — Orchestrator-restart recovery procedure and laboratory-worker identity continuity.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The procedure by which state is reconstructed after an orchestrator restart, and whether and how a laboratory worker's identity continues across its own restart. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-14 (Section 8.15, Recovery procedure after an orchestrator restart); SRC-15 (Section 8.15, Laboratory-worker identity continuity across a restart). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-LIFE-019, WP2-LIFE-022, WP2-SCOPE-015. |
| Required property or invariant | Any selection has to achieve the reconstruction WP2-LIFE-019 requires and to discharge the continuity decision WP2-LIFE-022 assigns to the admitting authority; WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting the procedure, and neither continuity nor discontinuity is preferred. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Re-reading durable records at startup; a separate recovery pass; an authority-issued reconciliation; re-admission under a recorded identity; an agent-held credential presented at re-admission; an external service record; another procedure or mechanism. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Startup re-reading needs no extra machinery but scales recovery time with record volume; a separate recovery pass bounds startup cost but leaves a window in which reconstruction is incomplete; authority-issued reconciliation centralizes the truth question but adds an authority round trip; identity continuity preserves attribution chains across restarts while discontinuity gives simpler admission at the cost of longer lineage joins. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Expected record volumes and restart frequency; the attribution needs of the evidence contract across restarts; and the conformance results of the restart and recovery checks the conformance document specifies conceptually. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version only. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan, the continuity decision itself resting with the admitting authority as WP2-LIFE-022 states; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-12 — Liveness and interruption-detection mechanism.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The mechanism that establishes laboratory-worker liveness and the mechanism that tells the laboratory an execution attempt was interrupted. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-16 (Section 8.15, Interruption detection); SRC-20 (Section 9.12, Liveness mechanism). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-LIFE-020, WP2-LIFE-021, WP2-SCHED-019, WP2-SCHED-020. |
| Required property or invariant | WP2-LIFE-020 and WP2-LIFE-021 state what follows from an interruption determination rather than how it is reached, and the liveness policy WP2-SCHED-019 and WP2-SCHED-020 refer to may rest on any arrangement at any granularity; no interval, threshold, or detector is selected. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Heartbeats; lease renewal; external health checks; an authority-issued probe; a durable marker; an authority-issued reconciliation; a liveness policy at any granularity; another arrangement. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Heartbeat-style detection is fast but misclassifies slow-but-live laboratory workers under load; renewal-based detection couples liveness to the grant mechanism HD-05 registers; external checks and probes give an independent signal at added infrastructure; durable markers are cheap but detect only what the interrupted party managed to record. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The failure and load profiles of the execution environment; the misclassification cost as it lands in typed dispositions under Section 14; and the conformance results of the crash, stale-lease, and interruption checks specified conceptually. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. A detection error changes which execution attempts receive interruption determinations — a consequence carried by the typed dispositions of Section 14 and the accounting of Section 15, never a rewrite of any attempt's treatment content. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version; where the registered design carries the liveness policy as registered content, a new version of that registered policy as well. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan, with any registered liveness policy carried by the registered design; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements, and registration for any registered portion. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-13 — Finer recorded states within the lifecycle models.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | Whether a laboratory records finer states inside the values of the two lifecycle models. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-17 (Section 8.15, Additional recorded states). Affected requirement ID named by that block: WP2-LIFE-013. |
| Required property or invariant | Finer states are neither required, prohibited, nor preferred, provided every transition of the model itself remains recorded with the provenance WP2-LIFE-013 requires. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | No finer decomposition; finer recorded states inside any value of either model, at any granularity the implementation finds useful. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Finer states aid diagnosis and operational insight but add record volume and a mapping discipline back to the model's own values; the coarse models alone are simpler but expose less operational detail between model-level transitions. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Operational diagnostic needs observed in practice, and the record-volume consequence under the applicable retention policies. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version only. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-14 — Queue, delivery, and provider dispatch-path realization.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The mechanism realizing queue presence and delivery events for scheduling, and the path by which a local dispatch attempt reaches a provider. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-18 (Section 9.12, Queue and delivery mechanism); SRC-42 (Section 12.10, Dispatch path). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-SCHED-001, WP2-SCHED-002, WP2-SCHED-003, WP2-SCHED-004, WP2-SCHED-005, WP2-SCHED-006 — SRC-42 naming no further requirement and recording that the events of Section 12.4 are recorded where each in fact occurs on whichever path exists. |
| Required property or invariant | Any selection has to satisfy the queue-presence and delivery-event properties WP2-SCHED-001 through WP2-SCHED-006 state, with the Section 12.4 events recorded where they in fact occur on whichever path exists; the path never alters the identity chain of what is dispatched, that chain being governed by Section 12. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | A message broker; a polled table; a work-stealing pool; a filesystem or object-store convention; a direct call; a queued or brokered arrangement; a pooled connection; another mechanism or path. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Brokered arrangements give delivery semantics and buffering at the cost of a new infrastructure dependency whose own failure modes enter the fault surface; polled tables and store conventions reuse existing stores but trade latency for polling cost; direct calls are simplest but concentrate backpressure and retry handling in the caller; pooled connections amortize setup cost but pool state becomes a contamination surface the isolation checks have to cover. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Expected dispatch volume and fan-out; the delivery and ordering guarantees candidate mechanisms document against the recorded scheduling events; and the conformance results of the dispatch, duplicate-delivery, and late-response checks specified conceptually. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Potentially both. The queue, delivery, and dispatch-path realization is part of the execution architecture and local queue delay the registered concurrency profile and the observed concurrency provenance of Section 10 record, and behavioral neutrality of a realized concurrency profile is what a separately authorized Work Package 3 parity study would have to establish; until such parity is earned within a preregistered claim boundary, a profile-affecting selection is treated as registered, versioned design content rather than as an interchangeable operational detail, the path itself never altering the identity chain of what is dispatched. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version, with profile changes compared under the rule WP2-SCHED-048 names and the charter's versioning and registration rules governing any change treated as behavior-relevant, charter Section 14.2 keeping an execution-architecture change out of one registered study unless that study's preregistered design treats the change as a condition. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-15 — Scheduling-policy content: dispatch and release, attempt simultaneity, and race-resolution direction.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The content of the scheduling policy this specification deliberately leaves to the laboratory and the registered design: whether attempts are released individually or in dispatch waves and at what sizing, pacing, and ordering; whether one nonterminal execution attempt of a planned run exists at a time or more than one; and which of a cancellation and an authoritative acceptance prevails where the two race. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-21 (Section 9.12, Dispatch and release policy); SRC-22 (Section 9.12, Simultaneity of attempts of one planned run); SRC-24 (Section 9.12, Race-resolution direction). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-SCHED-009, WP2-SCHED-011, WP2-SCHED-012, WP2-SCHED-015, WP2-SCHED-031, WP2-SCOPE-015. |
| Required property or invariant | WP2-SCHED-011 and WP2-SCHED-012 bind whatever waves the laboratory in fact forms; simultaneity is the registered design's decision under WP2-SCHED-009 and WP2-SCHED-015; the race direction is fixed by the registered authority policy WP2-SCHED-031 requires; and WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting a wave size, laboratory-worker count, or any other numeric operating parameter. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Individual release or dispatch waves, under any sizing, pacing, and ordering; one nonterminal execution attempt of a planned run at a time, or more than one, under the registered design; cancellation prevailing, or authoritative acceptance prevailing, as the registered authority policy fixes. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Wave release amortizes scheduling cost and makes provenance compact but couples sibling attempts operationally; single-attempt simultaneity simplifies identity and budget reasoning while multiple simultaneous attempts trade cost and complexity for latency; a cancellation-prevails direction bounds spend and exposure while an acceptance-prevails direction preserves completed work, and either direction is coherent under the required loser preservation. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Throughput, cost, and exposure analyses for the registered designs contemplated; the recorded concurrency provenance of any prior runs under Section 10; and the conformance results of the cancellation-race and grant-race checks specified conceptually. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Potentially both. The realized concurrency profile is a recorded experimental factor under Section 10, and behavioral neutrality of that profile is exactly what a separately authorized Work Package 3 parity study would have to establish; until such parity is earned within a preregistered claim boundary, a profile-affecting selection is treated as registered, versioned design content rather than as an interchangeable operational detail. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new version of the registered scheduling or authority policy wherever the selection is registered content, with profile changes compared under the rule WP2-SCHED-048 names; a new implementation version for the operational portion; and the charter's versioning and registration rules governing any change treated as behavior-relevant. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The registered study design for the registered portions, at registration and before any evidentiary run relying on them; the separately authorized future implementation plan for the operational portions, before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-16 — Concurrency-profile observation granularity, vocabulary, and comparison rule.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The granularity at which observed concurrency provenance is recorded, the vocabulary in which the non-vendor architectural facts are described, and the versioned rule by which observed provenance is compared against the registered profile. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-26 (Section 10.12, Granularity of observed provenance); SRC-28 (Section 10.12, Comparison rule); SRC-29 (Section 10.12, Architectural vocabulary). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-SCHED-039, WP2-SCHED-040, WP2-SCHED-046, WP2-SCHED-048. |
| Required property or invariant | The per-attempt record WP2-SCHED-039 requires exists at every granularity choice, each observation carrying the attribution WP2-SCHED-040 requires, a finer-grained observation being a record the per-attempt record references; the vocabulary satisfies the sufficiency property WP2-SCHED-046 states; and the comparison rule WP2-SCHED-048 requires is versioned, with no weighting, tolerance, or equivalence class selected here. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Observation per execution attempt, per dispatch wave, per interval, or finer; a comparison rule weighing categories equally, treating some categories as decisive, or specific to a registered design; a registered taxonomy, structured attributes, or another vocabulary. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Finer observation grounds deviation analysis better but multiplies record volume; coarse observation is cheap but can hide within-attempt variation the comparison rule would otherwise see; an equal-weight comparison rule is simple but insensitive to decisive categories, while design-specific rules fit their study at the cost of cross-study comparability; a fixed taxonomy eases comparison across designs while structured attributes describe unforeseen architectures more faithfully. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The deviation-materiality needs of the registered designs; record-volume consequences under the applicable retention policies; and the conformance results of the profile-recording and comparison checks. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations — the decisions describe and compare realized concurrency rather than change it; the comparison rule's contents decide which deviations are dispositioned as material, a research-accounting consequence carried by the registered, versioned rule itself. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new version of the registered comparison rule or vocabulary where those are registered content; a new implementation version for the observation machinery. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The registered study design for the rule and vocabulary, at registration; the separately authorized future implementation plan for the observation machinery, before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-17 — Nonsecret identity and credential-reference scheme.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The scheme realizing the nonsecret identity of Section 10 and the nonsecret credential reference of Section 16. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-27 (Section 10.12, Nonsecret-identity scheme); SRC-71 (Section 16.9, Nonsecret-reference scheme). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-SCHED-047, WP2-SEC-008. |
| Required property or invariant | Any selection has to yield the alias, digest, or registered identity WP2-SCHED-047 requires and to satisfy the property WP2-SEC-008 states for the nonsecret credential reference; the secret boundary itself belongs to Section 16 and is registered under HD-08. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | A registered mapping; a keyed digest; an authority-issued alias; another scheme. |
| Principal tradeoffs | A registered mapping is fully auditable but adds a registered artifact to maintain; a keyed digest derives references without a lookup but ties them to key custody and scheme stability; an authority-issued alias is simple and opaque but makes the issuing authority a dependency for every new reference. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The join patterns in which the references participate across evidence and accounting records; the custody consequences of any keyed scheme under Section 16; and the conformance results of the nonsecret-reference checks. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version only. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan, with any registered mapping carried under the applicable registered rules; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-18 — Time-domain realization: duration basis, instant representation, and synchronization.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The basis on which durations are measured, the representation of recorded wall-clock instants, and the arrangement establishing recorded clock-synchronization uncertainty. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-30 (Section 11.12, Duration basis); SRC-31 (Section 11.12, Timestamp and instant representation); SRC-32 (Section 11.12, Clock synchronization). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-TIME-013, WP2-TIME-014, WP2-TIME-015, WP2-SCOPE-015. |
| Required property or invariant | Any selection has to supply the monotonic or equivalently safe basis WP2-TIME-013 requires, the separately recorded wall-clock instants WP2-TIME-014 requires, and the recorded synchronization uncertainty WP2-TIME-015 requires; no library, counter, resolution, unit, format, epoch, precision, time zone, protocol, service, or tolerance is selected, WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibiting every numeric operating parameter. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | A platform monotonic counter, a steady clock, or a sequence of authority-issued marks for durations; any calendar, precision, or encoding the evidence contract later adopts for instants; a network time protocol, an authority-issued reference, or a measured offset for synchronization uncertainty. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Platform counters are cheap and fine-grained but scoped to one process or host, complicating cross-party duration joins; authority-issued marks give one shared ordering surface at a round-trip cost; richer instant encodings carry more context at more bytes per record; protocol-based synchronization tracks drift continuously while measured offsets are simple but stale between measurements. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The cross-host duration and ordering joins the evidence contract requires; the drift characteristics of the execution environment; and the conformance results of the timing-record and timing-integrity checks. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version only. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan, with the instant representation adopted by the registered evidence contract where Section 13 assigns it there; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-19 — Timeout, expiry, freshness, and stop-condition values.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | Which timeouts, expiries, freshness tests, and stop conditions a laboratory applies, and at what values. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-34 (Section 11.12, Timeout values and stop conditions). Affected requirement IDs named by that block: WP2-TIME-005, WP2-SCOPE-015. |
| Required property or invariant | Each applied timeout, expiry, freshness test, and stop condition has to identify its time domain, as WP2-TIME-005 requires; WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits this specification from selecting a duration, threshold, or bound, and none is selected. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Any set of timeouts, expiries, freshness tests, and stop conditions, at any values, carried as operational policy or, where a registered design adopts one as design content, as registered, versioned content. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Tight values bound spend, exposure, and stale work quickly but misclassify slow-but-live activity more often, enlarging the interrupted and cancelled population; loose values waste budget and hold resources longer but disturb fewer attempts; per-domain values fit each time domain at the cost of a larger policy surface to record and audit. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Observed latency and completion distributions of the execution environment and providers; the budget-exposure analysis of Section 12's accounting; and the sample-composition consequences visible through Sections 14 and 15 accounting in prior recorded runs. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations in content terms — no value edits treatment content — while the values decide which attempts complete, fail, or are cancelled, a sample-composition consequence the typed dispositions of Section 14 and the accounting of Section 15 carry; a registered design that adopts a stop condition as design content carries it registered and versioned. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new version of the applicable registered policy wherever a value is registered content; otherwise an operational change recorded with the implementation version it ships in. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The laboratory's operational policy and, for registered values, the registered study design; latest decision point: registration for registered values, and before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements for the rest. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-20 — Provider-behavior detection algorithms, windows, and thresholds.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | What tells a laboratory that a rate-limit window, an outage, a correlated failure window, a substitution, or a late completion occurred — the detection algorithm, the observation window, and every threshold. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-35 (Section 12.9, Detection algorithm and threshold); SRC-41 (Section 12.10, Provider-behavior detection). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-MODEL-049, WP2-MODEL-050, WP2-SCOPE-015. |
| Required property or invariant | WP2-MODEL-049 binds only the phenomena that in fact occur and are observable on a conforming laboratory's request path; the registered policy WP2-MODEL-050 requires each observation to identify is where a registered design records its own detection choices; no numeric threshold or window value is stated, and no monitoring product, detector, or telemetry arrangement is selected. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Any detection algorithm, observation window, and threshold, recorded under the registered policy identity and version; any monitoring or telemetry arrangement, as a family with no product named. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Sensitive detection surfaces provider phenomena early but misclassifies transient noise as phenomena, inflating the recorded incident population; conservative detection under-records phenomena that in fact occurred; window and threshold choices trade the same axes and are only auditable if the registered policy records them. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Observed provider-behavior distributions from recorded request paths; the incident-population consequences visible through Section 14 accounting; and the conformance results of the fake-provider checks exercising rate-limit, outage, and correlated-failure phenomena conceptually. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. Detections feed the registered retry and fallback policies HD-22 registers, and the behavioral consequence of those policies is registered there, not here. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new version of the registered detection policy where registered; a new implementation version for the detection machinery. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The registered study design for registered detection choices, at registration; the separately authorized future implementation plan for the machinery, before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-21 — Idempotency-resolution realization.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The arrangement realizing idempotency resolution for model requests: the resolution store, the key scheme, the replay-answer mechanism, and the treatment of matching duplicate presentations. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-37 (Section 12.10, Idempotency realization). Affected requirement IDs named by that block: WP2-ISO-015, WP2-MODEL-024, WP2-MODEL-025, WP2-MODEL-026, WP2-MODEL-055. |
| Required property or invariant | Any selection has to satisfy WP2-ISO-015 and WP2-MODEL-024 through WP2-MODEL-026, with matching duplicate presentations resolved to one of the typed dispositions WP2-MODEL-055 states; no store, key scheme, cache, wait strategy, coalescing or attachment mechanism, deferral arrangement, or duplicate-refusal rule is selected, and no WP2-MODEL-055 disposition is preferred over another. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | A durable record store; a keyed index; an authority-issued reservation; any key scheme; any replay-answer mechanism; coalescing, attachment, deferral, or refusal among the typed dispositions WP2-MODEL-055 states. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Durable stores answer duplicates across restarts but add write cost to every request path; indexes are fast but their loss degrades duplicate detection until rebuilt; coalescing and attachment avoid duplicate provider spend but couple the waiting presentation to the live one's outcome; deferral and refusal keep presentations independent at the cost of retry traffic. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Expected duplicate-presentation rates under crash, retry, and race conditions; the store guarantees available; and the conformance results of the duplicate-identity, duplicate-presentation, and replay-answer checks with their material interleavings enumerated conceptually. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. The at-most-one resolution the affected requirements state keeps every disposition from multiplying authoritative results. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version, and a new version of the registered presentation-disposition policy WP2-MODEL-055 requires every further presentation to be resolved under, wherever the selection changes that policy's content. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan for the realization, with the registered presentation-disposition policy WP2-MODEL-055 requires carried by the registered design; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements, and registration for the registered policy. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-22 — Retry, fallback, replacement, and eligibility policy parameters.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The parameters of the registered retry, fallback, and replacement policies: how many provider retries, in what order, against which fallback identities, under what policy representation; how many further execution attempts and registered replacements; and every eligibility criterion and threshold. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-38 (Section 12.10, Retry and fallback realization); SRC-56 (Section 14.10, Retry, replacement, and eligibility parameters). Affected requirement ID named by those blocks: WP2-SCOPE-015 — the blocks otherwise citing the registered retry, fallback, and replacement policy identities and versions that Sections 12 and 14 require to be named, without enumerating further requirement IDs. |
| Required property or invariant | Every parameter is the registered design's decision, carried by the registered policy identities and versions the affected sections require to be named; this specification selects no retry count, fallback order, replacement count, eligibility threshold, capacity, or policy encoding, WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibiting each such selection. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Any retry count, ordering, and fallback identity set; any policy representation; any further-attempt and replacement count; any eligibility criterion or threshold — each carried as registered, versioned policy content. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Generous retry and replacement budgets recover more units at more spend and more exposure to correlated provider failure; fallback identities preserve throughput during provider trouble but change which provider or model produces the content; strict eligibility keeps the sample close to its registration while permissive eligibility preserves sample size at the cost of heterogeneity — with the never-retroactive materiality discipline of Section 14 keeping observed favorability from steering any of it. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Provider reliability and cost distributions from recorded request paths; the sample-size and missingness needs of the registered designs; and the conformance results of the retry, fallback, replacement, and eligibility checks, including the misclassification fault family the conformance document catalogues. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Both. A fallback identity changes which provider or model produces treatment content; replacement and eligibility rules change sample composition; the registered, versioned policies carry both consequences. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new treatment, condition, or experiment version under the charter's versioning and registration rules wherever a selection alters treatment content, and a new registered policy version in every case. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The registered study design; latest decision point: registration, before any evidentiary run relying on the policy. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-23 — Budget-authorization boundary and usage-accounting realization.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | How budget authorization is realized at its required boundary, how reservations, quotas, pricing, and currency are handled, in what store accounting lives, and from which sources known incurred usage is measured. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-39 (Section 12.10, Budget authorization realization); SRC-40 (Section 12.10, Usage measurement source). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-MODEL-036, WP2-MODEL-040, WP2-SCOPE-015. |
| Required property or invariant | The authorization boundary WP2-MODEL-036 requires is atomic or equivalently safe — its realization family is the one HD-10 registers, which the source block incorporates by citation — and known incurred usage from more than one source is kept distinguishable as WP2-MODEL-040 requires, none treated as authoritative by this specification; no cap, quota, or budget value is selected, WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibiting one. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | For the boundary: the arrangements HD-10 registers. For the surrounding accounting: any reservation algorithm, quota arbitration, pricing source, currency rule, and accounting store. For measurement: provider reports, local derivation, billing or reconciliation records, or more than one of those. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Eager reservation prevents oversubscription at the cost of stranded budget when attempts fail early; late or optimistic accounting frees budget but risks unresolved exposure windows; provider-reported usage reflects what will be billed but arrives late and on the provider's terms, while local derivation is immediate but approximate, and reconciliation-based measurement is authoritative in hindsight only. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Spend and exposure distributions from recorded request paths; the divergence observed among candidate usage sources; and the conformance results of the budget-exhaustion, authorization-race, and unresolved-exposure checks specified conceptually. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. An exhaustion refusal changes which attempts run — a consequence accounted as typed refusals under Section 14 and sample composition under Section 15, never an edit of treatment content. |
