| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version; a new version of the registered budget policy wherever the registered design carries one. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan, with registered budget policies carried by the registered design; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements, and registration for registered portions. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-24 — Evidence-artifact representation: archive and serialization technology, inventory encoding, seal representation, and boundary labeling.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The representation of the evidence contract's artifacts: the archive and serialization technology of inventories, seals, packages, closure records, and integrity-basis records; the encoding of an inventory; the form of a seal; and the form in which a partial-or-snapshot label is carried. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-43 (Section 13.13, Archive and serialization technology); SRC-45 (Section 13.13, Inventory encoding and seal representation); SRC-46 (Section 13.13, Partial or snapshot boundary labeling). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-EVID-040, WP2-EVID-041, WP2-EVID-042, WP2-EVID-043, WP2-EVID-044, WP2-EVID-045, WP2-EVID-046, WP2-EVID-047, WP2-EVID-048, WP2-EVID-049, WP2-EVID-050, WP2-EVID-055, WP2-EVID-071, WP2-EVID-073, WP2-SCOPE-015. |
| Required property or invariant | Any selection has to satisfy the properties WP2-EVID-040 through WP2-EVID-050 state, WP2-EVID-071 for the ordinary evidence-intake-closure record, WP2-EVID-073 for the attempt-integrity-basis record, and WP2-EVID-055 with the study-level-seal and sealed-package entries of Section 13.3 for the label; WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting an archive or serialization technology, and no encoding, file layout, or representation is selected. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Any archive or serialization technology; an inventory as one record, a manifest, an index, or another arrangement; a seal in any form identifying what WP2-EVID-044 requires; the label on the seal record, in a separate labeling record, or in another explicit form. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Widely readable serializations favor long-horizon auditability while compact or specialized forms favor size and speed; single-artifact inventories are self-contained while indexed forms scale to large roots but multiply the surfaces a verification has to cover; labels carried on the seal itself travel with what they qualify, while separate labeling records keep seals minimal at the cost of a join every reader has to perform. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Expected evidence-root sizes and item counts; the audit horizon the retention policies imply; and the conformance results of the inventory, sealing, verification, and labeling checks. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version, and a new version of the registered evidence contract wherever that contract carries the representation. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The registered evidence contract of each study together with the separately authorized future implementation plan; latest decision point: registration of the study whose evidence relies on it, and before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-25 — Digest, hash, and canonicalization scheme.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The digest or hash algorithm and the canonicalization scheme on which the evidence contract's content digests, digest facts, and replay comparisons rest. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-44 (Section 13.13, Digest and hash algorithm). Affected requirement IDs named by that block: WP2-EVID-022, WP2-EVID-040, WP2-EVID-044. |
| Required property or invariant | The content digests of WP2-EVID-040, the digest facts of WP2-EVID-044, and the hashes or comparisons of WP2-EVID-022 may rest on any algorithm and any canonicalization scheme the registered evidence contract adopts; no algorithm, canonicalization scheme, length, or encoding is selected. The request content-identity and rendering digests WP2-MODEL-053 keeps distinct are co-recorded in the block HD-09 receives, and the stochastic event-keyed derivation example belongs to HD-06. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Any digest or hash algorithm; any canonicalization scheme; any digest length and encoding — each adopted by the registered evidence contract. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Stronger algorithms cost more computation over large evidence roots; canonicalization schemes trade implementation simplicity against determinism across platforms and serializations; an algorithm whose collision resistance does not outlast the retention horizon of the evidence it authenticates weakens what its seals later establish, and migrating algorithms later means recorded digests of two generations coexist under the correction discipline. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Evidence-root sizes and sealing throughput needs; the retention horizon the applicable policies imply; published collision-resistance standing of candidate algorithm families; and the conformance results of the seal, verification, and replay-comparison checks. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version, and a new version of the registered evidence contract that adopts the scheme. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The registered evidence contract of each study together with the separately authorized future implementation plan; latest decision point: registration of the study whose evidence relies on it, and before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-26 — Seal-verification scheduling.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | When and how often seal verification runs. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-47 (Section 13.13, Verification scheduling). Affected requirement ID named by that block: WP2-EVID-051. |
| Required property or invariant | Any schedule is admissible provided the verification an applicable requirement names exists and its verdict is preserved — the acceptance-eligibility verification under WP2-EVID-051 among them; no schedule or trigger is selected. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Verification at packaging; before acceptance eligibility; on resume; periodically; on another schedule or trigger. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Early and frequent verification detects integrity loss close to its cause at recurring cost over all evidence; verification only at required points is cheap but leaves loss undetected until the next required point; periodic sweeps bound detection latency but their period is exactly the kind of operating parameter the registered policy has to record. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Evidence volumes and verification cost per root; the integrity-incident history recorded once runs exist; and the conformance results of the verification and integrity-basis checks. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version; a new version of the applicable registered policy wherever the schedule is registered content. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan, with any registered schedule carried by the applicable registered policy; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-27 — Replay engine and UI path.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The engine, harness, or surface realizing the identified replay implementation, and any UI replay surface. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-48 (Section 13.13, Replay engine and UI path). Affected requirement IDs named by that block: WP2-EVID-022, WP2-EVID-025. |
| Required property or invariant | The replay implementation WP2-EVID-022 requires to be identified may be realized by any engine, harness, or surface; no engine, browser, headless split, or invocation path is selected, and WP2-EVID-025 keeps the absence of a UI replay surface from being a nonconformity. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Any replay engine or harness; any UI replay surface or none; any split between headless and interactive replay. |
| Principal tradeoffs | A headless-only path is simplest to identify, version, and run under conformance checking, while a UI surface aids human inspection at the cost of a second identified path whose equivalence to the headless one has to be evidenced; a shared engine under both surfaces keeps one identity but couples their release cadence. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The inspection needs of study review; the replay-comparison results the evidence contract requires; and the conformance results of the replay checks, which exercise re-derivation without any provider call. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. Replay re-derives from recorded evidence under an identified implementation and never calls a provider, so no engine choice reaches treatment content. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version, the replay implementation identity and version changing with it. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-28 — Retention, evidence-size, and storage-lifecycle realization.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | Retention durations, storage technologies, compression, deletion mechanisms, capacity values, and the realization of evidence-size accounting. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-50 (Section 13.13, Retention realization). Affected requirement IDs named by that block: WP2-EVID-059, WP2-EVID-060, WP2-EVID-061, WP2-EVID-062, WP2-EVID-063, WP2-SCOPE-015. |
| Required property or invariant | Any selection has to satisfy the recorded-policy, refusal, and preservation properties WP2-EVID-059 through WP2-EVID-063 state; WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting a duration or capacity value, and none is selected. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Any retention duration set, storage technology, compression arrangement, deletion mechanism, capacity value, and evidence-size accounting realization, carried under recorded retention policy. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Long retention maximizes later auditability and re-verification at growing storage cost; aggressive compression and tiering cut cost but add a transformation between the recorded artifact and its stored form that verification has to account for; deletion mechanisms trade reclaimed capacity against the irreversibility of what the recorded policy permits to be discarded after its defined period. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Measured evidence-size growth per attempt and study; storage cost at the expected scale; the re-verification and audit demand observed over time; and the conformance results of the retention, refusal, and preservation checks. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version; a new version of the recorded retention policy wherever the policy carries the changed content. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan together with the recorded retention policies the affected requirements name; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-29 — Quarantine and redaction mechanism.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The mechanism realizing the explicit disposition of evidence containing detected live secret material. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-51 (Section 13.13, Quarantine and redaction mechanism). Affected requirement ID named by that block: WP2-EVID-068. |
| Required property or invariant | The explicit disposition WP2-EVID-068 requires may be realized by any mechanism under the registered policy applicable to it, or, where none applies, under the absence that disposition states; no quarantine, redaction, deletion, or secret-management mechanism is selected, as Section 16.9 records for the neighboring boundary. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Quarantine; access restriction; removal to controlled custody; another mechanism satisfying the disposition's recorded requirements. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Quarantine in place preserves the evidentiary artifact with its context while continuing to hold the sensitive bytes within the boundary; access restriction is fast and reversible but rests on enforcement at every access path; removal to controlled custody minimizes ongoing exposure at the cost of a custody transfer the lineage records have to carry without concealment. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The exposure analysis for each mechanism against Section 16's boundaries; the auditability consequences for sealed and unsealed roots; and the conformance results of the persisted-secret and sanitized-lineage checks, exercised with synthetic fixtures only. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version; a new version of the applicable registered policy wherever it carries the mechanism. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan together with the applicable registered secret-handling rules; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-30 — Failure-class taxonomy realization.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The representation under which the failure-class taxonomy is realized, and whether a registered design distinguishes finer classes within it. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-53 (Section 14.10, Taxonomy realization). Affected requirement ID named by that block: WP2-FAIL-005. |
| Required property or invariant | Every record remains classifiable under the classes Section 14.4 states and WP2-FAIL-005 is satisfied, whatever the representation and whatever finer classes a registered design distinguishes; no error-code set, enumeration, schema, or encoding is selected. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | A closed enumeration; a hierarchical code set; a tagged record structure; another representation; finer registered classes within any Section 14.4 class. |
| Principal tradeoffs | A closed enumeration is simple to validate but absorbs new phenomena only by revision; hierarchical code sets keep the mapping to the Section 14.4 classes structural but grow deep; tagged structures describe novel incidents flexibly at the cost of weaker validation; finer registered classes sharpen analysis while enlarging the vocabulary each consumer has to understand. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The incident-class distributions observed once runs exist; the analysis needs of the registered designs; and the conformance results of the classification checks. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version; a new version of the registered design's finer-class vocabulary wherever one is registered. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan, with finer classes carried by the registered design; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements, and registration for registered finer classes. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-31 — Materiality-rule contents.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | Which divergences a registered materiality policy declares material, and by what criteria. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-57 (Section 14.10, Materiality-rule contents). Affected requirement IDs named by that block: WP2-FAIL-010, WP2-FAIL-011. |
| Required property or invariant | The contents are the registered policy's own; any such policy has to satisfy the properties WP2-FAIL-010 and WP2-FAIL-011 state — identified, versioned, and never applied retroactively after observing favorability — and no criterion, threshold, or comparison window is selected. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Any registered set of material-divergence criteria, at any granularity of divergence class, carried as identified, versioned policy content. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Broad materiality criteria disposition more incidents as material, protecting validity at the cost of discarding or replacing more work; narrow criteria preserve sample size but admit more divergent executions into the analyzable population; either way the criteria have to be fixed in advance, since the never-retroactive discipline forecloses tuning them after favorability is visible. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The divergence classes observed on recorded request and execution paths; the validity sensitivity of the registered designs to each class; and the conformance results of the materiality and misclassification checks, including the retroactivity fault family the conformance document catalogues. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations in treatment terms — the policy classifies and dispositions rather than changes what any unit executes — while its contents decide replacement eligibility and aggregate handling, a research-disposition consequence carried by the registered, versioned policy itself. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new version of the registered materiality policy in every case, and the registered design's own version discipline wherever the policy is part of a preregistered analysis consequence. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The registered study design; latest decision point: registration, before any evidentiary run the policy governs. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-32 — Incident and secret detection and classification mechanism.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | What detects an incident and assigns its class, and what realizes the detection of live secret material specifically. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-58 (Section 14.10, Detection and classification mechanism); SRC-69 (Section 16.9, Detection mechanism). Affected requirement ID named by those blocks: WP2-SEC-013 — SRC-58 naming no individual requirement and expressly deferring to what Section 16.9 records for secret detection specifically. |
| Required property or invariant | The detection WP2-SEC-013 presupposes may be realized by any registered detector or combination, applied under a registered secret-handling rule identity and version, and incident detection and classification generally are unselected; no monitoring product, detector, classification algorithm, threshold, scanning tool, or review procedure is selected, and the treatment of a detection failure belongs to the failure taxonomy of Section 14. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Monitors; validators; verification passes; authority review; pattern matching; entropy analysis; provider-issued marker recognition; a registered detector of another kind; a combination of them. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Sensitive detection catches more genuine incidents and secrets while raising false detections, each of which triggers the refusal and incident machinery; conservative detection quiets false alarms but leaves more genuine material undetected until a later surface; layered combinations improve coverage at the cost of a larger registered rule surface to version and audit. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Detection-performance evidence on synthetic, non-live fixtures; the incident-population consequences visible through Section 14 accounting; and the conformance results of the detected-secret checks before and after persistence, exercised with synthetic fixtures only. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. A detection error changes which records receive incident, refusal, and sanitization dispositions — a consequence carried by the typed dispositions of Section 14 and the accounting of Section 15 — and edits no treatment content. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version, and a new version of the registered secret-handling rule under which detection is applied. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan together with the registered secret-handling rules; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-33 — Registered analysis and reporting content: statistical method, inclusion rules, and denominator reporting form.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The statistical method of any registered analysis, every inclusion or exclusion threshold, every missing-data method, and the form in which the registered sample denominator and the operational denominator are presented. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-59 (Section 15.11, Statistical method and inclusion rules); SRC-64 (Section 15.11, Denominator reporting form). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-AGG-011, WP2-AGG-020, WP2-AGG-022, WP2-SCOPE-015. |
| Required property or invariant | The method, thresholds, and missing-data rules are the registered design's decisions, carried by the analysis identity and version and the registered rules WP2-AGG-020 and WP2-AGG-022 require to be named; the two denominators are never merged, as WP2-AGG-011 requires, whatever the presentation; WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting a threshold or any other numeric parameter, and no reporting format is selected. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Any statistical method; any inclusion or exclusion threshold; any missing-data method; denominators presented jointly, separately, or with further registered breakdowns. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Method and inclusion choices trade statistical power against robustness to the missingness and heterogeneity the parallel laboratory produces; missing-data methods trade bias against variance under exactly the incident and replacement populations Sections 14 and 15 account for; joint denominator presentation is compact while separate presentation makes the registered-versus-operational gap conspicuous — the gap itself remaining reportable under either form. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The preregistered hypotheses and their power analyses; the missingness structure observed in recorded completion ledgers; and methodological grounds recorded in the registered design rather than anything this specification supplies. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations — the decision governs registered analysis and reporting over already-recorded evidence and reaches no execution or treatment content; its research consequence travels entirely with the registered analysis identity and version. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new registered analysis version in every case, and a new experiment version wherever the registered design carries the analysis as part of its preregistered claim structure. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The registered study design; latest decision point: registration, before any evidentiary aggregation the analysis governs. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-34 — Aggregation algorithm and rebuild realization.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | The realization of the aggregation algorithm, the rebuild toolchain, and the representation of the identified execution environment. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-60 (Section 15.11, Aggregation algorithm and rebuild realization). Affected requirement ID named by that block: WP2-AGG-033. |
| Required property or invariant | The aggregation algorithm, the rebuild toolchain, and the representation of the identified execution environment WP2-AGG-033 names may be realized by any arrangement that satisfies Section 15, deterministic rebuild from identified sources in the identified environment included; no algorithm, toolchain, runtime, or environment-description scheme is selected. The registered identity of the analysis the algorithm realizes is HD-33's content; this entry registers its realization. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Any aggregation-algorithm realization; any rebuild toolchain; any environment-description scheme sufficient to identify the execution environment for deterministic rebuild. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Tightly pinned toolchains and environments reproduce exactly but age quickly and raise the cost of every legitimate upgrade; looser environment descriptions survive upgrades but widen the gap a rebuild has to bridge deterministically; richer environment representations make rebuilds more portable at more description to record and verify. |
| Evidence needed to choose | Rebuild determinism results across candidate toolchains and environments; the longevity needs the retention policies imply; and the conformance results of the deterministic-rebuild and lineage checks. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version; the registered analysis version is unaffected while the realization still rebuilds it deterministically, and a realization change that alters registered analysis results is not a realization change but an analysis change governed by HD-33. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-35 — Credential rotation and revocation schedule.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | Which credential rotations occur, on what schedule, and under which triggering conditions. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-68 (Section 16.9, Rotation and revocation schedule). Affected requirement IDs named by that block: WP2-SEC-010, WP2-SEC-011, WP2-SEC-017, WP2-LIFE-034, WP2-SCHED-052, WP2-SCOPE-015 — WP2-LIFE-034 and WP2-SCHED-052 entering through the block's citation of the boundary family HD-10 registers for the credential-lifecycle boundary WP2-SEC-017 requires. |
| Required property or invariant | Rotations, revocations, and replacements record and preserve what WP2-SEC-010 and WP2-SEC-011 state, under the applicable registered secret-handling rules; competing credential-lifecycle operations decide at the atomic or equivalently safe boundary WP2-SEC-017 requires, of the same family as the boundaries WP2-LIFE-034 and WP2-SCHED-052 require and realizable by the arrangements HD-10 registers; WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting a rotation interval or any other numeric operating parameter, and no primitive, store, arrangement, operation-record schema, or grouping mechanism is selected. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | Any rotation and revocation schedule and any triggering-condition set, carried by the registered secret-handling rules; for the competing-operation boundary, the arrangements HD-10 registers. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Frequent rotation shortens the life of any exposed credential but multiplies lifecycle operations and the race windows among them; trigger-driven rotation concentrates effort where risk appeared but depends on the detection quality HD-32 registers; infrequent rotation is operationally quiet at longer exposure lifetimes — history preservation being invariant under all of them, since rotation never rewrites credential history. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The exposure and incident history recorded under Section 16; provider constraints on credential lifecycles as they appear in recorded provider behavior; and the conformance results of the rotation-race and revocation checks specified conceptually. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new version of the applicable registered secret-handling rules; a new implementation version for the machinery realizing them. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The registered secret-handling rules together with the separately authorized future implementation plan; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-36 — Conformance-execution realization.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | How the conformance contract's checks are executed: the runner, harness, and venue realization of Section 17's check classes, the fake-provider realization, and the interleaving-control technique for concurrency and race testing. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | SRC-72 (Section 17.7, Conformance runner, harness, and venue realization); SRC-73 (Section 17.7, Fake-provider realization); SRC-74 (Section 17.7, Interleaving control). Affected requirement IDs named by those blocks: WP2-HAND-001, WP2-HAND-002, WP2-HAND-003, WP2-HAND-004, WP2-HAND-005, WP2-HAND-006, WP2-HAND-007, WP2-HAND-008, WP2-HAND-009, WP2-HAND-010, WP2-HAND-011, WP2-HAND-012, WP2-HAND-013, WP2-HAND-014, WP2-HAND-015, WP2-HAND-016, WP2-HAND-017, and WP2-SCOPE-015 — WP2-HAND-011 named individually by SRC-74 and WP2-HAND-012 by SRC-73, and WP2-SCOPE-015 named by SRC-72 for the workflow-framework and topology prohibition. |
| Required property or invariant | Every realization satisfies the conformance model of Section 17 unchanged: exact-version binding, the pass, fail, blocked-or-not-run, and justified-inapplicability outcome vocabulary, the no-false-pass discipline, the keyless boundary with no live provider dispatch and no live secret material, and forced or enumerated material interleavings rather than timing luck. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | A local runner over a fixed fixture tree; a hosted continuous-integration pipeline; scripted audit procedures for static document checks; deterministic-scheduler or interception-point interleaving control; conceptual enumeration over the operation model; a substitute provider realized as a process, a library, or another arrangement. |
| Principal tradeoffs | Reproducibility and evidence quality against setup cost; deterministic interleaving control against realism of contention; hosted-venue convenience against the environment-identification burden the conformance-evidence contract imposes. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The selected implementation's own realization decisions (the other canonical decisions of this register, as selected), and trial conformance-run evidence that a candidate realization satisfies the evidence-identification and race-testing disciplines Section 17 states. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Only operations: conformance execution observes the laboratory contract and supplies no laboratory behavior, and a realization able to alter the behavior under test would fail the no-false-pass discipline it is required to satisfy. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version of the conformance-execution realization itself, which changes the conformance-evidence identification under the exact-version and evidence-identification requirements of Section 17; no new treatment, condition, or experiment version of the laboratory. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized implementation effort under Operator authorization and Advisor review, at latest before the first conformance run whose evidence is offered toward the implementation-acceptance gate of Section 20. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |

**HD-37 — Execution environment and deployment topology of the laboratory.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Decision subject | How the laboratory is hosted and arranged in execution — the host or runner class, process and service arrangement, browser-or-headless split, gateway and inference-path topology, and containerization or cloud arrangement under which laboratory workers and the orchestrator execute. |
| Source section(s) and affected requirement IDs | No unselected-option source block states this decision; it is recorded on the directly necessary handoff ground that WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits this specification from selecting a cloud provider, container platform, or deployment topology while Section 10.5 requires the host or runner class, execution-environment identity, and process, browser-or-headless, gateway, and inference-path topology to be registered and observed. Affected requirement IDs: WP2-SCHED-035, WP2-SCHED-039, WP2-SCHED-046, WP2-SCOPE-015, together with the isolation invariants WP2-ISO-002 and WP2-ISO-007 that any arrangement has to leave satisfiable. |
| Required property or invariant | Any arrangement satisfies the isolation invariants of Section 7, is describable as the non-vendor architectural facts WP2-SCHED-046 requires, and is recorded and compared as the registered and observed content Section 10.5 assigns to those two categories. |
| Plausible implementation-neutral option families | A single host running several processes; several hosts; a containerized arrangement; a hosted or managed environment; a mixed arrangement. |
| Principal tradeoffs | A single host keeps operation and isolation surfaces local but concentrates capacity and failure on one machine; several hosts add capacity and failure independence at the cost of coordination across them; containerized arrangements make environment identity declarable and reproducible but add a platform layer whose own identity enters the recorded execution environment; hosted or managed environments shift operational burden outward while widening the environment-identification and custody surfaces Sections 10 and 16 make recordable; mixed arrangements carry these consequences per component. |
| Evidence needed to choose | The capacity, cost, and availability needs of the registered designs contemplated; an isolation analysis of each candidate arrangement against Section 7; the environment-identification and secret-custody consequences under Sections 10 and 16; and the conformance results of the checks that exercise the affected requirements under the realized arrangement. |
| Whether the decision can affect behavior, only operations, or both | Potentially both. The realized topology is part of the execution architecture the registered concurrency profile and the observed concurrency provenance of Section 10 record — a recorded experimental factor — and behavioral neutrality of a realized profile is exactly what a separately authorized Work Package 3 parity study would have to establish; until such parity is earned within a preregistered claim boundary, a profile-affecting selection is treated as registered, versioned design content rather than as an interchangeable operational detail. |
| Whether a change requires a new implementation, treatment, condition, or experiment version | A new implementation version, with profile changes compared under the rule WP2-SCHED-048 names and the charter's versioning and registration rules governing any change treated as behavior-relevant, charter Section 14.2 keeping an execution-architecture change out of one registered study unless that study's preregistered design treats the change as a condition. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The separately authorized future implementation plan, the registered concurrency profile of each study carrying the registered description of the realized arrangement; latest decision point: before the conformance assessment that exercises the affected requirements. |
| Current status | Unselected. Nothing in this entry ranks or prefers an option family. |
