### 18.4 The implementation handoff boundary

The handoff boundary fixes what completion of this specification changes and what it does not: the contract becomes available, exactly as written, to a future and separately authorized implementation effort, and nothing about approval, merge, the existence of the register in Section 18.3, or the conformance mapping the conformance document states selects an option, starts implementation, or relaxes a requirement.

**WP2-HAND-020.** Implementation of the laboratory, procurement or deployment of any component of it, and any activity WP2-SCOPE-014 enumerates **MUST NOT** be begun, resourced, or treated as authorized on the ground of approval, completion, or merge of this specification package, of any chunk approval within it, of the existence or completeness of the register in Section 18.3, or of the conformance mapping the conformance document states, implementation beginning only under the separate Operator authorization and applicable Advisor review that Section 1.3 records and charter Sections 13 and 14.10 require.

**WP2-HAND-021.** A future implementation plan for the laboratory **MUST** identify every canonical decision of the register in Section 18.3 that it selects together with the version under which each selection is recorded, its conformance strategy under Section 17 and the conformance document, its migration boundary from the current serial system whose baseline Section 5 records, and every behavior-affecting choice it contains — every choice that can affect a treatment, a condition, or observable simulated behavior — none of them left implicit in the plan.

**WP2-HAND-022.** A future implementation, its implementation plan, and any activity offered toward implementation acceptance **MUST NOT** silently reinterpret a requirement of this specification, silently narrow or extend a term Section 3.1 defines, or silently resolve an open question recorded under Section 21 or in the conformance document — an interpretation, narrowing, extension, or resolution existing only where an explicit, identified ruling or registered decision of the authority the applicable register states records it, and a divergence between implemented behavior and a requirement of this specification remaining a nonconformity rather than becoming an interpretation.

**WP2-HAND-023.** A mechanism selected under an entry of the register in Section 18.3 **MUST** satisfy the required property or invariant that entry states, and every requirement the entry's affected requirement IDs name, through the mechanism's own recorded behavior under the conformance model Section 17 states, independent of the technology chosen — no selection being citable as narrowing, discharging, or substituting for a requirement of this specification, and no property being treated as satisfied because a technology is reputed, documented, or marketed to provide it.

**WP2-HAND-024.** An implementation change that can affect a treatment, a condition, an experiment, or observable simulated behavior **MUST** follow the charter's versioning and registration rules — charter Section 14.2 governing whether the change requires a new registered treatment, condition, or experiment version, and charter Section 14.3 governing the registration that identifies what a study relies upon — before any registered study relies on the changed implementation.

**WP2-HAND-025.** Implementation acceptance **MUST** be established only under the exact-version conformance contract Section 17 states and the conformance document carries — one exact implementation build assessed against one exact main-specification identity and version and one exact conformance-document identity and version, with the outcomes, evidence, and coverage that contract requires — never by an informal judgment that the implementation appears to work, by operational history or demonstration, or by the absence of observed failure.

**Rationale.** The six requirements above keep the three gates of this work package distinct at the exact point where they are most easily conflated. WP2-SCOPE-014 already prohibits treating approval of this specification package as implementation authorization, and WP2-HAND-020 operationalizes that prohibition at the handoff boundary rather than restating it, naming the grounds — chunk approvals, the register, the conformance mapping — that a handoff could otherwise mistake for authorization; Section 20 states the acceptance criteria of each gate, and Section 19 states why no conformance or handoff fact reaches Work Package 3 parity, as WP2-SCOPE-011 separately prohibits. The plan contract of WP2-HAND-021 exists because the register in Section 18.3 makes silence detectable: a plan that selects a mechanism without naming the register entry it resolves is incomplete on its face. WP2-HAND-022 and WP2-HAND-023 close the two remaining paths by which a selection could quietly alter the contract — reinterpretation and technology substitution — and WP2-HAND-024 carries charter Sections 14.2 and 14.3 across the handoff so that a change that can touch a treatment is versioned and registered there exactly as it would be here. WP2-HAND-025 keeps acceptance a recorded, exact-version fact rather than an impression; the absence-never-passes discipline it relies on is stated where the conformance model states outcomes, in Section 17.

### 18.5 Boundaries assigned elsewhere

This section states which implementation decisions remain open, what any future selection of each of them has to satisfy, and under what boundary a future implementation effort takes up this contract. It deliberately defines none of the following: the conformance model under which the requirements of this section are checked, which Section 17 states; the conformance checks, fault injections, and pass conditions for every requirement stated here, which belong to Section 17 and to the conformance document; the dependency boundary toward, and the design of, any serial-versus-parallel parity study, which belong to Section 19 and to the separately authorized Work Package 3; the criteria under which the documentation package, a future implementation, and Work Package 3 parity are separately judged, which Section 20 states; and the register of open questions and later rulings, which Section 21 states — a canonical decision of Section 18.3 is a deliberately unselected choice with a stated decision authority, not an open question, and nothing in this section resolves, restates, or duplicates a Section 21 entry.

## 19. Work Package 3 dependency

### 19.1 What this section establishes

This section states the dependency boundary between the completed Work Package 2 specification and any Work Package 3 activity: that Work Package 3 remains separately registered and separately authorized, what Work Package 2 conformance contributes toward it and what conformance never establishes, how concurrency is treated until serial-versus-parallel parity is earned, what this specification package neither designs nor authorizes, how a change to the implementation build or to a registered concurrency profile unsettles earlier evidence, and what never substitutes for behavioral parity. It answers the part of the Section 15.12 assignment that belongs here — the dependency boundary toward any serial-versus-parallel parity study — while the design of that study belongs to the separately authorized Work Package 3 itself, exactly as Section 15.12 states, as Section 2.3 records in opening the specification, and as Section 10.13 repeats in assigning the design of any serial-versus-parallel parity comparison to the separately authorized Work Package 3. It is implementation-neutral, states a boundary rather than an experiment, and selects nothing; the selections WP2-SCOPE-015 enumerates remain prohibited throughout this document.

Every substantive statement here is a Required future invariant carrying exactly one `WP2-HAND-###` identifier, continuing the single sequence Section 17 begins, or a Design rationale introduced by a **Rationale.** lead-in, in the Section 4.3 sense, or else a boundary statement in Section 19.8. This section holds no structured block, no register, and no Unselected option lead-in — a deliberately unselected technical choice is an entry of the implementation-decision register in Section 18 — and it introduces no Section 3.1 term. Nothing in this section is a Current fact or a Current limitation; those belong to Section 5, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps a current mechanism from being read as a requirement. Every defined term used here carries the meaning Section 3.1 assigns it, as WP2-SCOPE-005 requires.

### 19.2 Separate registration and authorization

**WP2-HAND-026.** Work Package 3 **MUST NOT** be treated as registered, authorized, scheduled, or begun by virtue of this specification or its approval, of the completion or approval of any drafting chunk of it, of Work Package 2 documentation-package acceptance, of implementation acceptance, or of any conformance result or conformance evidence — Work Package 3 remaining separately registered and separately authorized under the charter's sequencing and approval rules.

**Rationale.** Charter Sections 13 and 14.10 make each later work package a separately authorized unit of work, and Section 1.3 records that authorization to draft this specification is authorization to write a document and nothing after it. WP2-SCOPE-014 already denies approval, completion, or merge of this package the force of authorization; WP2-HAND-026 extends the same denial along the whole chain a reader might otherwise walk — chunk approval, package acceptance, implementation acceptance, conformance evidence — so that no accumulation of Work Package 2 successes matures into Work Package 3 authority. Registration is named beside authorization because charter Section 14.3 makes registration a distinct recorded act, and a study that is authorized but unregistered is as far from beginning as one that is neither.

### 19.3 Conformance as a prerequisite, never as research evidence

**WP2-HAND-027.** A Work Package 2 conformance result, and the conformance evidence supporting it, **MUST NOT** be recorded, reported, or cited as serial-versus-parallel parity evidence, as study evidence for a claim about a candidate cognitive architecture or for any other research claim, or as anything beyond the infrastructure finding it is — its sealing, reproducibility, or deterministic rebuild conferring no research standing on it, and its standing as a prerequisite of parity-study eligibility never converting it into parity evidence.

**Rationale.** Section 2.3 opens the specification by denying infrastructure conformance every research reading, and WP2-SCOPE-011 and WP2-SCOPE-012 make that denial testable for conformance results; WP2-HAND-027 adds the dependency-boundary half. Conformance is what makes an implementation a candidate for the separately authorized parity study — the prerequisite the third gate of Section 20 presupposes — while the conformance evidence itself remains an infrastructure record whatever its form: a conformance-evidence package is sealed, verifiable, and rebuildable because Sections 13 and 17 require evidence discipline of it, and none of those properties is what distinguishes research evidence from operational evidence. The conformance document states the same separation from its own side, in its evidence contract and in its parity-boundary section, and charter Section 8.5 grounds both statements: throughput does not establish scientific validity, and parallel execution is not presumed behaviorally neutral.

### 19.4 Concurrency as a recorded experimental factor

**WP2-HAND-028.** Until serial-versus-parallel parity is earned through a preregistered parity study and within the registered claim boundary that study's own preregistration names, a conforming laboratory and every report over its evidence **MUST** treat concurrency as a recorded experimental factor — carried by the registered concurrency profile and the observed concurrency provenance Section 10 requires — and never as a behaviorally neutral operating detail, any parity later earned extending no further than the claim boundary within which it was earned.

**Rationale.** Charter Section 10.4 requires parallel execution to earn behavioral use through a preregistered parity study and keeps concurrency a recorded experimental factor until then, and charter Section 10.3 requires every future study to version and record a concurrency profile precisely because concurrency can change observed behavior. WP2-SCHED-035 and WP2-SCHED-039 already bind the two records this treatment rests on — the registered concurrency profile and the per-attempt observed concurrency provenance — and WP2-HAND-028 states the standing rule those records serve rather than restating their contents: what is recorded is a factor, not a neutrality finding, and the earning of parity is itself bounded, because charter Section 10.4 leaves concurrency a recorded experimental factor until the relevant parity boundary is established, and charter Section 14.3 requires a preregistered study's conclusions to remain bounded to what it registered.

### 19.5 No parity design, threshold, or run

**WP2-HAND-029.** This specification package **MUST NOT** be cited as designing a serial-versus-parallel parity experiment, as stating or implying a parity hypothesis, metric, threshold, tolerance, statistical test, or sample size, or as authorizing any parity run — this section stating a dependency boundary and nothing more.

**Rationale.** Charter Section 10.4 records that no parity threshold or test is predeclared, and WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits selecting a threshold or any other numeric operating parameter anywhere in this document. WP2-HAND-029 keeps this section from being read as the quiet beginning of the parity design: stating what parity evidence would depend on is not stating what would count as parity, and no sentence of this section supplies a hypothesis, a measure, a tolerance, or a run plan. The comparison discipline WP2-SCHED-048 requires for concurrency profiles is deliberately content-free on this point too — it fixes that comparison is category-by-category under a versioned rule, and the rule itself remains for the separately authorized study to preregister.

### 19.6 Change, renewed conformance, and the applicability of earlier parity evidence

**WP2-HAND-030.** Where the implementation build changes, or the registered concurrency profile under which evidence was produced changes, an earlier conformance verdict or earlier parity evidence **MUST NOT** be treated as covering the changed configuration — conformance being re-established for the exact changed build under the conformance model Section 17 states before implementation acceptance is claimed for that build, and the continued applicability of earlier parity evidence to the changed configuration being an explicit, recorded determination under the comparison discipline WP2-SCHED-048 and WP2-SCHED-049 state, never a presumption that survives the change unexamined.

**Rationale.** Conformance under the model Section 17 states binds to one exact main-specification identity and version, one exact conformance-document identity and version, and one exact implementation build, so a changed build has no passing basis to inherit — renewed conformance is the consequence of exact-version binding rather than a new obligation invented here. Parity evidence is bounded the same way from the scientific side: charter Section 10.3 versions the concurrency profile because execution architecture and contention conditions can change behavior, and WP2-SCHED-049 already prohibits treating two executions as architecturally alike on the basis of a profile name alone, so whether parity evidence produced under one profile speaks to execution under another is a question answered by recorded category-level comparison and explicit determination, not by optimism. Charter Section 14.2 supplies the versioning consequence: a change that alters what evidence means arrives under a new version, never silently under an old one.

### 19.7 No infrastructure substitute for behavioral parity

**WP2-HAND-031.** A record, verdict, result, or evidence package produced under this specification — an aggregate result, a deterministic rebuild, a seal or sealed package, a replay verdict, and a throughput result among them — **MUST NOT** be recorded, reported, or cited as establishing, or as substituting for, serial-versus-parallel behavioral parity, the parity question remaining answerable only by the separately authorized, preregistered Work Package 3 study.

**Rationale.** WP2-SCOPE-011 denies the parity reading to conformance results, WP2-SCHED-050 denies it to concurrency profiles, provenance records, their comparisons, and throughput results, and WP2-AGG-040 and WP2-AGG-041 deny it to sealed, rebuilt, or deterministically reproducible aggregations and to the completion machinery of Section 15. WP2-HAND-031 states the same denial once at the dependency boundary, over every record class this specification produces, so that no artifact assembled from parts individually prohibited from carrying the parity reading acquires it in combination or by omission — the closing device the Section 15.10 rationale describes, extended here to the specification package as a whole. What behavioral parity would take is exactly what none of these records contains: a preregistered comparison of behavior under serial and parallel execution, within a declared claim boundary, which is Work Package 3's subject and no record's side effect.

### 19.8 Boundaries assigned elsewhere

This section states the dependency boundary between this specification and any Work Package 3 activity. It deliberately defines none of the following: the conformance model, checks, and pass conditions for the requirements stated here, which belong to Section 17 and to the conformance document; the implementation handoff boundary and the register of deliberately unselected technical choices, which Section 18 states; the three acceptance gates and their non-inference rules, which Section 20 states; the recording of any genuinely open question about this boundary and of any later ruling, which Section 21 states; and the design, registration, preregistration, metrics, thresholds, analysis, execution, and claim boundary of the serial-versus-parallel parity study itself, which belong to the separately authorized Work Package 3 under the charter's sequencing and approval rules.

## 20. Acceptance criteria

### 20.1 What this section establishes

This section states the acceptance criteria of this specification package as three distinct gates — Work Package 2 documentation-package acceptance, implementation acceptance, and Work Package 3 parity eligibility and evidence — states what reaching each gate takes, and prohibits every inference from one gate to another. Section 1.4 assigns main-specification version `1.0.0`, but a version label alone reaches no gate: documentation-package acceptance requires the complete integration, exact-head continuous-integration, and final-clearance basis WP2-HAND-033 states. This section is implementation-neutral and selects nothing; the selections WP2-SCOPE-015 enumerates remain prohibited throughout this document.

Every substantive statement here is a Required future invariant carrying exactly one `WP2-HAND-###` identifier, continuing the single sequence Section 17 begins, or a Design rationale introduced by a **Rationale.** lead-in, in the Section 4.3 sense, or else a boundary statement in Section 20.6. This section holds no structured block, no register, and no Unselected option lead-in — a deliberately unselected technical choice is an entry of the implementation-decision register in Section 18 — and it introduces no Section 3.1 term. Nothing in this section is a Current fact or a Current limitation; those belong to Section 5, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps a current mechanism from being read as a requirement. Every defined term used here carries the meaning Section 3.1 assigns it, as WP2-SCOPE-005 requires.

### 20.2 The three gates and their distinctness

**WP2-HAND-032.** Work Package 2 documentation-package acceptance, implementation acceptance, and Work Package 3 parity eligibility and evidence **MUST** be recorded, reported, and evaluated as three distinct gates wherever any of them is recorded, reported, or cited — none of them claimed, satisfied, waived, or advanced through the satisfaction, partial satisfaction, or apparent nearness of another.

**Rationale.** The three gates answer three different questions — whether this documentation package is complete and integrated, whether a separately authorized implementation satisfies the contract, and whether parity has been earned — and the distinctions among specification acceptance, implementation authorization, implementation conformance, and Work Package 3 parity have governed this document from Sections 1.3, 2.3, and 3.5 onward. Keeping the gates distinct in every record and report is what makes the later gates meaningful: a gate that could be claimed through another would inherit exactly the inferences WP2-SCOPE-011 through WP2-SCOPE-014 prohibit. The conformance document states its conformance acceptance criteria separately under the same discipline and ties them to these gates by citation.

### 20.3 Documentation-package acceptance

**WP2-HAND-033.** The Work Package 2 documentation package **MUST NOT** be recorded, reported, or treated as accepted until every one of the following holds on one unchanged exact Git head: the normative contract of this specification is complete across Sections 1 through 21; the conformance document carries the complete, bidirectionally reconciled requirement, check, and fault mapping under the discipline Section 17 states; the implementation-decision register in Section 18 is complete, every deliberately unselected technical choice of this document mapped into it; no unresolved blocking contradiction stands — none recorded and escalated under Section 1.2 awaiting resolution, and no entry of the register in Section 21.3 whose recorded blocking effect names documentation-package acceptance standing open; the final integration audit is complete; distinct established main-specification and conformance-document identities and versions are assigned; the complete required continuous-integration workflow has succeeded on that exact head; and final Advisor clearance has been recorded for that same head.

**WP2-HAND-034.** Substantive completion of the contract or conformance document, approval of any individual drafting chunk, assignment of version labels, a completed integration audit, or continuous-integration success on any other head **MUST NOT** be recorded, reported, or cited as reaching or claiming documentation-package acceptance — acceptance requiring every condition of WP2-HAND-033 to hold together on the same unchanged exact Git head.

**Rationale.** The first gate requires one coherent content basis. Completeness, mapping integrity, register completeness, the absence of a blocking contradiction, distinct document versions, the integration audit, exact-head CI, and final Advisor clearance all attach to the same Git head, so no near-final or partially reviewed state can inherit acceptance. WP2-HAND-034 names the individual facts most easily mistaken for the gate and makes explicit that none substitutes for the whole basis.

### 20.4 Implementation acceptance

**WP2-HAND-035.** A future implementation of this specification **MUST NOT** be recorded, reported, or treated as having reached implementation acceptance until every one of the following holds: the implementation is separately and explicitly authorized, the authorization Section 1.3 and WP2-SCOPE-014 record as never implied by this package; every implementation-acceptance requirement of this specification has a current passing basis under the conformance model Section 17 states — or the explicitly justified inapplicability outcome WP2-HAND-008 permits, where that requirement's own language permits inapplicability — assessed against one exact main-specification identity and version, one exact conformance-document identity and version, and one exact implementation build; the conformance evidence is the complete, sealed, nonsecret, verifiable package the conformance document requires; and every blocking implementation decision of the register in Section 18, in the Section 18.2 sense, is resolved, recorded, and carried under its applicable version.

**WP2-HAND-036.** Implementation acceptance **MUST NOT** be inferred from documentation-package acceptance, from approval or merge of this specification package, from partial or favorable conformance results, from later registered-study or research evidence, or from an informal judgment that an implementation appears to work — the exact-version conformance contract of Section 17 and the conformance document remaining the only basis on which the gate of WP2-HAND-035 is reached.

**Rationale.** The second gate consumes what Sections 17 and 18 build. The conformance model fixes what a passing basis is, binds it to exact identities and versions, and keeps pass, fail, blocked, and inapplicable outcomes distinct, so "every implementation-acceptance requirement passing" is a checkable claim rather than a mood; the conformance document carries the checks, fixtures, faults, and evidence contract that claim rests on; and the implementation-decision register records the choices an implementation makes explicitly, because a blocking decision resolved silently would be exactly the silent reinterpretation the Section 18 handoff boundary prohibits. WP2-HAND-036 names the informal judgment beside the structural inferences because it is the failure mode most likely in practice: a system that demonstrably runs establishes nothing this gate can consume, and keylessness holds throughout — implementation acceptance rests on no live provider credential, repository game-model call, browser experiment, or Work Package 3 study, as Section 17 requires.

### 20.5 Work Package 3 parity eligibility and evidence

**WP2-HAND-037.** Work Package 3 parity evidence **MUST** come only from a separately authorized, preregistered serial-versus-parallel parity study conducted after the implementation it evaluates has reached implementation acceptance under WP2-HAND-035, within the registered claim boundary that study's own preregistration names.

**WP2-HAND-038.** Work Package 3 parity eligibility, parity evidence, or parity itself **MUST NOT** be inferred from documentation-package acceptance, from implementation acceptance, or from the two together — the eligibility implementation acceptance confers being eligibility to be considered for a separately authorized parity study, as Section 19 states, and never a parity result.

**Rationale.** Charter Section 10.4 requires parallel execution to earn behavioral use through a preregistered parity study, and Section 19 states the dependency discipline — separate registration and authorization under WP2-HAND-026, conformance as prerequisite and never as parity evidence under WP2-HAND-027, and no infrastructure substitute under WP2-HAND-031. WP2-HAND-037 and WP2-HAND-038 are that discipline in gate form: evidence follows a separately authorized, preregistered study, the study follows implementation acceptance, and neither eligibility nor evidence is inferred from the earlier gates, exactly as WP2-AGG-041 already prohibits treating any completion machinery of this specification as beginning, satisfying, or substituting for the parity study.

### 20.6 Boundaries assigned elsewhere

This section states the three gates and what reaching each of them takes. It deliberately defines none of the following: the conformance model under which an implementation-acceptance requirement is assessed, which Section 17 states; the checks, faults, fixtures, suite cases, evidence contract, and conformance acceptance criteria, which the conformance document states under Section 17's discipline; the implementation handoff boundary and the register of deliberately unselected technical choices whose blocking decisions the second gate consults, which Section 18 states; the parity-dependency discipline the third gate presupposes, which Section 19 states; and the register in which a question blocking a gate is recorded and later ruled, which Section 21 states.

## 21. Open questions and later rulings

### 21.1 What this section establishes

This section establishes the one place where a genuinely open question of this specification, and every later ruling that settles one, is recorded: the append-only, implementation-neutral open-questions-and-rulings register in Section 21.3, read under the Section 21.2 contract and bound by WP2-HAND-039. It is the register Section 4.3 anticipates — an Open question stays open, no other section resolves it implicitly, and any resolution arrives as an explicit ruling recorded where the question is recorded — and it is the destination Section 5.14 names. Version `1.0.0` holds exactly one entry: the nonblocking format-verification question Section 5.14 item 4 records and the Section 13.9 rationale expressly declines to settle. This section also states the discipline that keeps the register honest — no entry weakens an approved requirement, no entry duplicates the implementation-decision register in Section 18, and no editorial maintenance issue is dressed as an open question. The register is implementation-neutral, its entries select no mechanism, and the selections WP2-SCOPE-015 enumerates remain prohibited throughout this document.

Every substantive statement here is a Required future invariant carrying exactly one `WP2-HAND-###` identifier, continuing the single sequence Section 17 begins, or a Design rationale introduced by a **Rationale.** lead-in, in the Section 4.3 sense, or else an entry of the open-questions-and-rulings register in Section 21.3, together with the introducing statements of that subsection, whose normative force flows through WP2-HAND-039 rather than through an identifier of its own, an entry of the attribute table in Section 21.2, which states how the register is read and carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements that cite it, the definitional statement in Section 21.2 naming the register and its keys, which carries no obligation of its own beyond the requirements that cite it, or a boundary statement in Section 21.5. The register in Section 21.3 is the one structured block of this section, WP2-HAND-039 is its single incorporation requirement, and no cell of it commands: an entry records a question or a ruling, its question entries are this document's Open question class in the Section 4.3 sense and a ruling entry is the explicit ruling that class anticipates, and what binds is the key, field, and lineage contract of Sections 21.2 and 21.3 that WP2-HAND-039 incorporates. Nothing in this section is a Current fact or a Current limitation; those belong to Section 5, and WP2-SCOPE-010 keeps a current mechanism from being read as a requirement. No Unselected option lead-in appears here — a deliberately unselected technical choice is an entry of the Section 18 register, which WP2-HAND-042 keeps out of this one — and this section introduces no Section 3.1 term. Every defined term used here carries the meaning Section 3.1 assigns it, as WP2-SCOPE-005 requires.

### 21.2 How the open-questions-and-rulings register is read

Throughout this section, the *open-questions-and-rulings register* is the register in Section 21.3, and each of its entries carries a register key of the form `OQ-##`, allocated sequentially in order of recording. An `OQ-##` key is a register key, not a requirement identifier: it belongs to no requirement-ID namespace Section 4.2 reserves, it is visibly distinct from every `WP2-*` requirement identifier and from every test-document identifier of the conformance document, and neither a key nor an entry carries normative force of its own, the force of the register flowing through WP2-HAND-039 alone.

An entry is of one of two kinds. An *open question* records something this specification leaves genuinely unsettled, awaiting an explicit ruling. A *ruling* records the explicit decision that settles, revises, or supersedes one or more earlier entries, and it arrives only as a further identified entry carrying predecessor or supersession lineage — never as an edit to an earlier entry. Because the register is append-only, an entry's current-status cell speaks as of that entry's recording, and the register's current state is read from the register as a whole: a question entry that no later ruling entry names in its lineage remains open, and one that a later ruling entry names carries the status that ruling records.

The blocking-effect attribute speaks in the vocabulary of Section 20's gates and of the Section 18 handoff boundary: an entry states whether, while it stands open, it blocks Work Package 2 documentation-package acceptance under WP2-HAND-033, blocks implementation selection under the handoff boundary Section 18 states, blocks implementation acceptance under WP2-HAND-035, or blocks none of them. A question that removes the conceptual pass condition of a requirement is not a harmless open item, and the unresolved-questions discipline of the conformance document records none; WP2-HAND-041 keeps an entry here from being read as removing one.

Each entry states the same nine attributes in the same order.

| Attribute | What the entry states |
| --- | --- |
| Register key and entry kind | The entry's `OQ-##` key, and whether the entry is an open question or a ruling. |
| Predecessor or supersession lineage | For a ruling, and for any entry that revises or supersedes an earlier entry, the exact register key of every earlier entry it resolves, revises, or supersedes; for an initial question, the explicit statement that it has none. |
| Question or ruling | One statement of what remains unsettled, or of what the ruling settles and how. |
| Affected sections and requirements | The exact sections and requirement IDs of this specification the entry affects, together with the parts of the conformance document mapped to them. |
| Current status | The entry's status as of its recording, any later change arriving only as a further identified entry. |
| Deciding authority and latest decision point | The authority whose explicit ruling settles the question, under the authorities Section 1.2 states, and the latest point in the governed sequence of work by which the decision has to exist. |
| Evidence needed to decide | The recorded evidence a responsible ruling would rest on. |
| Blocking effect | Whether the entry, while open, blocks Work Package 2 documentation-package acceptance, implementation selection, implementation acceptance, or none of them. |
| Versioning consequence | Which identities of Section 1.5 a resolution would create or revise, and whether the resolution could affect a registered treatment or behavior in the charter Section 14.2 sense. |

**WP2-HAND-039.** Every current or later entry of the open-questions-and-rulings register in Section 21.3 **MUST** conform to every clause of that register's reading contract — the register-key, entry-kind, lineage, append-only-reading, and blocking-effect statements of Section 21.2 and the entry contract of Section 21.3 — and, for the applicable entry, its register key and entry kind, predecessor or supersession lineage, question or ruling, affected sections and requirements, current status, deciding authority and latest decision point, evidence needed to decide, blocking effect, and versioning consequence — no entry selecting a mechanism, ranking an option, or commanding of itself, the force of the register flowing through this requirement alone.

**WP2-HAND-040.** The register in Section 21.3 **MUST** remain append-only wherever it is maintained, read, or cited: a later ruling, correction, status change, or supersession is recorded as a further identified entry carrying explicit predecessor or supersession lineage naming every earlier entry it resolves, revises, or supersedes, and no previously recorded entry or register key is rewritten, renumbered, reused, or removed.

**Rationale.** WP2-HAND-039 is the single requirement that incorporates the register and makes its non-commanding clauses binding, in the device WP2-ID-027 uses for the entity register, WP2-EVID-001 for the evidence-record register, and WP2-SCHED-035 for the concurrency-profile tables, so that no cell carries a requirement identifier of its own and no obligation hides in a table. The append-only rule is this document's own correction discipline applied to itself: WP2-ID-025, WP2-ID-026, and WP2-LIFE-017 make correction and supersession further identified records with explicit lineage everywhere else in the laboratory, and a register of questions and rulings that could be silently rewritten would be unable to show what was asked, what was ruled, and in which order — the property the whole register exists to preserve. The two entry kinds keep the asking and the settling visibly separate, so that the register never resolves a question by drift: until a ruling entry exists, the question stands exactly as recorded.

### 21.3 The open-questions-and-rulings register

One entry follows for each question and ruling recorded so far, in register-key order. Each entry answers the nine attributes of Section 21.2 concretely. Version `1.0.0` holds exactly one entry and no ruling: the one question the contract expressly carries forward unsettled. No further entry is present because no other genuinely open question exists in this version.

**Open question OQ-01. Independent format verification of a future evidence-packaging version.**

| Attribute | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Register key and entry kind | `OQ-01`. Open question. |
| Predecessor or supersession lineage | None. This is the initial entry of its lineage. |
| Question or ruling | Whether, and under what recorded rules, a future evidence-packaging version is required to be independently format-verified — its committed archive verified as a well-formed, readable container under its recorded format, by a verification distinct from the digest facts a seal verification evaluates — and at which points such verification would be required. |
| Affected sections and requirements | Section 5.9 and Section 5.14 item 4, which record the question and its source — the confirmed archive-format defect of the Milestone 2 packaging version, whose receipt attests a digest over the committed archive rather than its container format; the Section 13.9 rationale, which expressly declines to settle the question and carries it forward; WP2-EVID-044, WP2-EVID-046, WP2-EVID-047, and WP2-EVID-048, whose sealed-package and seal-verification records an affirmative resolution would extend; the archive-and-serialization-technology and verification-scheduling options Section 13.13 leaves unselected; and the conformance checks the conformance document maps to those requirements. |
| Current status | Open as of recording. No ruling entry exists. |
| Deciding authority and latest decision point | An explicit later ruling under the authorities Section 1.2 states — a versioned specification revision or an explicit Advisor ruling, recorded here as a further entry with lineage to this one — and never an implementation's silent choice, which the handoff boundary Section 18 states prohibits. Latest decision point: before any registered evidence contract adopts a future evidence-packaging version whose sealed packages an acceptance-supporting verification would evaluate. |
| Evidence needed to decide | The recorded verification history of the current packaging mechanism and of any candidate future one, the Section 5.9 record of the Milestone 2 archive-format defect and its receipt semantics among it, together with an analysis of whether the failure class in question — a committed archive unreadable under its recorded format — is detectable from digest facts alone or only by independent format verification. |
| Blocking effect | None of the three. While open it blocks neither documentation-package acceptance, nor implementation selection, nor implementation acceptance: every affected requirement retains its conceptual pass condition, WP2-EVID-048 requiring of a verification, on this question, only that it be performed under identified rules and version with its verdict preserved, exactly as the Section 13.9 rationale records. An affirmative resolution would add an obligation rather than supply a missing one. |
| Versioning consequence | A resolution in either direction is a revision of this specification under Section 1.4's discipline, and it bears on the evidence-packaging version identity Section 1.5 keeps distinct from specification identity. It concerns evidence handling and operations rather than any registered treatment, so under charter Section 14.2 it would arrive as a new packaging version and specification revision, not as a new treatment, condition, or experiment version. |

**Rationale.** Section 5.14 item 4 and the Section 13.9 rationale record the confirmed archive-format defect of the current packaging transaction and deliberately leave independent format verification unsettled. `OQ-01` is the explicit register entry for that one question. No second entry exists because the remaining Section 5.14 items are resolved terminology or specification decisions, or current limitations addressed by completed normative sections; deliberately unselected technical choices belong to the Section 18 register under WP2-HAND-042, and editorial maintenance issues remain outside this register under WP2-HAND-043.

### 21.4 Register discipline

**WP2-HAND-041.** An entry of the register in Section 21.3 **MUST NOT** be recorded, read, or cited as weakening, suspending, reinterpreting, or reopening an approved requirement of this specification, as removing or deferring the conceptual pass condition of any requirement, or as permission to diverge from any requirement while the question stands open.

**WP2-HAND-042.** A technical choice the implementation-decision register in Section 18 records as deliberately unselected **MUST NOT** be recorded in the register in Section 21.3, the two registers remaining distinct: an unselected implementation decision awaits an authorized selection under the handoff boundary Section 18 states, and an entry here records a question this specification itself leaves genuinely unsettled, or the explicit ruling that settles one.

**WP2-HAND-043.** An editorial, stale-language, status, navigation, or cross-section-integration maintenance issue **MUST NOT** be recorded in the register in Section 21.3 as an open question or unresolved design question.

**Rationale.** The three disciplines keep the register honest in three directions. Toward the approved contract: Section 4.3 fixes that an open question is settled only by an explicit ruling, and WP2-HAND-041 states the converse — recording a question changes nothing until its ruling arrives — so the register can never serve as a soft amendment channel, and no entry narrows what the approved requirement set demands. Toward Section 18: a deliberately unselected technical choice is not an unsettled question of this specification — the contract states the invariant and leaves the mechanism to a governed future selection, which is a handoff posture rather than uncertainty — so the two registers answer different questions, and WP2-HAND-042 keeps each in its own. Toward document maintenance: WP2-HAND-043 keeps editorial, status, navigation, and integration work from being dressed as unsettled design.

### 21.5 Boundaries assigned elsewhere

This section states where a genuinely open question of this specification and every later ruling are recorded, what an entry identifies, and the discipline that bounds the register. It deliberately defines none of the following: the unresolved conformance questions of the conformance document, which that document records under its own test-document keys and its own entry contract, citing this specification and never cited by it as a source of requirements; the implementation-decision register and the handoff boundary under which an unselected choice is later selected, which Section 18 states; the acceptance gates an open entry can block, which Section 20 states; the conformance treatment of any requirement an entry affects, which Section 17 and the conformance document state; and editorial, status, navigation, or cross-section maintenance, which is not an open-question entry.
